# Review log: Lenny-issued bearer tokens verify only on the process that signed them

## Standing context

Changelog (compaction pass, 2026-10-24, after non-spec round 12). Read the whole ledger, [spec.1.*] through [non-spec.12.*] plus [reconcile.1] to [reconcile.3], the owner-decision entries, and [redesign.1] and [redesign.2], and lifted each FACT, DECISION, WATCHOUT, MISTAKE, and unclosed OPEN, UNVERIFIED, and DEFERRED into the sections below. Applied the DEFERRED on this section from [spec.6.fix-G1.1] (carried by [reconcile.1] to [reconcile.3]): owner decision 5 and the D13 trap now say JWTs. Deleted, because a later entry closed them: the S20 checklist Open ([non-spec.1.fix-G7.1], [non-spec.2.fix-G3.1]); the Deferred docs items DOCS-2 now stages, namely the in-memory store mirrors, security.md and architecture.md, the configuration.md `tokenExchange` TTL rows, and the client-guide `expires_in` example ([non-spec.1.fix-G2.1]); the SPEC-5 Edit 4 render-condition deferral ([non-spec.2.fix-G3.1]); every checklist-gloss deferral ([reconcile.1], [reconcile.2]); the CODE-8 form-encoded `expires_in` deferral ([reconcile.2]). Closed Opens and UNVERIFIEDs that later entries answered: embedded-mode `lenny-ops` callers, rule-4 versus rule-7 precedence, lease tokens as JWTs, the §25.4 KMS dependency row, the IAM grant scope, the HMACSigner tier-3 ports, CODE-11 manifest regeneration, the CHART-1 dev-render change, the OpenAPI `expires_in` row, the opsDsn PgBouncer credentials, and the rule-1 read deadline (refuted as hardening). Dropped the two "related-passages lookup returned leads" OPENs from [redesign.1] and [redesign.2], whose own text records that no edit site followed. Did not lift the per-round anchor-sweep and citation-resolution FACTs, each of which records only that the staging resolved in one round. Length: 191 lines, within the 200 target. Traps carry the withdrawn options of the non-spec loop, and each guards a design that still stands, so none was cut.

### Settled

Owner decisions:
- **Owner decision 1, signing model** (2026-10-23): production signing is remote asymmetric KMS signing, and every verifier verifies locally against public keys selected by `kid`. Rejected: one shared HS256 key sealed by the KMS, and a per-replica KMS-signed intermediate key as a default.
- **Owner decision 2, fixed lifetime** (2026-10-23): Lenny-issued tokens have a fixed lifetime, the gateway never re-mints per interaction, and holders refresh by pulling.
- **Owner decision 3, 15-minute default** (2026-10-23): the default capability token lifetime is 15 minutes, operator-tunable within the bounds SPEC-3 Edit 1 states (`120 ≤ ttl ≤ 3600`).
- **Owner decision 4, `lenny-ops` revocation set** (2026-10-23): option B. Home: SPEC-11 Edit 2 **Revocation set** rules 1 to 7; implementation: CODE-6 item 6; reasons, residual risks, and rejected options A and C: summary D22.
- **Owner decision 5, default scope** (2026-10-23): the 15-minute default governs every Lenny-issued JWT whose holder can refresh it by pulling: playground bearers and `POST /v1/oauth/token` JWTs requested without `expires_in` (user bearers from rotation and scope narrowing, and operability-scope tokens). An explicit `expires_in` is honoured within the per-dialect caps. The session-creation `session_capability` token and the delegation `actor_token` path keep their caps. Credential-lease tokens are opaque handles outside the default. Home: SPEC-3 Edit 1; implementation: CODE-8 items 3 to 5; summary D13.
- **OD1, revoked-token response** (owner's delegate, 2026-10-24): option (a). `lenny-ops` reuses `authmw` unchanged and emits `401 TOKEN_REVOKED`; SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 3 names the gateway's revoked-token response and no code; the §15 row and §13.3 lowercase spelling are a Defects follow-up. Summary D23.
- **Owner decision 6, readiness** (owner, 2026-10-24, answering OD2 with option (b)): production readiness passes when Postgres or the Kubernetes API is reachable. Home: SPEC-11 Edit 6; implementation: CODE-13; summary D24. Accepted cost: a replica whose own Postgres link broke stays Ready with `degradation.warnings`.
- **OD3, `lenny-ops` dev gate** (owner's delegate, 2026-10-24, D26): option (c). §17.4 item 3 stays universal, `lenny-ops` stays on `--production` in CODE-6, and the mismatch is a Defects entry for a direct fix after 0097 lands. Do not re-raise it.
- **Session-token renewal follow-up**: gateway-to-pod renewal of the session-creation `session_capability` token belongs to a follow-up proposal (summary Defects and Non-goals); that token keeps its 24 h per-dialect cap; D21 keeps its substance ([owner-decisions-2026-10-23]).
- **Problem-statement wording**: owner decision 4 keeps its 1-hour bound and "§13.3 mechanism" wording as the owner's input; D22 records the departure ([redesign.1]).

Signing topology and keys:
- **Mint-list homes**: §4.3 item 3 lists Token Service mints, §10.2 "Signing topology" lists gateway in-process mints, and each cites the other (D9); SPEC-7 Edit 5 turns the §13.3 "one exception" sentence into a pointer.
- **Session-creation mint**: stays a gateway in-process mint, recorded in `issued_tokens` with a `token.exchanged` row under §13.3 statements (1) to (3), never step (1a). It has no CODE deliverable in 0097 (summary Defects).
- **`session_mint` home**: the only `exchange_type` enumeration is §16.7 (SPEC-1 Edit 5); no schema, OpenAPI, SDK, or OCSF mapping lists it.
- **`typ` stamping**: SPEC-1 Edit 4 reads "The minting component stamps `typ` alongside the other claims when it signs the token".
- **KMS IAM grant**: one home, SPEC-1 "Signing topology": gateway and Token Service SAs hold `Sign` and `GetPublicKey` on every key either alias names; `lenny-ops` holds `GetPublicKey` only; SPEC-2 rotation step 1 grants before it maps ([spec.12.fix-G1.1]).
- **Key identity**: reserved aliases `platform:jwt-signing` and `platform:jwt-verify` in `kms.aliases` (D5); a startup-loaded static key set replaces a refresh rule (D8).
- **`LoadKeySet` signature**: `LoadKeySet(ctx, provider, aliasToKey)` decides "mapped" from the alias map and returns every `PublicKey` error; the local provider's unmapped-verify `ErrUnknownKEK` case is removed ([non-spec.5.fix-G2.1]).
- **Repointable-reference predicate**: one home, SPEC-1 Edit 1 **Production**; CODE-1 item 4 and the spec edge case point at it ([non-spec.5.fix-G2.1]).
- **Verifier set**: the LLM proxy runs inside the gateway, so gateway, Token Service, and `lenny-ops` are the complete verifier set for the SPEC-2 rollouts.
- **Rotation rollouts**: `kms.aliases` render as `--kms-alias` args, so an alias change rolls the pods; gateway termination grace (240 to 300 s) is negligible against the 24 h step-3 wait.
- **Only 10-year token**: rotate-token serves only `lenny-admin`, so the bootstrap admin credential is the only 10-year Lenny-issued token; SPEC-3 Edit 1 states the figure from `admintoken.DefaultTokenTTL`, and §17.6 is cited for rotation only.
- **Per-dialect caps**: 24 h, 1 h, and 1 h are stated only in SPEC-7 Edit 2 and match `cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go`; the impersonation 1 h cap exists in code only.
- **Dev HMAC sites**: every spec "dev HMAC JWT" site is staged in SPEC-1 Edits 3, 6, and 7 and SPEC-3 Edits 4 and 6; devTenantId rows read "dev-mode playground JWT".
- **Dev bearer-trust hook**: stays HMAC under D12; the gateway hook is fatal without `--dev-mode` (LENNY_DEV_MODE); SPEC-9 Edit 2 reads "(TLS, dev bearer trust)" and drops "JWT signing is not relaxed in dev mode".
- **Embedded master key**: a Secret that `lenny up` creates if absent and never updates; the chart mounts it through `kms.masterKeySecret`; the lookup-generated Secret renders only when that value is empty (CHART-2, D12).
- **§17.4 "State and resets"**: only the in-memory store list loses KMS (SPEC-9 Edit 3); the "no separate ... KMS ... process" clauses stay; `--purge` clears the substrate that holds the Secret.
- **SA annotations**: CHART-2 item 2 adds `tokenService.serviceAccount.annotations` and `ops.serviceAccount.annotations`, mirroring `gateway.serviceAccount.annotations`, the chart's only identity hook.

Lifetime and exchange:
- **TTL bounds**: `120 ≤ ttl ≤ 3600`, one home SPEC-3 Edit 1; the floor is 120 because SPEC-8 divides by `ttl − 60`.
- **Request `expires_in` floor**: a value below 60, zero and negatives included, is rejected with `invalid_request`; home SPEC-3 Edit 1; it agrees with §13.3 "minimum token lifetime is 60s" ([spec.14.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **§13.3 request row**: SPEC-3 Edit 7 adds a request `expires_in` row that points at the §10.2 `expiry` rule and restates nothing.
- **Exchange `exp` formula**: one home, SPEC-7 Edit 2: "A JWT minted on an exchange" receives `min(requested_exp, subject_token.exp, per-dialect cap)`, plus `actor_token.exp` when present ([spec.10.fix-G1.1]).
- **a2a children take no default**: the SPEC-3 Edit 1 sentence that an absent `expires_in` leaves `requested_exp` out for an `actor_token` child stays; CODE-8 item 4 depends on it.
- **Dev-row `exp`**: the §27.3.1 dev Auth-by-mode row keeps `exp = now + ttl` deliberately (no subject in dev, cap never binds below 3600).
- **`Request.ExpiresIn` type**: becomes `*int64` with `json:"expires_in,omitempty"`; the form branch sets it when present ([non-spec.10.fix-G1.1]).
- **Lease tokens**: opaque `randomString("lt", 32)` handles that sign nothing, with the §4.9 lease TTL from `ResolveLeaseTTL`; `lease_issue` is a misnomer label for a no-actor `session_capability` exchange.
- **Rotation cannot extend**: a self-rotation is clamped to `subject_token.exp`; refresh means a new exchange from an upstream subject.

Step (1a) and issuance:
- **Step (1a)**: passes a token with no `issued_tokens` row and rejects only on `revoked_at`; the fail-closed missing-row rule is withdrawn ([spec.2.fix-G1.1], [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **(1a) placement**: CODE-9 takes the lock through a new exported `auditstore.AcquireLockInTx`, runs (1a), then calls `AppendInTx`; the xact lock is reentrant and READ COMMITTED sees a concurrent commit ([non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]).
- **CODE-9 test split**: tier 1 tokenservice, tier 2 store, tier 7a rotation race, tier 9 refusal calling the Token Service directly.
- **Row retention**: `issued_tokens` rows live until `exp` plus one hour, so cleanup never removes a presentable token's row.
- **Token Service sign order**: it already signs before the persist transaction, so SPEC-7 Edit 1 changes no code there.

KMS failure mode and alerts:
- **Retry-After home**: SPEC-4 Edit 1: every `Sign` error is `503 KMS_SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE`, Retry-After is the remaining cooldown when open and 1 s otherwise; the §15 row is a pointer.
- **"Existing sessions unaffected" withdrawn**: a running session's delegation and refresh mints fail until signing recovers.
- **Sign-result classification**: one home, CODE-3 item 1: ok, throttled, caller-done, counted error; `SigningCallTimeout` is 5 s with a `CallTimeout` override; throttled and caller-done are neutral and release a half-open probe.
- **`SigningError`**: CODE-3 item 2: one `*jwt.SigningError{RetryAfter}` for every non-success result; breaker-open wraps `ErrSigningUnavailable`; `RetryAfterSeconds()` rounds up with a floor of 1 ([non-spec.6.fix-G1.1]).
- **SDK retry off on `Sign`**: CODE-1 item 2 disables it per call (AWS `RetryMaxAttempts = 1`, Azure `MaxRetries: -1`, GCP nil `gax` Retryer) ([non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]).
- **Per-replica sign limit**: deleted (D15).
- **Sign-rate formula**: SPEC-8 Edit 2 has no worked Tier figures and no lease term (D16).
- **KMSSignQuotaApproaching**: threshold from the gateway gauge, `0.8 * scalar(max(lenny_kms_sign_quota_per_second) > 0)`, rendered on every provider; CHART-5 renders only `--kms-sign-quota-per-second`; the SPEC-5 Edit 4 row states the threshold in GatewayActiveStreamsHigh style with no render condition.
- **Alert pins**: CODE-11 tier-11 check, the tier-2 catalog crosschecks, the CODE-7 tier-1 gauge case, the CHART-5 flag unittest, and tier-4 promtool cases; `rules_test.go` lists change with `rules.go`.
- **KMSSigningUnavailable**: `rate(...[30s]) > 1` and `reason` {inner, rejected} already match code.
- **Admin writers**: CODE-7 targets `writeImpersonationError` and `handleRotateToken`, which map sign errors to 500 today.

Egress and chart:
- **KMS egress**: home SPEC-10 Edit 1 Gateway row: `kms.endpointCIDR` on TCP 443 when `kms.provider` is not `local`; refuse `0.0.0.0/0` or `::/0` on a cloud provider; use the private KMS endpoint subnet.
- **`lenny-ops` Postgres path**: pooler only; `LENNY_POSTGRES_DSN` from `lenny-datastore-conn` key `postgres-dsn` as the gateway does; `allow-pgbouncer` admits `app: lenny-ops`; a `gatewayEgressCIDR` ipBlock peer; the self-managed topology is a summary Defects bullet ([non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Schema source**: `values.schema.json` is generated from `pkg/chart/values`; `auth`, `kms`, `postgres`, `tokenService`, and `ops` are open objects; a bounded `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` needs an `Auth` struct tagged `open:"true"`.
- **Helm unittests**: run only at tier 1.
- **Embedded render**: holds gateway, `lenny-ops`, controller, and the PrometheusRule, with no Token Service or playground; CODE-6, CODE-11, CODE-13, and CHART-2 regenerate it; CODE-8 and CHART-1 flag renames do not touch it.
- **Token Service mTLS case**: its two `isNull` asserts become `notContains` with `any: true` (CHART-2).

`lenny-ops`:
- **Token classes**: accepts every audience and every Lenny-issued class except the playground bearer (rule 7, D25).
- **Rule 4**: names the §13.3 Postgres-unreachable response and takes precedence over rules 3 and 7; `authmw` order (Verify, RevocationFreshness, Revocations, tenant extraction, PlaygroundRevocations) gives it with no new surface.
- **Rule 1 replace semantics**: each read replaces the set with its rows plus rule-2 jtis added after the read began; CODE-6 changes the shared `Cache.Rehydrate` for the gateway too ([spec.16.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Replace is safe**: every `Cache.Revoke` caller pushes after the durable `revoked_at` commit, the lister includes tombstoned tenants, and Postgres RPO is 0.
- **Revoked admin credential**: the rehydration predicate is `exp > now() - 1h`, so a revoked 10-year credential stays in the set.
- **`buildAuthConfig`**: returns an `authmw` config whenever `--production` or the trust flag is set; nil only outside production without the flag; the issuer check wraps only the dev trust member.
- **Key set load**: CODE-6 item 3 loads it in every `lenny-ops` process.
- **Probes**: permissive `/healthz` runs `probe.Run` over `postgres` and `kubernetes` only, and the chart sets `timeoutSeconds: 3` on all three probes in every render (CODE-13).
- **Embedded `lenny-ops`**: has no DSN, so rule 4 refuses key-set tokens for life; no embedded flow presents one (no Token Service, playground off, CLI uses the dev bearer); not a regression.
- **Break-glass**: §25.15 Path D uses `kubectl create token`, so rule 4 does not block it.
- **GatewayClient**: holds the projected SA token and requests no `/v1/oauth/token` token; the SPEC-8 GatewayClient example is withdrawn.
- **§25.15 closing paragraph**: stays true under owner decision 4 for a `lenny-ops` that loaded its set before the outage.
- **SPEC-11 Edit 5**: now the §25.4 Dependencies KMS row and the §25.1 bullet; unrelated to the retired `/v1/admin/me` Edit 5.
- **SPEC-11 Edit 6 bullet**: cites "Startup and Readiness" below; reasons and cost live in D24.

Token Service caller refusal:
- **SPEC-7 Edit 7**: the Token Service refuses a playground-origin caller token with `401 invalid_client`; CODE-5 item 4 implements it in `authenticateCaller`; D27 holds the reason and the subject_token residual.
- **Delegation path**: §8.2 child minting is in process (`childtoken`), never reaches `authenticateCaller`, so the refusal breaks no delegation.
- **D27 allow case**: a tier-1 case pins that a playground `subject_token` under an origin-less caller returns an `access_token`.

Process and scope:
- **Tier lists**: checklist tier lists are final-gate sets over regression reach; S20 dropped 7a, S19 gained 3, S23 gained 2 and 4, S18 gained 7a and 11.
- **spec-map**: each deliverable updates `tests/spec-map.json` in its own commit; TEST-1 item 7 is deleted.
- **§17.7 audit-lock remedy**: SPEC-6 Edit 2 lowers the hot tenant's concurrent-session quota.
- **Checklist numbering**: S26 is removed and S27 to S32 keep their numbers.
- **Scope departures**: per-replica limit (D15), §12.2 rows (D20), reserved aliases (D5), static key set (D8).
- **Plan conformance**: 0097 claims only F-10.2.22; the sealed-key mechanism is a recorded divergence; the plan's "§10.3" is a stale citation; the P1-P5 rules do not bind it.
- **Kubernetes surface**: no CRD, status, finalizer, webhook, or controller; only probes, SA annotations, the master-key Secret, NetworkPolicy, and rollouts.
- **Client surface**: OpenAPI `/v1/oauth/token` has no request or response schema and no JWKS path; no SDK reads `expires_in`, Retry-After, or `KMS_SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE`.
- **Tier-5 Kind**: sets `postgres.dsn` and `bearerTrustKeySecret` with `ops.production` false; kindnet does not enforce NetworkPolicy.

### Traps

- **Trace every caller before a fail-closed rule.** Round 1 (the step (1a) missing-row rule broke delegation from playground and impersonation sessions) and round 4 (the `lenny-ops` refusal during a Postgres outage broke the §25.15 loop) both broke a downstream flow by not doing so.
- **Withdrawn: step (1a) missing-row rule.** "Verified by the key set" does not imply "has a row": playground and impersonation bearers are key-set-verified with no row and are the §8.2 delegation `subject_token`. Do not reintroduce it by origin, typ, caller, or verifier, and do not add a (1a) sentence on where playground revocation is checked.
- **D13 scope is fixed.** Playground bearers plus `POST /v1/oauth/token` JWTs requested without `expires_in`. Do not narrow it to playground bearers, and do not extend it to the session-creation token, delegated children, or credential-lease tokens. The `expires_in` floor is validation and does not reopen owner decision 5.
- **Withdrawn: per-replica sign limit.** A static split of `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` starves the fixed-size Token Service. Do not re-add it for the problem statement's scope bullet; D15 records the departure.
- **Withdrawn: lease terms.** Do not re-file a credential-lease issuance or renewal term in SPEC-8, a KMS dependency of lease renewal, or "Sign-rate formula omits leases". Do not restore worked Tier figures without a spec source for the holder count.
- **Withdrawn: unscoped exchange formula.** [MISTAKE, spec.10] Round 9 widened SPEC-7 Edit 2 to "a token minted on an exchange", which reached credential-lease tokens that §13.3 routes through the same exchange and cost a round. Keep "A JWT", and add no lease-lifetime sentence there.
- **Withdrawn: `RevocationFreshness` on `lenny-ops`.** It turns stale after 90 s of failed reads, which is the rejected option A. Do not use `Cache.Stale` as the startup gate.
- **Withdrawn: "the gateway's revocation-cache mechanisms".** §13.3 also holds the per-request fallback and the fail-closed refusal that `lenny-ops` does not apply, and it states no periodic read. Cite the rules by number.
- **Withdrawn: 1-hour missed-revocation bound.** `lenny-ctl` presents its gateway bearer to `lenny-ops` and no verifier enforces `aud` by default. Do not wire `lenny-ops` without the chart's Postgres DSN either.
- **Withdrawn: impersonation refusal and Redis playground check on `lenny-ops`.** See D25. A rule-7 test must use a valid tenant, because the playground check runs after tenant extraction.
- **No literal codes in rules 3, 4, or 7.** §15 defines no `token_validation_unavailable` or revoked-token row; each rule names the gateway response by section (D23).
- **Withdrawn: add-only rule 1.** The add-only wording grows the set for the process lifetime against the 256Mi limit. Do not keep jtis revoked before the read began; that is the old carry-forward. `TestRehydrateUnionsLocalRevocations` is rewritten, never deleted.
- **Rehydrate 7a test.** The fake's last `ListRevoked` starts the `Revoke` goroutines and returns without waiting, and the predicate is "once `Rehydrate` and every `Revoke` goroutine have returned". Waiting in the fake collapses the case into tier 1.
- **Replace rule assumes a primary read.** A lagging replica would drop a rule-2 jti for one 30 s cycle; no spec change was made.
- **Withdrawn: lenny-ops-only Postgres routes.** [MISTAKE, non-spec.2 and non-spec.3] Three rounds staged narrower routes, `postgres.opsDsn` to `lenny-pgbouncer` last. PgBouncer listens on 6432 behind 5432-only policies and has no `[databases]` upstream, and the gateway has no path in those renders either. Do not add `app: lenny-ops` to `allow-postgres-self-managed` (§25.4 "does not bypass the pooler"), and do not write "lenny-ops reaches Postgres wherever the gateway does".
- **Withdrawn: round-2 Postgres predicate.** [MISTAKE, non-spec.2] "The rule-1 read runs when `postgres.dsn` names PgBouncer or the endpoint `gatewayEgressCIDR` admits" was false: the CIDR admits egress, but `allow-postgres-self-managed` drops `lenny-ops` at ingress.
- **Retry-After rounding home.** [MISTAKE, non-spec.6] Round 5 replaced "round up, floor 1" with "the SPEC-4 value", but SPEC-4 fixes no direction and the tests pin 2.3 s to 3. State the direction once in CODE-3 item 2; do not move it to CODE-7, restate it per writer, or put the "1 second" value back in CODE-3.
- **Withdrawn: typed-error rejection.** The round-5 rejection of a typed `BreakerSigner` error held only for writers that see the raw error. The admin writers see it wrapped beside audit and store errors that stay 500, so the type is required.
- **Writers outside the breaker.** A test signer that bypasses `BreakerSigner` must return `*jwt.SigningError`, or the asserted Retry-After has no source. Only the tier-2 in-process test pins the throttle value.
- **Classify caller-done by the caller's `ctx.Err()`.** Never by `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)`: a `SigningCallTimeout` expiry is also DeadlineExceeded and must count, or a hung KMS never trips.
- **Half-open probe slot.** Any `Sign` path that calls neither `recordSuccess` nor `trip` leaves `probeInFlight` held for good. A raw half-open `RetryAfter` may be zero or negative, so tier 1 asserts `RetryAfterSeconds()`.
- **Breaker trip is a consecutive run.** `recordSuccess` clears failures, so fail, fail, ok, fail, fail inside 30 s stays closed. Any invariant on the trip predicate excludes half-open, where one failed probe reopens.
- **Do not re-edit CODE-3 for throttling.** Two breaker-side rewrites failed; the fix lives in the adapter that owns the SDK call. The GCP `clientAdapter` discards its options, so its wrapper must forward the `gax` option; the GCP test case uses `Unavailable`; the Azure test must not count the challenge request.
- **Quota alert guard.** Both `max()` and `> 0` are required: gauges pre-materialize at 0, and `scalar()` of a multi-replica vector is NaN. The gauge goes in `alertSupportCatalog`; `lenny_jwt_sign_requests_total` goes in `metricCatalog` and `spec161Metrics`.
- **RunbookURL has no fragment.** Use `runbook("jwt-key-rotation")`; tier-11 tests stat the whole suffix plus `.md`, and S23 must not assert a section that S30 creates.
- **Withdrawn: per-alias `ErrUnknownKEK` skip.** Adapters map a not-found on a mapped key to `ErrUnknownKEK`. Do not re-add a `PublicKey` not-found adapter case or narrow the sentinel; the discriminating test is a mapped alias whose error wraps `ErrUnknownKEK` failing the load.
- **No stub dial path for subprocesses.** The KMS stub is in-process only. Do not add a stub or endpoint flag to production binaries; subprocess startup failure uses `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_KMS` with dummy credentials.
- **Playground caller refusal stays caller-only.** Do not extend SPEC-7 Edit 7 to `subject_token` or `actor_token`. The delegation-mint follow-up must not present the playground bearer as caller. The (1a) "narrower form" WATCHOUT does not govern this refusal. TEST-1 item 6 is a deny case; the allow side stays at tier 1.
- **Shared verifier call.** The Token Service verifies caller, subject, and actor through one `s.verifier.Verify`, so an origin check in the verifier passes every deny test and still breaks delegation.
- **`lenny-ops` IAM identity.** Do not point `lenny-ops` at the gateway's SA annotations or IRSA role, which hold KMS Encrypt and Decrypt.
- **`buildAuthConfig` issuer check.** Wrapping the whole key set in `NewClaimChecker` rejects Lenny-issued tokens whose `iss` differs (Token Service, playground). Tests asserting a token outcome on `lenny-ops` pass `--bearer-trust-hmac-key-file`.
- **Tier-5 `lenny-ops` acceptance.** Assert 200 with the subject on `GET /v1/admin/me`, never "not 401", and never with a playground bearer.
- **Probe timeouts.** `timeoutSeconds` stays above `probeTimeout` (2 s) and is not conditional on `ops.production`. Do not restore an unconditional permissive 200; neither-dependency liveness failure follows §25.4.
- **Embedded and helm render traps.** A chart change visible under the dev preset needs `make generate` in the same commit. The ops cold-start case keeps an exact env list. `notContains` with a partial map needs `any: true`. The existing token-service cloud case uses the retired `platform:token-service-signing` alias.
- **Forgery test port.** `jwt_forgery_test.go` keeps its three names (spec-map maps them twice) and stamps the key set's signing `kid`, or every case stops at `unknown_kid`.
- **`NewServer` zero-TTL fixtures.** About 27 callers break when `NewServer` errors on a zero `CapabilityTokenTTL`. Several lenses judged it a mechanical fixture update the S21 gates surface; do not file it.
- **Settled non-findings.** Do not file: the cold-start Ready-but-refusing replica (D22, D24); the §25.15 loop ending with a 15-min token in a Postgres outage; liveness on dependencies; the §16.5 and §17.7 token-store rows; `oauth.rateLimit.tenantPerSecond` outside §17.7 audit item (2); §13.3 "minimum token lifetime is 60s"; the never-evicting gateway cache as a 0097 regression; Retry-After without jitter; lock-retry Sign cost; the dev bearer as HS256; the tier-2/3 preset OD3 mismatch.
- **Settled docs non-findings.** The skeptic refuted every docs-only completeness gap on materiality (runbook causes, metrics rows, total-outage, web-playground flag sentence, upgrade render refusal). Only a docs contradiction of staged spec or an accepted outcome with no landing text clears the bar. security.md:164 "signed JWTs" for leases predates 0097.
- **Pre-existing drifts not to file.** Spec PgBouncer TCP 5432 against chart 6432; §25.4 "replaces" the PgBouncer rule while the chart adds; §17.4 State and resets omitting the dev bearer key; `writeOAuthError` lacking `detail`; §16.1 `lenny_oauth_token_5xx_total` labels; the plan's "§10.3".
- **Fixer must update Files touched.** A fixer that adds a target file to a deliverable also adds it to "Files touched on application" in the same file.

### Open

- **StoreRouter for `issued_tokens`** (separate, predates 0097): no routing rule, and §25.4 "Storage Routing" has no `lenny-ops` row ([spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **`lenny-ops` IdP verification** (separate, predates 0097): production `lenny-ops` verifies no upstream IdP token, so SPEC-11 Edit 2's IdP clause is unimplemented ([spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Playground `subject_token` revocation** (separate): §27.3.1 and §13.3 do not say whether a body-borne playground `subject_token` is checked against `pg:revoked` or whether the child inherits `origin`; D27 records the residual; impersonation bearers have no revocation record or row ([non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **§4.9 "Upstream egress"** (separate, predates 0097): says all other gateway egress is blocked, which was already false ([spec.1.fix-G4.1]).
- **Lease forgery clause** (separate, predates 0097): spec/04 "LLM Reverse Proxy" says KMS-key control lets an attacker forge lease tokens, against opaque leases ([spec.5.fix-design-G3.1]).
- **§17.4 surviving state** (separate): the dev bearer key also survives a non-purge `lenny down` ([spec.5.fix-design-G4.1]).
- **§8.2 child `exp`** (separate, predates 0097): `min(parent.exp, now + leaseTTL)` differs from the §13.3 formula ([spec.8.fix-G1.1]).
- **Operability agents in a Postgres outage** (separate, predates 0097): an agent holding only Lenny-issued tokens loses access within its token lifetime ([spec.16.review-reliability.1]).
- **Self-managed Postgres topology** (separate, predates 0097): answer files point `postgres.dsn` at `lenny-postgres-rw`; a direct chart fix owns it (summary Defects) ([non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]).
- **Azure Workload Identity** (separate, predates 0097): no pod-label hook on any deployment; the Azure overlay writes an unrendered `gateway.podAnnotations` ([non-spec.1.fix-design-G3.1]).
- **Unbacked `tokenExchange` rows** (separate): `rateLimitPerUser`, `requireActorToken`, and `supportedSubjectTokenTypes` in configuration.md name no chart key ([non-spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]).
- **Gateway `aud` pinning**: whether the gateway should pin `aud` on Lenny-issued bearers needs a spec decision ([non-spec.1.review-security.1]).
- **Playground `retryable` field** (separate, predates 0097): the playground `KMS_SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE` envelope omits `retryable: true` that §10.2 requires ([non-spec.2.fix-G3.1]).
- **Bootstrap `provisionAdminToken`**: it swallows a `Provision` sign failure, which SPEC-4 "every mint path" may contradict ([non-spec.5.fix-design-G3.1]).
- **Stale tier-5 comment** (separate): `admin_scope_narrowing_test.go` header says `/v1/oauth/token` is unreachable cross-process ([non-spec.5.fix-design-G4.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **Session-creation per-dialect cap**: whether the gateway session mint honours the cap, wired only in the Token Service ([spec.8.review-fresh.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **Embedded in-memory revocations**: a gateway pod restart may drop `issued_tokens` revocations while old tokens keep verifying ([spec.14.review-reliability.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **§11.6 breaker wording**: "managed by each gateway replica" while SPEC-4 puts a breaker in the Token Service ([spec.16.review-edit-sites.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **Rule 6 through `IsRevoked`**: `authmw` calls `IsRevoked(jti)` with no class information; practically moot ([non-spec.1.review-single-source.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **`waitReady` race**: the harness may return before the rule-1 startup read, racing the rule-4 gate in a tier-4 test ([non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **`waitReady` client timeout**: 500 ms against a `/readyz` that waits up to 2 s on a hanging dependency ([non-spec.10.review-fresh.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **Client-guide playground note**: whether docs/client-guide/authentication.md needs a note that a playground bearer is refused on `/v1/oauth/token` ([non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **S18 to S25 window**: a `--production` render with no `--kms-*` flags is fatal in intermediate commits ([non-spec.8.review-reliability.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **`lenny-ops` PlatformRoles**: `lenny-ops` trusts the token's roles claim while the gateway prefers the stored role; predates 0097 ([non-spec.8.review-security.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **S19 grep gate**: the "no non-test reference to RotatingVerifier" gate may hit comments in claimchecker.go and admintoken.go ([non-spec.12.review-applicability.1]).
- UNVERIFIED **install-wizard.md**: lists only the backup effect of `ops.production` ([non-spec.12.review-docs-alignment.1]).

### Deferred

- [summary.md, D19] ([spec.1.follow-up-fix.1], carried by [reconcile.1] to [reconcile.3]): D19 gives the reason for the `0.0.0.0/0` refusal but not for the private-endpoint requirement SPEC-10 Edit 1 now states. Append "and a public KMS endpoint spans provider ranges no single CIDR covers, so a cloud deployment uses the private endpoint's subnet". [non-spec.5.fix-design-G5.1] adds that D19 names only `0.0.0.0/0`, while the predicate also names `::/0`.
- [non-spec-changes.md, Edge cases and CHART-4] ([spec.1.follow-up-fix.1], carried by [reconcile.1] to [reconcile.3]): add "**Upgrade of a cloud-provider install.** The chart refuses to render until `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` is set and `kms.endpointCIDR` names the private KMS endpoint subnet (SPEC-10 Edit 1); the shipped `0.0.0.0/0` default fails, which also blocks the Token Service upgrade." CHART-4's `values.yaml` comment should name the private endpoint subnet rather than "a narrower CIDR". [non-spec.12.review-docs-alignment.1] lists "upgrade render refusal" among refuted docs-polish findings; a later pass may retire this on that ground.
- [non-spec-changes.md, DOCS-2] ([spec.1.review-docs-alignment.1], carried by [reconcile.1] to [reconcile.3]): DOCS-2 names no docs page mirroring the "dev HMAC JWT" `playground.devTenantId` wording other than configuration.md, and docs/runbooks/gateway-replica-failure.md has no KMS key-set startup-exit cause; the docs loop greps docs/ for "dev HMAC". [non-spec.12.review-docs-alignment.1] lists gateway-replica-failure among refuted docs-polish findings.
- [non-spec-changes.md, DOCS-2 configuration.md bullet] ([non-spec.5.fix-design-G5.1]): the docs/reference/configuration.md bullet says `kms.endpointCIDR` "must be narrower than `0.0.0.0/0` on cloud providers" and omits `::/0`, which SPEC-10 Edit 1 also refuses.
- [spec-changes.md, SPEC-11 Edit 6] ([non-spec.8.fix-G1.1], [non-spec.8.fix-design-G1.1], [non-spec.10.review-single-source.1]): the §25.4 "Probes" YAML sets no `timeoutSeconds`, so it implies kubelet's 1 s default against the §25.6 2 s probe timeouts. True after CODE-13: the chart renders `timeoutSeconds: 3` on all three probes. A spec round may add `timeoutSeconds: 3` to each probe in that YAML. It predates 0097 and the YAML is illustrative (its liveness values already differ from the chart), so it is a separate finding.
- [non-spec-changes.md, Edge cases and DOCS-2 web-playground.md bullet] ([non-spec.8.review-docs-alignment.1]): the D27 accepted residual (a playground bearer, before or after logout, exchanged as `subject_token` on `POST /v1/oauth/token` yields a child valid until the bearer's `exp`) is in summary D27 and this section's Open only; the non-spec Edge cases section and the DOCS-2 web-playground.md bullet should state it.

Absorbed on 2026-10-23 (owner-decisions fixer): CODE-9 now targets `cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go` and its test fake (CODE-9 item 4); CHART-1 moves the schema minimum and `schema_test.go` to 120; checklist S1, S3, S11, S14, S18, S20, S21, and S27 are reworded, S26 is removed, and S7 depends on S1.

## Ledger

### [spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` governs only the bearers `POST /v1/playground/token` mints; the session-creation `session_capability` token (pods present it; it is the delegation `actor_token`) keeps its per-dialect cap — BECAUSE pods cannot reach /v1/oauth/token and a session_capability rotation copies typ and caps exp at subject.exp (pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go header comment and Derive), so a 900 s session token would break pod→gateway calls and delegate_task 15 minutes into a session (sessions run up to maxSessionAgeSeconds 7200, spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:414) — ALTERNATIVES: gateway-side renewal timer (new mechanism for an out-of-scope, unimplemented mint); move pod auth to the SA token (leaves actor_token bounding delegation trees); user_bearer→session_capability grant (breaks typ immutability, spec/10:227); bound by maxSessionAgeSeconds (active-time cap that pauses in suspension).
WATCHOUT: Owner decision 2 in the problem statement names "/v1/oauth/token or the playground endpoint" as holder refresh paths. SPEC-3 Edit 1 no longer names /v1/oauth/token because no TTL-governed token renews through it; this narrows the scope of the 15-minute lifetime, it does not reopen decisions 2 or 3 (fixed lifetime, no re-mint, 900 default all hold). Do not re-add the /v1/oauth/token renewal clause or apply the TTL to the session-creation token without a renewal mechanism. — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Owner decisions" item 2; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:223
WATCHOUT: The knob name `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` now governs exactly the tokens `playground.bearerTtlSeconds` governed. A later finding may propose reverting the rename; it was kept because it matches owner decision 3's term and reverting cascades into CODE-8, CHART-1, DOCS-2, SPEC-3 Edits 3–6, SPEC-8, and the checklist. — EVIDENCE: summary.md D13
DECISION: Floor raised to 120 (`120 ≤ ttl ≤ 3600`) in the one home, SPEC-3 Edit 1; §27.2 row, §27.3.1 sentence, §17.8.1 row, CODE-8, CHART-1, and DOCS-2 drop their copies or point at SPEC-3 Edit 1 — BECAUSE ttl − 60 in the SPEC-8 sign-rate formula is zero at ttl = 60 and §27.3.1 fixes the client lead at 60 s (spec/27_web-playground.md:141) — ALTERNATIVES: writing 120 into every copy (keeps several homes).
FACT: §17.6 item 6 states no lifetime for the bootstrap admin credential; the 10-year value lives only in code, `DefaultTokenTTL = 10 * 365 * 24 * time.Hour` in pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go. SPEC-3 Edit 1 now states it in §10.2, which makes SPEC-2 step 2's "Its lifetime exceeds the step-3 wait" rest on spec text. — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:494-512
FACT: Playground mint invariant 4 (spec/10:259) now points at the "JWT claims" `expiry` rule (SPEC-3 Edit 2); CODE-8 item 2 cites SPEC-3 Edit 1 as the exp home. The dev-mode `exp = now + ttl` in the §27.3.1 Auth-by-mode row (SPEC-3 Edit 6) is left as is: dev has no subject and the per-dialect cap never binds below 3600.

### [spec.1.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The session-creation `session_capability` mint stays a gateway in-process mint and is bound to §13.3 write-before-issue statements (1)-(3) by one SPEC-1 "Signing topology" sentence, with `session_mint` added to the §16.7 `token.exchanged` exchange_type enum (SPEC-1 Edit 5). BECAUSE: SPEC-6 removes "Session tokens" from §4.3 item 3, which drops the issued_tokens row and audit that §4.3 plus §13.3 ("Internal token issuance paths ... flow through the Token Service", spec/13_security-model.md:656) implied, and §11.4 item 5 revokes through issued_tokens (spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:274). The admin rotation is the precedent: a gateway in-process mint that records both rows (spec/13_security-model.md:664). ALTERNATIVES: moving session tokens back to the Token Service needs a tokensv1 mint RPC (summary non-goal); a row without the audit row breaks the §13.3 "no token without an audit row" invariant.
WATCHOUT: The recording sentence names statements (1), (2), and (3) and never step (1a). A session mint has no `subject_token` to re-read, so writing "under the write-before-issue ordering" unqualified would lead an implementor to run (1a) on the authenticating token. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1 Edit 1 "Signing topology".
WATCHOUT: Do not extend the recording rule to impersonation bearers. A `token.exchanged` row for a cross-tenant mint falsifies "The `token.exchanged` audit event therefore always carries a single `tenant_id`" (spec/13_security-model.md:650). Under the fail-closed step (1a), impersonation and playground bearers cannot be exchanged, which matches "NOT routed through /v1/oauth/token". EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:650.
DECISION: Step (1a) fails closed on a missing row for a token the verifier key set verified, reusing `subject_token_revoked`/`actor_token_revoked`. BECAUSE: as staged it passed every row-less token, including a revoked playground bearer (Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27_web-playground.md:89,96). ALTERNATIVES: a new reason `subject_token_unrecorded` adds protocol surface for a case treated as revoked.
WATCHOUT: `session_mint` has one home, the §16.7 enum in SPEC-1 Edit 5. SPEC-1 Edit 1 names the audit row but not the value. Keep it that way.
FACT: The session-creation mint and its recording have no CODE deliverable and no test in this proposal (summary "Defects in the shipped tree": pkg/gateway/sessionserver has no signer). The later session-mint fix owns a tier-4 test asserting the issued_tokens row and the `session_mint` audit row commit before the token returns.
DECISION: §4.3 item 3 is the home for what the Token Service mints and §10.2 "Signing topology" for what the gateway mints in process; SPEC-1's Token Service list is cut to a pointer and SPEC-6's inserted sentence points at the §10.2 list. D9 reworded. BECAUSE: §4.3 item 3 is the tree's existing minted-here list and SPEC-6 already edits it.
DECISION: The `typ` stamping sentence (spec/10_gateway-internals.md:227) is restaged as SPEC-1 Edit 4: "The minting component stamps `typ` alongside the other claims when it signs the token". BECAUSE: SPEC-7 moves signing ahead of the transaction and SPEC-1 makes the Token Service a signer. Left "minted on every gateway-issued JWT" (line 221) alone: the Token Service already minted a2a_delegation and service_token under the current §4.3.
DECISION: SPEC-7 Edit 2's restatement of the subject_token_revoked rule and the closing "step (1a) re-read rejects ..." clause are deleted; step (1a) in Edit 1 is the one home. The exp-clamp half of the closing sentence stays, because it replaces the current "re-read happens inside the same advisory-locked transaction as the signing step" justification.
OPEN: spec/13_security-model.md:616 says the bootstrap lenny-admin rotation is "the one exception" to issuance through /v1/oauth/token. SPEC-6 and SPEC-1 (pre-existing in the proposal, before this group) make session-creation, playground, and impersonation gateway in-process mints, so that sentence is false after the proposal lands and no staged edit covers it. A later spec round should judge whether to stage it.

### [spec.1.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: One KMS egress peer (`kms.endpointCIDR`, TCP 443) for the gateway and `lenny-ops`, rendered whenever `kms.provider` is not `local`, with a chart render refusal of `0.0.0.0/0` or `::/0` on a cloud provider. Normative home is SPEC-10 Edit 1 (§13.2.4 Gateway row); lenny-ops row, §25.4 YAML comment, §17.1 row, CHART-4, and D19 point at it — BECAUSE the gateway's only external 443 path (`allow-gateway-egress-llm-upstream`) renders only with a proxy-mode pool (spec/13_security-model.md:389), excepts RFC1918, and can be narrowed by `egressCIDRs.llmProviders`, so a gateway could not reach KMS for `LoadKeySet`/`Sign` — ALTERNATIVES: private-endpoint-only condition for the gateway (crashloops on public endpoint without proxy pool); copy allow-token-service's always-rendered 0.0.0.0/0 peer (cancels NET-057 excepts, spec/13:475); render llm-upstream unconditionally (broadens egress, still blocks private endpoints); preflight check instead of render fail (CHART-1 already uses render-time fail for signQuotaPerSecond).
WATCHOUT: The retired option "lenny-ops KMS peer only when endpointCIDR is narrower than 0.0.0.0/0; a public KMS endpoint passes the external HTTPS/webhook rule" lost because that reasoning holds for lenny-ops but never for the gateway, and under the render refusal the narrower-than condition is always true (a dead second condition). Do not reintroduce it. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:275 (Gateway row has no KMS peer), :389 (llm-upstream render condition)
FACT: The §13.2.4 Gateway row anchor "`kube-system` CoreDNS (UDP/TCP 53); external HTTPS (TCP 443, rendered" occurs once in spec/; the only chart KMS peer today is in `allow-token-service` (charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:271); values.yaml:894-897 comment claims the 0.0.0.0/0 default is "bounded by the per-port (443) egress rule". — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:894
WATCHOUT: Existing helm unittests set a cloud provider without `kms.endpointCIDR` (charts/lenny/tests/token-service-deployment_test.yaml:166 `kms.provider: aws`; gateway-deployment_test.yaml:736 `provider: azure`). helm-unittest renders only the templates a suite names, so the guard in system-network-policies.yaml/ops-network-policies.yaml will not fire there, but any suite that renders those templates under a cloud provider must set a narrow CIDR. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/tests/token-service-deployment_test.yaml:166
FACT: The Token Service row and `allow-token-service` peer are unchanged; under the refusal its rendered CIDR is simply the narrow operator value. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:276

Lands the `lenny-ops` KMS egress rule." CHART-4 now renders the gateway and `lenny-ops` KMS peers and the render refusal across system-network-policies.yaml, ops-network-policies.yaml, and values.yaml; S27 should read "Lands the gateway and `lenny-ops` KMS egress rule." Dependencies S10 and S25 stay correct. S10 ("Lands the §13.2.4, §25.4, and §17.1 egress edits") stays true.
OPEN: spec/04_system-components.md §4.9 "Upstream egress" says all other gateway egress is blocked; already false before this proposal (PgBouncer, Redis, Token Service, apiserver). Separate finding for a later round; not edited here.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds governs only the bearers POST /v1/playground/token mints. The session-creation session_capability token (the token pods present, and the parent token used as the §8.2 delegation actor_token) keeps its per-dialect cap, the same way D13 already exempts delegated children — BECAUSE no holder of that token has a refresh path: pods cannot reach /v1/oauth/token, and a rotation of a session_capability token is capped at subject.exp and copies typ (pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go:232-260). A 900 s cap would break pod→gateway calls and delegate_task (actor_token_expired) 15 minutes into a 7200 s session — ALTERNATIVES: (a) a gateway-side timer that renews each session's token (new per-session state, has to survive coordinator handoff; rejected as a large new mechanism for a mint path that is out of scope and unimplemented); (b) moving pod auth to the SA token only (it does not fix the delegation actor, whose exp still bounds the whole tree); (c) building the actor from session state in place of a token (a large §8.2/§13.3 rewrite that weakens the actor_token_expired predicate); (d) letting a user_bearer exchange mint a session_capability (contradicts typ immutability at spec/10:227 and spec/13:646).
DECISION: the lower bound rises to 120, so ttl − 60 (the §27.3.1 client refresh lead) is at least 60 s. SPEC-3 Edit 1 is the only site that states the bound. The §27.2 row, §27.3.1 Edit 4, and the §17.8.1 row drop their copies.
DECISION: SPEC-3 Edit 1 now states the bootstrap admin credential's 10-year lifetime, matching pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go DefaultTokenTTL. §17.6 states no lifetime, so the old citation pointed at nothing.
FACT: playground bearers are typ session_capability with no session_id (pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go completeMint). The shipped code sets exp = now + BearerTTL and does not apply the subject-exp term. CODE-8 item 2 now points at the SPEC-3 Edit 1 rule, which carries all three terms — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go:259
FACT: maxSessionAgeSeconds caps active time only and pauses while the session is suspended (spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:414). It is therefore the wrong wall-clock bound for a session token, so the session-creation token gets the per-dialect cap — EVIDENCE: spec/05:414
WATCHOUT: under this design the knob governs the same tokens playground.bearerTtlSeconds governed, so the rename is cosmetic and D13 justifies it only by owner decision 3's name. A later round may file "revert the rename". That revert would cascade into CODE-8, CHART-1, DOCS-2, SPEC-3 Edits 3-6, SPEC-8, and the checklist. Do not do it piecemeal.
WATCHOUT: the SPEC-8 Tier-3 example still uses 10,000 holders. Under this design that figure counts every Tier-3 session as a playground holder, which makes it an upper bound. It is not wrong, but a capacity lens may question it.
OPEN: whether session-creation session_capability tokens should eventually carry the 15-minute lifetime (which needs a gateway-side renewal of the session's token for pods and the delegation actor) belongs to the follow-up proposal that implements the session-creation mint and delegation signing. It is not an owner question for 0097.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: session-creation `session_capability` stays a gateway in-process mint, and SPEC-1 "Signing topology" gains one clause: the gateway records it in `issued_tokens` with its `token.exchanged` row under statements (1)-(3) of §13.3 "Write-before-issue ordering". §16.7 gains `exchange_type: session_mint` — BECAUSE the bootstrap admin rotation is already a gateway in-process mint that records both rows (spec/13_security-model.md:664, cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go adminRotationExchangeType), so this extends an existing pattern — ALTERNATIVES: keep "Session tokens" in §4.3 at the Token Service (rejected: playground bearers are already gateway-minted session-capability JWTs, the restaged "After authentication, the gateway mints a signed JWT" would contradict §4.3 again, and it needs the Token Service mint RPC the non-goal at summary.md:64 rejects). Extending the rule to impersonation bearers was also rejected: their missing row predates this proposal, and recording `token.exchanged` for a cross-tenant mint breaks spec/13_security-model.md:650 "therefore always carries a single tenant_id".
DECISION: step (1a) fails closed. A token that the verifier key set verified and that has no `issued_tokens` row is rejected with the same `*_token_revoked` reason. Only a token the key set did not verify (an upstream IdP token) passes on a missing row — BECAUSE playground bearers (revoked in Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27_web-playground.md:96) and impersonation bearers have no row, so a revoked playground bearer presented to /v1/oauth/token would otherwise mint a fresh recorded token — ALTERNATIVES: a new reason code (rejected: new surface for a case that is treated as revoked).
FACT: issued_tokens rows outlive a token's exp by the trailing hour (§13.3 "Authoritative durability"; GC, erasure, and tenant deletion Phase 4 delete only past it), so the missing-row rejection never fires on a valid, recorded token — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:676
FACT: no gateway path mints a `user_bearer` today; the only gateway mints are impersonation (no row, pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go), admin (row, pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go IssuedTokens), and playground (no row, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go). The §10.2 "bootstrap OIDC login path" user_bearer mint has no code.
WATCHOUT: once step (1a) fails closed, a later "bootstrap OIDC login" user_bearer mint that writes no row would make that bearer impossible to rotate. Whoever implements that path must record the row — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:222
WATCHOUT: RecordWithRotationAudit's ErrNoRows branch (pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go "Previous token absent or already revoked") is reachable after CODE-9 only for an IdP-issued subject, so CODE-9 item 3 must say that rather than "a missing row".
DECISION: finding 2 makes §4.3 item 3 the home for Token Service mints and §10.2 "Signing topology" the home for gateway mints. Each cites the other, and D9 is reworded to match — BECAUSE §4.3 is the tree's existing list ("minted here"), so the proposal's duplicate in §10.2 is the copy to remove.
OPEN: impersonation bearers have no issued_tokens row, so §11.4 item 5 user invalidation cannot revoke them durably. This predates 0097 and is separate from G2.
DEFERRED [0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: step S1 says SPEC-1 "Lands the §10.2 signing model, signing topology, and playground signer edits". After G2, SPEC-1 also carries Edit 4 (the §10.2 `typ` stamping sentence) and Edit 5 (§16.7 `token.exchanged` `session_mint`), so S1 should name SPEC-1 without listing its parts, or add those two.

FACT: chart default `kms.provider` is "local" but nothing ties `global.devMode`/`LENNY_DEV_MODE` to it, so staged text must not say dev mode signs "with the local provider key". EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:879, spec/27_web-playground.md:61.

The docs mirrors (quickstart.md:58, installation.md:33, local-development.md:18 and :32) drop key management/KMS from their in-memory store list in DOCS-2. Their "no separate ... KMS ... process" clause stays, because it remains true.

### [spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: one KMS egress rule for the gateway and lenny-ops. The peer is `kms.endpointCIDR` on TCP 443, rendered when `kms.provider` is not `local`, and the chart refuses to render on a cloud provider while `kms.endpointCIDR` is `0.0.0.0/0` or `::/0`. The rule's home is the §13.2.4 Gateway row (SPEC-10 Edit 1). The lenny-ops row and the §25.4 YAML comment point to it — BECAUSE the gateway has no unconditional external-HTTPS rule (llm-upstream renders only with proxy pools, and `egressCIDRs.llmProviders` can narrow it), so "a public endpoint passes the external 443 rule" (old D19) holds for lenny-ops only. Requiring a narrow CIDR also makes lenny-ops's "narrower than 0.0.0.0/0" condition always true, so keeping two conditions would be hair — ALTERNATIVES: (a) a gateway peer only when the CIDR is narrower, as lenny-ops had it: a default cloud install with a public endpoint and no proxy pool then crashloops at LoadKeySet; (b) copy the Token Service's always-rendered peer with default 0.0.0.0/0: NetworkPolicy allows are additive, so the peer cancels the llm-upstream excepts, which violates NET-057; (c) render llm-upstream unconditionally: this broadens gateway egress for no-proxy installs, and llmProviders narrowing would still block KMS; (d) a 0.0.0.0/0-with-excepts KMS peer: this re-broadens an llmProviders-narrowed gateway, which defeats the compliance narrowing.
FACT: the gateway already takes `--kms-provider` and wraps §4.9 connector credentials through KMS (values.yaml kms comment, gateway-deployment.yaml --kms-provider). Its missing KMS egress therefore predates 0097, and 0097 makes it fatal at startup. allow-gateway-egress has no KMS peer, and allow-token-service always renders `kms.endpointCIDR` — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml (allow-token-service ipBlock kms.endpointCIDR; allow-gateway-egress ends at kube-dns); charts/lenny/templates/gateway-llm-upstream-egress.yaml is the separate llm-upstream template.
FACT: the §17.1 NetworkPolicies row lists only lenny-ops policies, and no gateway-egress row exists in §17.1. The gateway clause therefore does not go into §17.1 — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.1 table, NetworkPolicies row.
WATCHOUT: helm-unittest renders only the templates a suite lists. Put the endpointCIDR `fail` in a template that every cloud-provider render reaches (or in a helper that system-network-policies.yaml and ops-network-policies.yaml both call). Existing suites that set `kms.provider: aws` and render those templates need `kms.endpointCIDR` set — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/tests/gateway-deployment_test.yaml, token-service-deployment_test.yaml set a cloud provider.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S27 reads "Lands the `lenny-ops` KMS egress rule", but CHART-4 now lands the gateway and lenny-ops KMS egress peers and the cloud-provider `kms.endpointCIDR` render guard. Reword S27 to "Lands the gateway and `lenny-ops` KMS egress rule" (dependencies S10, S25 unchanged).
OPEN: spec/04 §4.9 "Upstream egress" says all gateway egress other than llm-upstream "including cluster-internal CIDRs ... is blocked". That was already false (PgBouncer, Redis, apiserver, Token Service) before this fix. It is a separate finding for a later round.

### [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
Corrected (spec-changes.md only):
- SPEC-8 Edit 2 "KMS Sign rate": Tier 3 example input "10,000 holders" -> "at most 10,000 playground bearer holders, the Tier 3 session ceiling" (an upper bound from a §16.5 tier parameter, so "terms come from §16.5" holds; figures 230/s and 690/s unchanged).
- SPEC-10 Edit 1 (§13.2.4 Gateway row): after the 0.0.0.0/0 refusal, added "so a cloud deployment sets it to the subnet of the provider's private KMS endpoint".
DECISION: keep the chart-wide 0.0.0.0/0 refusal and require the private KMS endpoint subnet on cloud providers — BECAUSE the gateway's only external-HTTPS rule (allow-gateway-egress-llm-upstream) renders only under gateway.llmUpstreamEgress.enabled, so the gateway needs its own KMS peer, and a public regional KMS endpoint spans provider ranges no single CIDR covers — ALTERNATIVES: list-valued kms.endpointCIDR (provider ranges for KMS are not published narrowly: AWS ip-ranges has no KMS service key, GCP uses all Google API ranges, Azure's AzureKeyVault tag is large; also forces a type change on the always-rendered allow-token-service peer); scoping the refusal to gateway/lenny-ops peers (the value is shared, so the gateway peer would still be unrestricted); omitting the peer when unrestricted (fails when llmUpstreamEgress is disabled or egressCIDRs.llmProviders is narrowed).
FACT: gateway external HTTPS egress is conditional — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-llm-upstream-egress.yaml:22 `{{- if .Values.gateway.llmUpstreamEgress.enabled }}`; allow-gateway-egress (system-network-policies.yaml:535) has no 443 internet rule.
WATCHOUT: the refusal breaks `helm upgrade` for every cloud-provider install on the shipped default endpointCIDR "0.0.0.0/0" (charts/lenny/values.yaml:897), Token Service included, exactly as CHART-1 item 2's required kms.signQuotaPerSecond already does — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-10 Edit 1; non-spec-changes.md CHART-1 item 2.
DEFERRED [summary.md]: D19 gives the reason for the refusal but not for the private-endpoint requirement SPEC-10 Edit 1 now states; append "and a public KMS endpoint spans provider ranges no single CIDR covers, so a cloud deployment uses the private endpoint's subnet".
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, Edge cases]: add "**Upgrade of a cloud-provider install.** The chart refuses to render until `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` is set and `kms.endpointCIDR` names the private KMS endpoint subnet (SPEC-10 Edit 1); the shipped `0.0.0.0/0` default fails, which also blocks the Token Service upgrade." CHART-4's values.yaml comment should name the private endpoint subnet rather than only "a narrower CIDR".
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, DOCS-2 + Files touched]: SPEC-9 Edit 3 removes KMS from the §17.4 in-memory store list, but docs/getting-started/quickstart.md:58 ("Postgres, Redis, and KMS"), docs/operator-guide/installation.md:33 and docs/runtime-author-guide/local-development.md:32 ("Postgres, Redis, key management, and artifact storage") still list it; add a DOCS-2 bullet removing KMS/key management from those lists (the local KMS is keyed by the chart-generated master key Secret) and add the three paths to files-touched. Not landed: authoring is outside this loop's non-spec grant.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-9 tests]: the tier-2 issuedtokenstore cases cannot pin the Lenny-vs-IdP classification, which persistIssuedToken makes (CODE-9 item 1). Add tier-1 pkg/tokenservice case: key-set-verified subject's jti is in the recheck list, upstream-verified subject's is not, and a key-set-verified subject whose fake store reports a missing row returns 400 invalid_grant subject_token_revoked.
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md, CODE-9 Targets + Files touched]: RecordWithAudit is also declared in the adminIssuedTokenStore interface and called from cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go (:177, :269) with a fake in user_revocation_test.go (:247); add both to CODE-9 targets (the admin-rotation call passes an empty recheck list) and to the cmd/lenny-gateway files-touched list.

### [spec.1.review-performance.1]
FACT: Tier-3 replica counts that size the SPEC-8 per-replica sign limit are gateway 5 min / 30 max and Token Service 4 — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.8.2 "Gateway replicas (min / max)" row and "Operational defaults by tier" Token Service replicas row
DECISION: filed SPEC-8/SPEC-4/SPEC-5 per-replica limit floor(Q/(G+T)) with G = HPA max as a design defect — BECAUSE at min scale the fleet sign capacity is (5+4)/34 of Q (about 180/s at Q=690), below SPEC-8's own 230/s sustained figure, and rate_limited is excluded from both alerts so the refusals are silent — ALTERNATIVES: a separate alert-only finding (rejected, same root)
FACT: the shipped gateway already calls the KMS at startup (NewKMSSigner seals through WrapDEK and log.Fatalf on error), so SPEC-2's load-at-start crashloop is no regression for the gateway — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/kmssigner.go:65-88, cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:88-90
FACT: SPEC-7 replaces the Redis-backed revocation-cache read with a Postgres issued_tokens re-read inside the lock; this is more durable under a Redis reset, and the sign moved outside the lock removes the per-tenant serialization — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md §13.3 "Actor-token freshness under concurrent rotation"

### [spec.1.review-plan-conformance.1]
FACT: The only plan step 0097 claims is the queued direct fix F-10.2.22 under "Queued direct fixes filed by 0095's implementation (2026-10-23)". Its deliverables are one shared signing key on every gateway and Token Service replica, its rotation set, and a spec statement of the shared key source. SPEC-1 and SPEC-2 stage all three. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3138-3142
WATCHOUT: The plan says "one shared sealed key" and "(§10.3)". Both are stale. Owner decision 1 rejects the sealed HS256 key, and the problem statement records that divergence. Rotation lives in §10.2. Neither point is a conformance finding. EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md:12, :172
FACT: The plan-wide claim-register and reachability rules in plan §2.1 and §2.3 cover the gateway-runtime channel surface. JWT signing sits outside that surface. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:113-117, :139

### [spec.1.review-reliability.1]
FACT: §10.2 says pods hold session_capability tokens ("Pods present these back to the gateway"; "The gateway validates the signature on every pod→gateway request"), and §8.2/§13.3 make the parent session token the delegation actor_token. Default maxSessionAgeSeconds is 7200. With a fixed 900 s capability lifetime, the SPEC-3 refresh sentence names only playground and API-client holders, so pod-side use and delegation after minute 15 have no stated path. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:223, :246; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59; spec/05_runtime-registry-and-pool-model.md:413
FACT: the existing JWTSigner breaker code has a 30 s cooldown with a half-open probe, so "remaining breaker cooldown" in the SPEC-4 Retry-After rule refers to a real quantity even though the spec does not state the cooldown. — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:53-55
FACT: the bootstrap admin token the gateway mints lives 10 years (admintoken.DefaultTokenTTL) and impersonation bearers default to 1 h, so SPEC-2 step 3's statement that the 24 h cap bounds every non-bootstrap old-key token holds for those classes. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go:65; pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go:63
DECISION: did not file the missing KMS Sign per-call deadline — BECAUSE request contexts may already bound it and code-best-practices requires outbound timeouts; it is implementation hardening rather than a spec defect — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a breaker-never-trips defect was rejected as unsure.
DECISION: did not file the crashloop-on-KMS-outage-at-start behaviour — BECAUSE the proposal's edge-case list accepts it explicitly as the fail-closed cost.

### [spec.1.review-security.1]
FACT: The gateway has no KMS egress in spec or chart. §13.2.4 Gateway row lists no KMS endpoint (only the Token Service row does), and the gateway's external HTTPS policy renders only when a proxy-mode credential pool exists (spec/13_security-model.md:389; charts/lenny/templates/gateway-llm-upstream-egress.yaml `if .Values.gateway.llmUpstreamEgress.enabled`). allow-gateway-egress (charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:535) has no kms.endpointCIDR peer; allow-token-service does (:270). — EVIDENCE: as cited
WATCHOUT: A gateway KMS peer cannot use the Token Service's default `kms.endpointCIDR: 0.0.0.0/0` form, because NET-057 forbids the gateway weaker SSRF boundaries than lenny-ops and an additive 0.0.0.0/0 allow cancels the RFC1918/IMDS excepts. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:475; charts/lenny/values.yaml:897
FACT: §13.3 Write-before-issue already has the rejected-exchange audit discipline SPEC-7 step (1a) points to ("For rejected exchanges ... written under the same advisory lock and COMMIT discipline"). — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:654
FACT: Playground bearers are revoked in Redis (`pg:revoked:{jti}`) rather than in issued_tokens. — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:96
FACT: §17.6 states no lifetime for the bootstrap admin token. The code uses 10 years (pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go:65). — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:505-510
FACT: The embedded-mode HMAC dev bearer (§17.4 "Production warning banner") is treated by the proposal as a separate issuer (summary.md:79), so SPEC-1's "rejects HS256" for Lenny-issued tokens does not contradict it. Do not file it.
OPEN: Pods present session_capability tokens back to the gateway (§10.2 typ bullet). SPEC-3 gives refresh paths only for playground and API-client holders. Nobody has checked whether a 15-min fixed lifetime strands pod-held tokens. Not filed, because §10.2 says little about the pod-held token.

### [spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: Step (1a) re-reads only the `issued_tokens` rows that exist and rejects only on `revoked_at`; a token with no row passes (SPEC-7 Edit 1). CODE-9 lists the subject and actor `jti` unconditionally and fails only on `revoked_at` — BECAUSE playground bearers (revoked in Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27 §27.3.1 "Per-request revocation check") and impersonation bearers (ticket store, pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go) never have rows, so failing closed on a missing row rejected every §8.2 delegation whose `subject_token` is such a bearer (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md "Child-token minting": subject_token is the authenticated user's JWT) — ALTERNATIVES: external-only rejection (Token Service has one handler for internal and external exchanges, no predicate); exempting by `origin`/typ (enumerates exceptions; each future unrecorded mint becomes another); recording playground/impersonation bearers (dual-write of playground revocation; cross-tenant impersonation breaks the single-`tenant_id` token.exchanged rule, spec/13 §13.3); actor-only missing-row rule (no consumer).
WATCHOUT: The withdrawn fail-closed missing-row rule (round 1, [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]) lost because "verified by the key set" does not imply "has an issued_tokens row": playground and impersonation bearers are key-set-verified with no row. Do not reintroduce it in a narrower form (external-only, by origin, typ, or caller), and do not add a step (1a) sentence saying where playground/impersonation revocation is checked for a subject_token: no spec section or code path does that today. — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:96-97; spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go (jti recorded only on the oidc session record)
OPEN: A revoked playground or impersonation bearer presented as a `subject_token` to /v1/oauth/token (or as a delegation subject) is not checked against `pg:revoked` or the impersonation ticket store by the Token Service. This predates 0097 and no staged edit addresses it; a later proposal owns it.
FACT: RecordWithRotationAudit's `pgx.ErrNoRows` branch ("Previous token absent or already revoked") is reachable after CODE-9 only for a subject with no row (any row-less subject, playground included, not only IdP). — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go:276
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1]: the DECISION "Step (1a) fails closed on a missing row" and the WATCHOUT clause "Under the fail-closed step (1a), impersonation and playground bearers cannot be exchanged" are withdrawn; a row-less token passes step (1a).
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]: the DECISION "step (1a) fails closed", the WATCHOUT that a row-less bootstrap-OIDC user_bearer would be impossible to rotate, and the WATCHOUT that the ErrNoRows branch is reachable "only for an IdP-issued subject" are wrong; row-less tokens pass and the ErrNoRows branch is reachable for any row-less subject.
CORRECTS [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]: the DEFERRED tier-1 pkg/tokenservice Lenny-vs-IdP classification case is moot; CODE-9 no longer classifies.
DECISION: §13.3 "Token issuance and rotation" exception sentence is staged as SPEC-7 Edit 5 and points at §10.2 "Signing topology" rather than listing the mints — BECAUSE "Signing topology" is the one home for the gateway's in-process mint set (D9) and SPEC-7 already owns §13.3, so checklist S7 and Spec-files-touched stay true — ALTERNATIVES: enumerating the mints in §13.3 (second home); staging in SPEC-6 (makes checklist S6 stale, adds spec/13 to SPEC-6); deleting the sentence (loses the admin-rotation parenthetical's referent).
USEFUL [spec.1.fix-G2.1]: its OPEN on spec/13_security-model.md:616 "the one exception" named the exact sentence.
WATCHOUT: docs/getting-started/architecture.md:192 and docs/operator-guide/security.md:108 and :114 still say the bootstrap admin credential is the only in-process mint (and :114 lists session tokens on the RFC 8693 path). These are false after SPEC-1/SPEC-6 land, independent of SPEC-7 Edit 5; the non-spec docs loop owns them (DOCS-2 does not cover them).

### [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Step (1a) re-reads only the issued_tokens rows that exist. The key-set/IdP classification clause is replaced by "a token with no `issued_tokens` row passes this step". CODE-9 item 1 lists the subject and actor jti without classifying them, and fails only on `revoked_at`. — BECAUSE the fail-closed rule rested on the premise that every Lenny-issued token has a row. That premise is false for playground bearers (revoked in Redis `pg:revoked`, spec/27_web-playground.md "Per-request revocation check") and for impersonation bearers (ticket store, pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go End). An issued_tokens re-read cannot see their revocation either way, and it rejected every §8.2 delegation whose subject_token is such a bearer (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md "Child-token minting"). The actor always has a row: it is the SPEC-1 session_mint token or a Token Service child. — ALTERNATIVES: (a) apply the missing-row rule only to the external endpoint. This narrows a false premise, and the Token Service has one handler for internal and external calls. (b) Exempt `origin: "playground"` and impersonation bearers. This enumerates exceptions and is hair. (c) Record playground and impersonation bearers in issued_tokens. This is a new mechanism, it needs a dual-write of playground revocation, and a token.exchanged row for a cross-tenant impersonation mint breaks spec/13 "always carries a single tenant_id".
WATCHOUT: Do not restore any missing-row rejection, whether by origin, by caller, or by verifier. The round-1 reason for it ("a revoked playground bearer presented to /v1/oauth/token would mint a fresh recorded token") is a revocation-store gap that predates 0097. The Token Service's `verifyExchangeTokens` (pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go) checks no revocation for subject_token, and §27.3.1 owns playground revocation. A rotation of a row-less subject commits through RecordWithRotationAudit's ErrNoRows branch, and its exp is capped at the subject's exp. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go RecordWithRotationAudit "Previous token absent or already revoked"
OPEN: (security lens, separate finding, predates 0097) Nothing in spec or code applies the §27.3.1 playground revocation check, or the impersonation ticket end, to a subject_token presented on /v1/oauth/token. §13.3 "no grace period" says every validation consults the revocation cache. Whether the Token Service's subject verification is such a validation is unstated.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G2.1]: "Under the fail-closed step (1a), impersonation and playground bearers cannot be exchanged, which matches 'NOT routed through /v1/oauth/token'" is wrong twice. "NOT routed" describes how those bearers are minted, not whether they can be used as a delegation subject. Step (1a) no longer fails closed.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G2.1]: the step (1a) fail-closed DECISION is withdrawn (see above). The WATCHOUT on RecordWithRotationAudit's ErrNoRows branch now reads "reachable only for a subject with no row", not "only for an IdP-issued subject".
CORRECTS [spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]: the DEFERRED tier-1 pkg/tokenservice case that pins the Lenny-vs-IdP recheck classification is moot. CODE-9 no longer classifies.
DECISION: The §13.3 "Token issuance and rotation" sentence "The bootstrap `lenny-admin` Secret rotation is the one exception ..." becomes a pointer to §10.2 "Signing topology", as new SPEC-7 Edit 5. It names the admin rotation only so the following parenthetical keeps a referent. — BECAUSE "Signing topology" is the one home for the gateway's in-process mint set (D9), and SPEC-7 already owns §13.3, so checklist S7 ("Lands the §13.3 and §8.2 edits") and the Spec-files-touched list stay true. — ALTERNATIVES: list the mints in §13.3 (a second home, and the same enumerating answer one step wider); put the edit in SPEC-6 (S6 "Lands the §4.3 item 3 edit" would then be stale, and the fixer cannot change the checklist).
OPEN: (docs loop) docs/operator-guide/security.md "Serves the canonical POST /v1/oauth/token ..." bullet and its "Bearer tokens" item, and docs/getting-started/architecture.md "Externally, token issuance ...", still name the admin credential as the only in-process mint and list session tokens on the RFC 8693 path. SPEC-6 and SPEC-1 make that false. DOCS-2 does not cover these pages.

### [spec.3.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: The §8.2 delegation exchange sets actor_token to the parent session token and subject_token to the user's JWT. A playground or impersonation bearer as subject passes (1a) and is revoked through the playground Redis key, a separate pre-existing mechanism.
