### [spec.3.review-security.1]
DECISION: no security finding on the r2->r3 diff (SPEC-7 Edit 1 step (1a) now passes any token with no issued_tokens row; new SPEC-7 Edit 5) — BECAUSE Postgres issued_tokens.revoked_at is the sole authoritative revocation store and the revocation cache is fed only from it, so a row-less token (playground, impersonation) was never revocable through that store; passing it at (1a) removes no existing control — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing fail-closed missing-row, rejected as it was the r2 defect that broke delegation from playground/impersonation sessions.
FACT: revocation cache is a latency optimization over issued_tokens; validation falls through to `SELECT revoked_at FROM issued_tokens WHERE jti = $1` — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:674, :676; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:274
FACT: SPEC-7 Edit 5 target text "The bootstrap `lenny-admin` Secret rotation is the one exception: it is a gateway-direct in-process mint that does not go through `/v1/oauth/token`" exists verbatim — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:616

### [spec.4.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 3 points the §25.4 "Startup and Readiness" listener sentence at the §10.2 key-set load ("starts its HTTP listener immediately after it loads the verifier key set") — BECAUSE §10.2 (SPEC-2) already states "a process that cannot load a mapped entry does not start"; a citation avoids a second home — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's "one exception" sentence (restates SPEC-2); lazy key-set load (contradicts SPEC-2's single startup load).
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 2 cites §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation" and "Authoritative durability for revocation" for Lenny-issued tokens on lenny-ops, with the fail-closed refusal scoped to Lenny-issued tokens — BECAUSE §13.3's fail-closed sentence names "a gateway replica" and refuses every token; IdP tokens have no issued_tokens row, so refusing them gains nothing — ALTERNATIVES: reviewer's literal revoked_at clause (omits cache, propagation, fail-closed); fail-open on stale cache (contradicts §13.3 "we refuse to serve"); gateway introspection RPC (new surface); refusing Lenny-issued tokens on lenny-ops entirely (§25.1 "Issuing a scoped token" needs them).
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 4 appends "`lenny-ops` refuses Lenny-issued tokens for the duration ([Section 25.4](#254-the-lenny-ops-service) "Authentication")." to the §25.15 "Postgres down" and "Postgres + Redis both down" Impact cells — BECAUSE those rows are lenny-ops' per-dependency impact inventory and would otherwise overstate what a scoped-token caller can do.
FACT: lenny-ops has no revocation read today; buildAuthConfig wires only an HMAC verifier plus optional iss ClaimChecker; authmw.Options already has Revocations and RevocationFreshness hooks, consulted after Verify, and RevocationFreshness.Stale() is applied to every bearer — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:171-189; pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:195-208, :487, :495
WATCHOUT: the design for this group asked the fixer to add CODE-6 item 6, tier-1 lenny-ops revocation tests, a TEST-1 rotation assertion, a DOCS-2 bullet, and a summary CODE-6 index suffix. The spec-loop grant forbids authoring non-spec content, and no existing non-spec statement is falsified by the spec edit, so none of these landed. The summary CODE-6 index line was left unchanged so it stays true of CODE-6 as written. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 items 1-5 (no revocation item)
DEFERRED [non-spec-changes.md]: CODE-6 does not implement SPEC-11 Edit 2's revocation check. What is owed: wire authmw.Options.Revocations and RevocationFreshness in cmd/lenny-ops buildAuthConfig from a revocation.Cache rehydrated from issued_tokens and fed by token.revoked, as cmd/lenny-gateway does; scope both checks to tokens the KeySet verified (unscoped wiring 503s IdP and dev-hook callers during a Postgres outage). Tier-1 cmd/lenny-ops tests (// spec: 25.4, 13.3): revoked key-set token -> TOKEN_REVOKED; stale cache -> 503 token_validation_unavailable for a key-set token, while a dev-trust-hook token is admitted (the discriminating case). TEST-1 item 2 Rotation: the replaced admin credential is refused on lenny-ops after step 2. DOCS-2: docs/operator-guide/agent-operability.md failure table rows "Postgres down" and "Postgres + Redis both down" (lines ~356, ~358) state that lenny-ops refuses Lenny-issued tokens while Postgres is unreachable. Summary CODE-6 index line then gains ", and the revocation check".
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S11 says "Lands the §18.33 and §25.4 authentication edits." SPEC-11 now also lands the §25.4 "Startup and Readiness" edit (Edit 3) and the §25.15 Postgres outage rows (Edit 4). Rewrite S11 as the pointer "Lands SPEC-11."
OPEN: spec/25 §25.15 closing paragraph ("even during storage outages (Postgres and/or Redis down), the core operational loop ... continues to function") stays true for IdP-authenticated callers but not for agents holding §25.1 scoped Lenny-issued tokens once SPEC-11 Edit 2 lands. A later round decides whether it needs a caveat or an Edit 4 extension.
OPEN: lenny-ops' new issued_tokens read has no §25.4 "Storage Routing" row, and §12.6 assigns issued_tokens no StoreRouter method for any reader (design marked separate-finding).

### [spec.4.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: Removed the client-side per-replica KMS sign rate limit (`⌊kms.signQuotaPerSecond ÷ (G + T)⌋`, `--kms-sign-rate-limit`, `--kms-sign-burst`, `BreakerSigner.Limit/Burst`, the `rate_limited` outcome, and CHART-3) from the whole proposal. Provider throttling is the only quota enforcement. — BECAUSE an even per-replica split caps the fixed-size Token Service (2/2/4 replicas, no autoscaler) at 4Q/34 of the quota at Tier 3, below its lease-plus-delegation demand, and the limiter was undefined under `kms.provider: local`. The provider throttle already returns the same retryable 503 fleet-wide and does not trip the breaker. — ALTERNATIVES: rejected a per-component Token Service share value (adds a value, validation, docs, and chart formulas, and still only approximates the provider throttle); rejected non-partitioning ceilings ⌊Q/G⌋ and ⌊Q/T⌋ (the fleet can reach 2Q, so the limiter protects nothing); rejected adding only a "not local" condition (leaves the starvation); rejected an optional limiter (a dual mode). The reason lives only in summary D15.
WATCHOUT: Do not reintroduce a client-side sign rate limit or a static split of `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` across replicas. The Token Service does not autoscale, so any split sized to the gateway HPA maximum starves it. `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` remains solely as the `KMSSignQuotaApproaching` threshold. — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.8.2 table row "| Token Service replicas | 2 | 2 | 4 |"; §17.8.2 Step 3 "`autoscaling.maxReplicas: 30`"; charts/lenny/presets/values-tier3.yaml `tokenService: replicas: 4`.
FACT: CHART-3 is a retired ID. CHART-4 and CHART-5 keep their numbers, and the range phrase "CHART-1 to CHART-5" in non-spec **Design** and the tests heading still stands with the gap. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md "Tests that land with CHART-1 to CHART-5".
FACT: CODE-3 items were renumbered to 1 to 3 (the Limit/Burst item was removed), and CODE-7 items to 1 to 2 (the flag item was removed). No proposal file cited CODE-3 or CODE-7 by item number before this edit (grep `CODE-(3|7).*item`).
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0097/proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: S26 ("CHART-3. Lands the sign-rate flags.") names a deleted deliverable. Remove S26. No other step lists S26 in Depends on.
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0097/proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: S20 says CODE-7 "Lands the outcome mapping, the sign-rate flags, and the sign metrics". It should say "Lands the outcome mapping and the sign metrics", because CODE-7 no longer adds flags.
DEFERRED [/home/ec2-user/lenny/.claude/worktrees/draft-0097/proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: S14 (CODE-3) lists tier 7a. Its only 7a test, the limiter concurrency test under "Tests that land with CODE-2 and CODE-3", was deleted, so S14's tiers should be 0 and 1.

### [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: lenny-ops applies the §13.3 revocation check (cache + token.revoked + fail-closed while Postgres is unreachable) to Lenny-issued tokens only, stated once in SPEC-11 Edit 2 by pointer to §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation" and "Authoritative durability for revocation" — BECAUSE §13.3 makes Postgres the sole revocation authority and the 10-year bootstrap admin credential is retired through revoked_at at SPEC-2 step 2; scoping to Lenny-issued tokens keeps IdP callers usable on the outage tool — ALTERNATIVES: refuse Lenny-issued tokens on lenny-ops entirely (rejected: §25.1 Scoped Tokens are minted by POST /v1/oauth/token and presented to lenny-ops); honour the last-known cache during a Postgres outage (rejected: contradicts §13.3 fail-closed); new introspection RPC to the gateway (rejected: new surface, existing authmw hooks cover it).
DECISION: §25.4 Startup and Readiness gets a POINT edit ("starts its HTTP listener immediately after it loads the verifier key set of §10.2 ...") rather than an exception sentence — BECAUSE §10.2 already states that a load failure stops the process.
FACT: lenny-ops already uses authmw.Options (cmd/lenny-ops/main.go buildAuthConfig), which carries Revocations and RevocationFreshness hooks; the gateway wires them from revocation.Cache + revocationprop.Propagator with a 30s Rehydrate loop (cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go, revocation.go). Cache.Stale() is true until the first successful Rehydrate and after defaultMaxStaleness=90s — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:36,157
WATCHOUT: authmw's RevocationFreshness gate fires for EVERY bearer before the revocation lookup (pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:487). Wired unscoped in lenny-ops it refuses non-Lenny-issued callers during a Postgres outage, contradicting SPEC-11 Edit 2's "applies to them".
WATCHOUT: lenny-ops in production verifies no upstream IdP token in code today (only the HMAC trust key; no JWKS/OIDC verifier in cmd/lenny-ops). With the revocation fail-closed, production lenny-ops serves nobody during a Postgres outage until IdP verification exists. SPEC-11 Edit 2's IdP clause is unimplemented — a separate gap, not this group's.
OPEN: issued_tokens has no StoreRouter routing rule for any reader (gateway included); §25.4 Storage Routing has no row for lenny-ops' new issued_tokens read. A later round assigns the routing and adds the row.
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S11 says "Lands the §18.33 and §25.4 authentication edits"; after this fix SPEC-11 also lands the §25.4 Startup and Readiness edit and the §25.15 Postgres-down and Postgres + Redis both down rows. Rewrite as a pointer: "Lands SPEC-11."

DECISION: delete the client-side per-replica KMS sign rate limit (SPEC-4 sentence, SPEC-8 Edit 2 last formula sentence, `rate_limited` outcome, CODE-3 item 2, CODE-7 item 1 flags, CHART-3, its Helm and tier-7a tests, the HPA edge case); the reason lives once in summary D15 — BECAUSE the provider's own throttle enforces the quota exactly and fleet-wide and already maps to the same retryable 503 without tripping the breaker, while any static split of Q refuses demand the quota could serve (gateway below its HPA max; Token Service fixed at 4 replicas at Tier 3, spec/17_deployment-topology.md §17.8.2 "Token Service replicas" row); deleting it also leaves nothing undefined under `kms.provider: local` — ALTERNATIVES: (a) "When kms.provider is not local" condition on the formula (closes finding 1 only); (b) per-component share value `kms.tokenServiceSign...` with ⌊(Q−S)/G⌋ and ⌊S/T⌋ (new value, validation, docs, Helm test; operator must size S; limiter still only approximates the provider throttle); (c) non-partitioning ⌊Q/G⌋ and ⌊Q/T⌋ (fleet can reach 2Q, so the limiter protects nothing).

### [owner-decisions-2026-10-23]
DECISION: Owner decision 4 is option B. `lenny-ops` keeps an in-memory revocation set for Lenny-issued tokens, loaded from `issued_tokens` at startup and kept current while Postgres is reachable by the gateway's §13.3 revocation-cache mechanisms. During a Postgres outage it keeps accepting key-set tokens not in the set. SPEC-11 Edit 2 is the one home and cites §13.3 instead of restating it. SPEC-11 Edit 3 adds that the set gates neither the listener nor readiness, which keeps §25.4 "Startup and Readiness" true. SPEC-11 Edit 4 replaces the "refuses Lenny-issued tokens for the duration" sentence in the §25.15 "Postgres down" and "Postgres + Redis both down" rows with acceptance against the pre-outage set plus the cold-start refusal, matching the existing "populated cache" caveat. SPEC-11 Edit 5 is retired, because the `/v1/admin/me` Degradation sentence holds unqualified. With Redis down and Postgres up, the `issued_tokens` reads keep the set current, which follows the §13.3 "Authoritative durability for revocation" fall-back and the gateway's periodic rehydration; with both down the set is frozen. — BECAUSE the owner chose B on 2026-10-23, and §13.3's fail-closed sentence would disable the §25.15 loop — ALTERNATIVES: option A (refuse all Lenny-issued tokens while Postgres is down) and option C (accept only `lenny-ops`-audience tokens and defer revocation), both rejected by the owner.
DECISION: CODE-6 item 6 implements the revocation set: rehydration with the gateway's query, the gateway's propagator, periodic rehydration, `authmw.Options.Revocations` scoped to key-set tokens, and a startup gate that refuses key-set tokens only until the first successful load. The gateway's `RevocationFreshness` gate is not wired. Tests: tier 1 in CODE-6's testing list, tier 4 (non-revoked accepted and revoked refused with Postgres down, cold start refused) in TEST-1 item 2, and tier 9 revocation enforcement in TEST-1 item 6. The tier 4 and tier 9 tests sit in TEST-1 because they need the TEST-1 item 1 harness, and S29 already depends on S18.
DECISION: CODE-6 item 3 now loads the key set in every `lenny-ops` process, with or without `--production`, because an unset `--kms-provider` resolves to `local` in `providerflags.Resolve`. This reconciles CODE-6 with SPEC-2's every-process load.
DECISION: Owner decision 5. `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` is the one default for playground bearers and for `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens requested without `expires_in`. SPEC-3 Edit 1 states the default and the bounds once. The §27.2 row and the §17.8.1 row name it as a default lifetime of refreshable tokens. CODE-8 item 3 replaces the hard-coded 1-hour `durationFromExpiresIn` default in `pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go` with `Options.CapabilityTokenTTL`, set from a new `lenny-token-service` `--capability-token-ttl-seconds` flag that the chart renders from the same value. CODE-8 item 4 leaves `RequestedExp` zero for an `actor_token` exchange with no `expires_in`, which `tokenexchange.Derive` already treats as absent, so delegated children never take the default. SPEC-8 Edit 2's formula now counts every refreshable-token holder with its own refresh lead. — BECAUSE owner decision 5 (2026-10-23) — ALTERNATIVES: a separate Token Service default value (two values for one lifetime, rejected with the same reasoning as keeping `playground.bearerTtlSeconds`); keeping the 1-hour code default for delegation children (a non-spec constant with no override).
DECISION: The gateway-to-pod renewal path for the session-creation `session_capability` token is recorded as a follow-up proposal in the summary's "Defects in the shipped tree that this proposal does not stage" and in the non-goals. D21 keeps its substance, because nothing in this proposal's own contract is deferred.
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, per-replica sign rate limit. The problem statement's scope line "There is also a per-replica sign rate limit" is not implemented (summary D15). Do not re-file it. — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", throttling bullet; summary.md D15
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, §12.2 rows. The problem statement asks for capacity guidance in §17.8 and §12.2 and for token-issuance rows in the Postgres write budget; capacity text lands in §17.8 only (summary D20). — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", capacity bullet; summary.md D20
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, key-identity surface. The problem statement asks for key-identity flags and Helm values; the reserved aliases in the existing `kms.aliases` map replace them (summary D5). — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", key-identity bullet; summary.md D5
WATCHOUT: Scope departure, verifier refresh rule. The problem statement asks for a refresh rule for the verifier key set; the startup-loaded static set replaces it, because pinned key versions are immutable (summary D8). — EVIDENCE: problem-statement.md "Scope", verifier key set bullet; summary.md D8
WATCHOUT: The checklist keeps S27 to S32 at their numbers after S26 was removed, so ledger entries that cite S27 still point at CHART-4.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-G1.1]: the DECISION that `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` governs only playground bearers, the WATCHOUT "Do not re-add the /v1/oauth/token renewal clause", and the WATCHOUT that the knob governs exactly the tokens `playground.bearerTtlSeconds` governed are superseded by owner decision 5. The default also governs `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens requested without `expires_in`. The session-creation token keeps its cap.
CORRECTS [spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]: the DECISION that `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` governs only playground bearers, and the WATCHOUT that the rename is cosmetic, are superseded by owner decision 5 in the same way.
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-G1.1]: the DECISION that SPEC-11 Edit 2 carries the §13.3 fail-closed refusal for Lenny-issued tokens, the DECISION that SPEC-11 Edit 4 appends "`lenny-ops` refuses Lenny-issued tokens for the duration", and the DEFERRED item to wire `RevocationFreshness` in `lenny-ops` are superseded by owner decision 4. The OPEN on the §25.15 closing paragraph is closed (Standing context, Settled).
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-design-G1.1]: the DECISION that `lenny-ops` applies the §13.3 check with the fail-closed refusal while Postgres is unreachable is superseded by owner decision 4.
CORRECTS [spec.4.follow-up-fix.1]: SPEC-11 Edit 5 and the summary index naming the `/v1/admin/me` degradation sentence are withdrawn under owner decision 4.
FACT: S7 depends on S1, because SPEC-7 Edit 5 cites the §10.2 "Signing topology" label that SPEC-1 creates (the S7 WATCHOUT in [spec.4.review-applicability.1]). The checklist row is corrected.

DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 2 now holds the `lenny-ops` revocation set as numbered **Revocation set** rules 1 to 6 (startup and 30-second reads that only add, `token.revoked` events, 401, the 503 gate before the first successful read, acceptance after it, and IdP exclusion). Open decisions OD1 (revoked-token code) and OD2 (production readiness during a Postgres outage) are recorded in the summary with default (a) each.

### [spec.5.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-4 Edit 1 (§10.2 "KMS signing failure mode") is the single home for the KMS_SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE causes, the Retry-After values ("remaining breaker cooldown when open, and 1 second otherwise"), and the outage impact; the §15 row (SPEC-4 Edit 2) is now a pointer with no cause list and no existing-sessions sentence — BECAUSE the §15 cause list was a copy that drifted from §10.2 and omitted the closed-breaker hard error — ALTERNATIVES: adding the hard-error cause to the §15 row (two copies again); narrowing §10.2 so a closed-breaker hard error stays 500 (a KMS 5xx is transient, and §10.2's lead sentence already makes every failure a 503); a typed BreakerSigner error carrying RetryAfter for hard errors (new mechanism; the HTTP-layer floor of 1 already covers it).
FACT: today a hard inner Sign error with the breaker closed reaches 500 server_error on the Token Service, because BreakerSigner.Sign returns the raw inner error and the mint path only maps errors.Is(err, jwt.ErrSigningUnavailable) to 503 — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:108-115; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:705-711. CODE-7 item 1 now maps every Sign error to 503 and the Tier 2 bullet asserts the closed-breaker case with Retry-After: 1.
DECISION: "Existing sessions are unaffected" is withdrawn from SPEC-4 Edit 1, the §15 row, the SPEC-5 Edit 3 alert row, and summary D8; §10.2 now says previously issued tokens keep verifying until exp and a running session's own mints (example: delegation child token) fail until signing recovers — BECAUSE delegation and refresh mints for running sessions call Sign — ALTERNATIVES: naming credential-lease renewal as the example (false: lease tokens are randomString handles, pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:121,142); listing every affected mint (restates other sections).
WATCHOUT: Do not name credential-lease issuance or renewal as a KMS mint anywhere; leases sign nothing. The spec's Token Service outage text (§4.3 "continue operating", §17.7 Token Service runbook, TokenServiceUnavailable alert) concerns Token Service availability and stays true — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:224; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:827; spec/16_observability.md:429.
FACT: problem-statement lines 47 and 104 describe what current §10.2/§15 say ("existing sessions are unaffected") as evidence the spec assumes fixed-lifetime tokens; they remain accurate descriptions of the current spec and were left unedited — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md:1135; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:244.


### [spec.5.fix-G3.1]
DECISION: SPEC-8 Edit 2 "KMS Sign rate" premise now reads "Every Lenny-issued JWT costs one KMS `Sign` call"; formula, 230/s and 690/s figures, and the quota sentence are unchanged — BECAUSE credential-lease tokens are opaque TokenStore handles and cost no Sign, so the finding's lease terms would count Sign calls that never happen — ALTERNATIVES: adding lease issue and renewal terms (oversizes the quota); "figures exclude leases" (implies leases sign); a defence sentence in SPEC-8 (restates §13.3 and §4.9).
FACT: Lease tokens sign nothing. §13.3 RFC 8693 table types lease tokens as `access_token`; §4.9 "LLM Reverse Proxy" step 3 validates the lease token against `TokenStore`; `credential.MintLease` builds the proxy-mode token with `randomString("lt", 32)`; direct mode carries no Lenny token. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:625; spec/04_system-components.md:1594; pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:140-145, 187-195
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]: the UNVERIFIED "formula has no credential-lease term" is answered; no lease term is owed. Standing context Open item 1 lost its lease clause; the API-client-holder recheck remains open.
CORRECTS [spec.4.review-performance.1]: same answer for its lease undercount concern.
WATCHOUT: Do not re-file "Sign-rate formula omits credential-lease mints". Leases are opaque handles; only JWT mints reach the KMS. — EVIDENCE: pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:140-145
OPEN: The spec/04 "LLM Reverse Proxy" threat-model clause saying KMS-key control lets an attacker "forge lease tokens entirely" contradicts opaque leases; predates 0097, separate finding.


### [spec.5.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: The Embedded Mode local KMS master key is a Secret that `lenny up` creates in the embedded cluster when it is absent and keeps on AlreadyExists. The chart mounts the Secret that the new `kms.masterKeySecret` value names, the dev preset sets that value to a fixed name, and the lookup-generated Secret renders only when the value is empty (helm install, Kind tier 5). SPEC-9 Edit 1 KMS row, CHART-2 Targets and item 1, the Helm unittest bullet, a new tier-2 envtest bullet, Files touched, and summary D12 and the CHART-2 index line were edited to match. BECAUSE embedded manifests are pre-rendered by `helm template --no-hooks`, `lookup` returns empty there, so a chart-generated key would be fixed per build and shared by every install, and it would also break the tier-11 byte-for-byte sync. This mirrors `security.oidc.bearerTrustKeySecret` and `createDevBearerSecret`. ALTERNATIVES: a pinned dev key in presets/dev.yaml (rejected: it is a live signing key); a host file at `Paths.KMSMasterKey()` (rejected: adds surviving state outside the substrate); requiring `kms.masterKeySecret` (rejected: `kms.provider` defaults to `local`); overwriting a chart-rendered Secret after apply (rejected: still nondeterministic, and a re-apply clobbers the key).
FACT: Embedded manifests come from `helm template --no-hooks` over the dev preset, and TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender fails on any byte difference. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.go:9-25; tests/tier11_docs/embedded_manifests_sync_test.go:56-77
FACT: `createDevBearerSecretFromConfig` does Create and then Update on AlreadyExists. The master key Secret must NOT copy that, because an update would rotate the live signing and KEK key. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/bringup.go:140-166; the call site is stack.go:316
WATCHOUT: Do not re-file "the §17.4 State and resets sentence omits the KMS key". The Secret is held in the embedded cluster's datastore, which is part of the persisted substrate, and `--purge` clears it. Neither that sentence nor SPEC-9 Edit 3 needs an edit. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/lifecycle.go:75-135 (per design; not re-read)

Its step text stays accurate.

### [spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: lenny-ops refuses every Lenny-issued token carrying `origin: "playground"` with 401 UNAUTHORIZED, appended as SPEC-11 Edit 2 **Revocation set** rule 7 (no renumbering, so Edit 4, Edge cases, DOCS-2 and the tests keep citing rules 4 and 5) — BECAUSE no flow carries a playground bearer to lenny-ops (playground CSP connect-src is the gateway only; §15 playground endpoints are gateway-only; exhaustive meaning search over spec/25, 27, 15 found none), so refusing is smaller than enforcing §27.3.1 — ALTERNATIVES: wiring authmw `PlaygroundRevocations` (Redis GET on `t:{tenant}:pg:revoked:{jti}`, fail-closed 503) on lenny-ops, rejected because it adds a Redis hot-path dependency and a fail-closed 503 on a surface no playground client reaches; folding an exclusion into the Authentication sentence, rejected because "validates ... other than" does not say the outcome.
WATCHOUT: an implementor can realise rule 7 with zero new authmw surface by wiring `authmw.Options.PlaygroundRevocations` on lenny-ops with a checker that always reports revoked; authmw then writes 401 UNAUTHORIZED reason bearer_revoked. That runs after tenant extraction, so before the first read a playground token may get the rule-4 503 instead; the tier-1 rule-7 case asserts after a read for that reason. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:525-538
DECISION: rule 5's "because no revocation can commit while Postgres is down" is deleted rather than replaced — BECAUSE the rule's own clause ("a failed read leaves the set unchanged and a token not in the set is accepted") already states the outcome, and D22 is the reason home and already records the read-fault residual — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's replacement clause, rejected as a second statement of D22's residual risk.
DECISION: §25.4 Dependencies gains a KMS row (Required "Yes, at startup", Used-by a pointer to §10.2 "Verifier key set and rotation"), the Postgres row's Used-by gets the Edit 4 pointer sentence to rules 4 and 5, and the §25.1 Component Model dependency list gains a KMS bullet, all as a new SPEC-11 Edit 5 — BECAUSE SPEC-2 makes KMS a startup dependency of lenny-ops and both dependency summaries would otherwise omit it.
WATCHOUT: the new SPEC-11 Edit 5 reuses the number of the retired `/v1/admin/me` Edit 5 named in Standing context; they are unrelated.

Nobody has traced whether any embedded-mode lenny-ops caller presents a Lenny-issued token (rather than a dev trust-hook token, rule 6). A later round should trace it per the Traps entry.


### [spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: every error from a signer's `Sign` is 503 `KMS_SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE`; Retry-After is the remaining cooldown when the breaker is open and 1 s otherwise (SPEC-4 Edit 1 is the one home). The §15 row (SPEC-4 Edit 2) drops its cause list and its "existing sessions" sentence and points at §10.2 — BECAUSE the row's cause list was a second copy of the §10.2 rule and it drifted (it left out hard errors that hit while the breaker is closed). Deleting the copy closes the drift for good, where adding a third cause would not — ALTERNATIVES: keeping hard errors closed-breaker as 500 `server_error` (today's code, tokenservice.go:705-711) was rejected, because §10.2's lead sentence "if a signer cannot obtain a signature" already covers them and a KMS 5xx or timeout is transient. Wrapping hard inner errors in a typed BreakerSigner error so each carries RetryAfter was rejected as new mechanism; the HTTP layer applies the 1 s default.
DECISION: "Existing sessions are unaffected" is withdrawn from §10.2, §15, and §16.5 — BECAUSE credential-lease issuance (exchange_type lease_issue), delegation, and refresh are all Token Service or gateway mints made on behalf of running sessions (spec/04 §4.9 renewal loop step 2 calls the AssignCredentials path; tokenservice.go classifyExchangeType). Only "previously issued tokens keep verifying until exp" stays true.
FACT: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go Sign passes a hard inner error through raw (no typed wrapper) after recordFailure. Only breaker refusal returns ErrSigningUnavailable. Callers that branch only on the typed errors therefore send hard errors to their generic 500 path — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:108-115, pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:705-711
WATCHOUT: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:2126 also carries the generated KMSSigningUnavailable description ("Existing sessions are unaffected until JWT expiry."). make generate regenerates it, but CODE-11's target sentence names only the chart and docs rules.yaml outputs. Run the tier-11 TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender after CODE-11 — EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:1-5


### [spec.5.fix-design-G3.1]
FACT: Credential-lease tokens cost no KMS `Sign`. The spec types them as opaque `access_token` handles (spec/13_security-model.md §13.3 RFC 8693 table: `requested_token_type` row and `subject_token_type` "access_token for opaque tokens"), and the LLM Proxy validates one by `TokenStore` lookup (spec/04_system-components.md "LLM Reverse Proxy" request flow step 3). In code `credential.MintLease` (pkg/credential/lease_mint.go) generates the proxy lease token with `randomString`, and the Token Service `AssignCredentials` gRPC path (pkg/tokenservice/grpc.go) holds no signer. A direct-mode lease carries no Lenny token at all. The only `signer.Sign` in pkg/tokenservice is `mintToken` (tokenservice.go ~:698), and `classifyExchangeType`'s `lease_issue` arm fires only for a session_capability exchange issued with no actor, which no lease path produces. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:624, :629; spec/04_system-components.md:1594; pkg/credential/lease_mint.go MintLease; pkg/tokenservice/grpc.go
DECISION: G3 closes by replacing "Every Lenny-issued token costs one KMS `Sign` call" with "Every Lenny-issued JWT costs one KMS `Sign` call" in SPEC-8 Edit 2. The formula, the Tier-3 figures (about 230/s and 690/s) and every other site stay as they are. — BECAUSE the formula already counts every JWT mint, and the word "token" is what led the reviewer to count opaque lease tokens. — ALTERNATIVES: adding lease issue and lease renewal terms and recomputing the figures would oversize the quota for mints that never happen; stating that the figures "exclude leases" implies leases sign; adding a sentence that lease tokens are opaque restates §13.3.
CORRECTS [spec.4.review-performance.1]: its FACT that each credential lease "costs one KMS Sign under SPEC-8's own premise" is wrong. Lease tokens are opaque and sign nothing (see the FACT above). The premise sentence it relied on is now scoped to JWTs.
CORRECTS [spec.4.fix-design-G2.1]: its UNVERIFIED about a missing credential-lease term is resolved. No lease term is owed.
WATCHOUT: A lease renewal still calls the Token Service (and Postgres, under §13.3 "Internal token issuance paths"), so any G2 text that depends on lease renewal reaching the Token Service stays valid. A G2 claim that lease renewal costs a KMS `Sign` would be wrong. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md "Proactive Lease Renewal" step 2; spec/13_security-model.md:656
OPEN: spec/04_system-components.md "LLM Reverse Proxy" threat-model paragraph, item (b), says an attacker who controls the KMS signing key "can forge lease tokens entirely". That contradicts opaque lease tokens validated by `TokenStore` lookup. The contradiction predates 0097 and is a separate finding.


### [spec.5.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: Embedded Mode local KMS master key = a Secret `lenny up` creates once (create-if-absent, never updated) in the persisted substrate before it applies the Deployments; the dev preset names it through a new `kms.masterKeySecret` chart value, and the chart's lookup-preserved Secret stays only as the fallback when that value is empty (Helm install, tier-5 Kind). Home of the mechanism: CHART-2 item 1 plus a new CHART-2 item for pkg/embedded/stack; spec home: SPEC-9 Edit 1 row. — BECAUSE the embedded manifests are a `helm template --no-hooks` render (pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.go header) where `lookup` returns nothing, so a chart-generated random key is baked into the binary and changes every `make generate`, breaking TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender; the tree already solves the same problem for the dev bearer key (createDevBearerSecretFromConfig in pkg/embedded/stack/bringup.go + `security.oidc.bearerTrustKeySecret` set in charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml). Keeping the key in the substrate keeps §17.4 "State and resets" true without an edit (`--purge` clears the substrate). — ALTERNATIVES: host file at Paths.KMSMasterKey() (~/.lenny/kms/master.key) mirrored into the Secret, rejected because no host-side process reads the KMS key and it would add a surviving-state item §17.4 does not list; chart-only with render failure when `kms.masterKeySecret` is empty, rejected because `kms.provider` defaults to `local` so every default render and the tier-5 Kind install would need the Secret pre-created; overwriting the baked Secret after apply, rejected because every re-apply would fight it.
WATCHOUT: createDevBearerSecretFromConfig updates the Secret in place on AlreadyExists (it rotates by design). The KMS master key Secret must NOT copy that branch: an update rotates the master key and orphans every signed token and wrapped DEK. The tier-2 envtest's discriminating assertion is that a second call leaves the key bytes unchanged. — EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/bringup.go createDevBearerSecretFromConfig
FACT: `lenny down --purge` removes the whole ~/.lenny state directory and the substrate (pkg/embedded/stack/lifecycle.go Purge/purgeRoot); Paths.KMSMasterKey() exists but has no caller. — EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/paths.go KMSMasterKey; lifecycle.go purgeRoot
FACT: charts/lenny/values.schema.json types `kms` as a bare object, and pkg/chart/values/values.go holds no kms keys, so a new `kms.masterKeySecret` needs no schema or values.go edit. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.schema.json properties.kms

OPEN: (separate finding, predates 0097) §17.4 "State and resets" names only the substrate and image store as surviving state, while the dev bearer key at ~/.lenny/oidc/signing.key also survives a non-`--purge` down.
### [spec.5.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: Embedded Mode local KMS master key comes from a Secret that `lenny up` creates in the embedded cluster when absent (random `kms.DEKSize` bytes generated in memory, Create and keep on AlreadyExists, created before the Deployments are applied), and the chart mounts it by name through a new `kms.masterKeySecret` value that the dev preset sets. The chart's lookup-preserved generated Secret stays only for an empty `kms.masterKeySecret` (helm install, Kind tier 5). BECAUSE `lookup` returns empty under `helm template --no-hooks`, so a chart-generated key is a build-time constant shared by every install and a random render breaks TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender; the name-value-plus-bring-up-create pattern already exists for the dev bearer-trust Secret. ALTERNATIVES: pin a fixed dev key in presets/dev.yaml like `etcdEncryption.aescbcKeys` (rejected: unlike the inert etcd ConfigMap this key signs live ES256 tokens and wraps DEKs, so a repo-published key would be accepted by every embedded gateway); persist the key at `Paths.KMSMasterKey()` on the host like the dev bearer key (rejected: adds surviving state outside the substrate that §17.4 "State and resets" does not list, and the CLI never needs the key); refuse to render `local` without a named Secret (rejected: `kms.provider` defaults to `local`, so every default render and helm unittest would fail).
FACT: Embedded manifests are `helm template --no-hooks` of charts/lenny under presets/dev.yaml and the tier-11 test compares bytes. A random value in a dev-profile render breaks it, which is why dev.yaml pins the etcd aescbc key. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.go:8-25; tests/tier11_docs/embedded_manifests_sync_test.go:88; charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml:217-232
FACT: The existing embedded Secret precedent: dev.yaml sets `security.oidc.bearerTrustKeySecret: lenny-embedded-dev-bearer-key`, the chart only mounts it, and `createDevBearerSecret` in pkg/embedded/stack/bringup.go creates it before the gateway Deployment is applied (stack.go "creating the dev bearer-trust secret"). EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml:158-166; pkg/embedded/stack/bringup.go:116-168
FACT: The embedded render carries the lenny-controller, lenny-gateway, and lenny-ops Deployments and no Token Service Deployment. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml (kind: Deployment names)
WATCHOUT: `kms.LoadOrCreateMasterKey` creates the file when it is absent. On a read-only Secret mount a missing key therefore fails rather than silently minting a per-process key. Do not make the mount `optional: true`. That would reintroduce per-process keys. EVIDENCE: pkg/kms/masterkey.go:85-91
WATCHOUT: `lenny down --purge` removes the substrate and the whole state directory, so a Secret held in the substrate is cleared by purge. SPEC-9 Edit 3 (KMS dropped from the in-memory list) and the §17.4 surviving-state sentence therefore stay true with no edit. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/stack/lifecycle.go:75-135; spec/17_deployment-topology.md "State and resets"

### [spec.5.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: playground bearers are revoked only in Redis (`t:{tenant_id}:pg:revoked:{jti}`), and §27.3.1 "Per-request revocation check" says every authenticated request carrying a playground-origin bearer MUST consult it and fail closed with 503 REDIS_UNAVAILABLE when Redis is down. FACT: on a hard KMS error while the breaker is closed, BreakerSigner returns the inner error, and the Token Service writes 500 server_error. Only ErrSigningUnavailable maps to 503 today. SPEC-4 Edit 1's first sentence covers this case as a 503 but its Retry-After rule gives no value for it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:108-115; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:698-712

### [spec.5.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed the SPEC-8 Edit 2 missing credential-lease term (closes the Standing-context Open on the 230/s and 690/s figures) — BECAUSE §13.3 routes credential-lease issuance through the Token Service exchange (audience `llm-proxy`, `lease_issue`), one lease per authorized provider per session at `AssignCredentials` plus renewal every leaseTTL − renewBeforeBuffer (default 3600 − 300 s), and SPEC-8's own premise is one Sign per Lenny-issued token. At Tier 3 with one proxy-mode provider per session that adds about 200/s sustained and 600/s in a storm, roughly doubling both figures — ALTERNATIVES: leaving it as an Open (rejected: an operator who sizes the quota to the stated storm figure gets 503 on lease issue during storms).
FACT: The shipped gateway revocation Cache.Rehydrate carries forward every existing entry (add-only, never pruned), so the lenny-ops set's "each successful read adds the rows" is no worse than the shipped gateway; do not file unbounded set growth as a 0097 regression. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:121-150
FACT: lenny-ops's 30 s full rule-1 read adds one or two readers to the 30 gateway replicas at Tier 3 that already rehydrate on the same 30 s cadence; incremental load is negligible. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:30-36
USEFUL [spec.4.review-performance.1]: its lease FACT (spec/04_system-components.md:683, :1286) saved re-deriving the lease issue path.

### [spec.5.review-reliability.1]
FACT: credential-lease tokens are Token Service mints (§4.3 item 3, `lease_issue` exchange type in pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:295), and §4.9 "Proactive Lease Renewal" item 2 issues a replacement lease with fresh expiresAt before renewBefore (expiresAt - 300 s, default leaseTTLSeconds 3600). Under SPEC-4 every Token Service mint needs a live KMS Sign, so a KMS outage longer than about one lease TTL fails renewal (3 retries, then CREDENTIAL_RENEWAL_FAILED fault rotation, which is also a mint) and running sessions lose LLM access. SPEC-4 Edit 1's unqualified "Existing sessions and previously issued tokens are unaffected" is false for them. — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md:1565, :1573; spec-changes.md:130
WATCHOUT: the gateway revocation cache never evicts (Rehydrate carries every previously held jti forward), and SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 1 ("each successful read adds the rows it returns") copies that for lenny-ops, so both sets grow for the life of the process. Not filed here: a capacity question for the performance lens, and the gateway behavior predates 0097. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:137-143
FACT: a retried rotation failing with subject_token_revoked is not a new reliability regression. §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation — no grace period" already forbids the old token from validating after the rotating COMMIT. Only the code (CODE-9 item 3) changes. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:662, :672
FACT: session creation already requires a Postgres INSERT (§10.1.3 item 2), so the SPEC-1 session_mint recording adds no new outage dependency. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:48
USEFUL [Standing context Traps, trace every caller]: walking lenny-ops callers (watchdog, lenny-ctl, GatewayClient SA token) confirmed that rules 4 and 5 match D22. Nothing to re-file.

### [owner-decisions-2026-10-24]
DECISION: OD1 is option (a), decided by the owner's delegate. `lenny-ops` reuses `authmw` unchanged and emits `401 TOKEN_REVOKED`, the gateway's existing revoked-token response. SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 3 now reads "refused with the response a gateway replica returns for a revoked token" with a §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation" link, so the staged rule and the staged tests (CODE-6 rule-3 case, TEST-1 items 2 and 6) agree. The summary's Defects bullet now records that §15 has no revoked-token catalog row and that §13.3 spells the code in lower case, for a follow-up to reconcile. Summary D23. — BECAUSE the §15 error catalog lists no revoked-token code in either spelling, every §15 code is upper snake case, and §13.3's `token_revoked` reads as an informal mention, so naming either spelling in rule 3 would restate a code §15 does not define — ALTERNATIVES: option (b), changing `authmw` to emit `token_revoked` (client-visible on every gateway route, widens CODE-6 into the gateway); naming `TOKEN_REVOKED` in rule 3 (no spec site names it). — EVIDENCE: spec/15_external-api-surface.md error code catalog (no `*_REVOKED` token row); spec/13_security-model.md §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation"; pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go (`writeError(..., "TOKEN_REVOKED", ...)`)
DECISION: OD2 is option (b), owner decision 6 (owner, 2026-10-24). SPEC-11 Edit 6 stages the §25.4 "Probes" change: the YAML `readinessProbe` path becomes `/healthz`, the permissive bullet says it is used by liveness, startup, and readiness and states the accepted single-replica cost, and the strict bullet says no probe uses `/healthz?strict=true` and that it is a diagnostic check. A spec sweep (lookup exact on `strict=true`, `/readyz`, and `readiness`, and meaning queries on `lenny-ops` readiness) found no other spec site saying `lenny-ops` readiness uses the strict gate; §25.4 "Startup and Readiness" already states the either-dependency rule and §25.15, §17, and §16 carry no `lenny-ops` probe text. CODE-13 makes the permissive `/healthz` handler implement the either-dependency rule (today it returns 200 unconditionally), moves `opsprocess.waitReady` to `/readyz`, renders `/healthz` as the production readiness probe in `ops-deployment.yaml` with a rewritten comment, and replaces the Helm case that pins `/healthz?strict=true`. TEST-1 item 2's Postgres-outage case runs `lenny-ops` against a Kubernetes API and asserts `/healthz` 200 through the outage and after a restart during it. Problem statement owner decision 6, summary D24, goals, non-goals, What changes, the deliverable index (SPEC-11, CODE-13), and checklist S11, S33, and S29 are updated. The Defects bullet on the strict probe is removed. — BECAUSE a Postgres outage fails every replica at once, so strict readiness empties the Service and makes the §25 degraded-mode design (owner decision 4, the Tier 2 and Tier 3 remediation locks, the Redis escalation fallback, and the §25.15 rows) unreachable in production — ALTERNATIVES: option (a), deferring the probe fix to a follow-up (rejected by the owner); switching the chart to `/healthz` without the handler change (the probe would never fail); a new readiness-only endpoint (adds spec surface where §25.4 already names `/healthz`). — EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md §25.4 "Probes" and "Startup and Readiness"; pkg/ops/opsserver/opsserver.go `handleHealthz`; charts/lenny/templates/ops-deployment.yaml `readinessProbe`; charts/lenny/tests/ops-deployment_test.yaml "uses the strict readiness gate when ops.production is true"; tests/testinfra/opsprocess/opsprocess.go `waitReady`; cmd/lenny-ops/deps_setup.go probe registration
WATCHOUT: Because liveness and startup share the permissive `/healthz`, CODE-13 makes a pod that reaches neither Postgres nor the Kubernetes API fail liveness, as §25.4 already states. `TestHealthzPermissiveIgnoresDependencyOutage` encodes the opposite and changes. Do not restore an unconditional permissive 200, and do not re-file the liveness change as a regression.
WATCHOUT: The redesign run that restructures the `lenny-ops` token-class acceptance and the KMS sign-rate formula did not run as part of these edits. Only the edits OD1 and OD2 require were made.
CORRECTS [redesign.1]: the DECISION that records OD1 and OD2 "with default (a) each", and its listing of "the production strict readiness probe" among the summary's Defects, are superseded. OD1 is settled as (a) with rule 3 reworded, and OD2 is settled as (b), so the strict-probe defect is staged rather than recorded.
CORRECTS [spec.5.review-client-surface.1]: the WATCHOUT that SPEC-11 rule 3 says `401 token_revoked` no longer holds. Rule 3 now refers to the gateway's revoked-token response and names no code.
FACT: `opsprocess.waitReady` treats any HTTP response, including a 503, as ready, so the CODE-13 move to `/readyz` is not needed for a harness process without Postgres to start; CODE-13 item 3 and the summary watch-out state the move's actual reason. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/opsprocess/opsprocess.go `waitReady`
FACT: the `ops-deployment.yaml` comments CODE-13 rewrites sit outside the `ops.production` branch, so they appear in the dev-preset render that `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml` embeds and that `TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender` checks byte for byte; CODE-13 now regenerates the file and S33 runs tier 11. — EVIDENCE: Makefile `EMBEDDED_MANIFEST_HEADER`; pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml lenny-ops Deployment probe comments

### [redesign.2]
DECISION: `lenny-ops` refuses only the playground bearer among Lenny-issued tokens (SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 7) and accepts a platform-admin impersonation bearer under rules 1 to 5. Rule 4 now names the gateway's Postgres-unreachable response by its §13.3 section and takes precedence over rules 3 and 7, and rule 7 names the §27.3.1 revoked-bearer response. CODE-6 item 6 sets `authmw.Options.PlaygroundRevocations` to an always-revoked checker. Summary D25 is new; D23, Defects, Non-goals, the CODE-6 tier-1 cases, and TEST-1 items 2 and 6 follow. Reasons: summary D25 and D23. — ALTERNATIVES: a new impersonation claim with a refusal on `lenny-ops`; a Redis-backed playground check on `lenny-ops`; per-request `issued_tokens` reads.
FACT: an impersonation bearer is minted as `typ: user_bearer` with no distinguishing claim, ending the session stamps a replica-local `MemStore` that no verifier reads, and the bearer has no `issued_tokens` row. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go:146-158, :209-221; cmd/lenny-gateway/adminrouter.go:424-428
FACT: `authmw` writes 503 `token_validation_unavailable` for a stale revocation set before the revoked-`jti` check (401 `TOKEN_REVOKED`), and keys the playground check on `claims.Origin`, answering a revoked bearer with 401 `UNAUTHORIZED` and `details.reason: bearer_revoked`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:484-498, :525-538; spec/27_web-playground.md:97
FACT: `token_validation_unavailable` appears in spec only in §13.3, and §15 has no row for it. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:660, :676
DECISION: SPEC-8 Edit 2 drops the Tier 3 worked figures (about 230/s sustained, 690/s in a storm). It keeps the formula, rewritten as a sum over the §10.2 "Signing topology" mint classes with a refresh term over every holder that obtains a new token before `exp`, and it keys the quota instruction to the peak sign rate (`burst_arrival_rate` plus the peak delegation rate). No credential-lease term is staged. Reasons: summary D16. — ALTERNATIVES: recomputing with an assumed API-client count (a figure resting on an invented parameter); adding a `capacityPlanning.*` holder value (new chart, schema, and docs surface for guidance text); adding lease issue and renewal terms (about one `Sign` per session per provider plus renewals, for calls that never happen).
FACT: the only 3× in §17.8.2 is the Postgres "Estimated burst write IOPS (3×)" row; §16.5 derives about 30/s claims and about 15/s `budget_reserve.lua` invocations from `capacityPlanning.*`; §27.3.1 "Caching" fixes the playground refresh lead at 60 s before expiry. — EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1180; spec/16_observability.md:596, :616; spec/27_web-playground.md:141
FACT: lease issue and renewal sign nothing. `GRPCServer.AssignCredentials` and `RotateCredentials` go through `credassign` to `credential.MintLease`, which builds the proxy token with `randomString("lt", 32)`. The only `signer.Sign` in pkg/tokenservice is `mintToken`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/grpc.go:70, :121; pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:142; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:698; spec/13_security-model.md:625; spec/04_system-components.md:1594
FACT: `classifyExchangeType` returns `lease_issue` for a no-actor, non-rotation exchange whose issued `typ` is `session_capability`, and no credential-lease path reaches it. The name is a misnomer that predates 0097 (separate finding). — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:306-317
WATCHOUT: no test owns the sign-rate formula, which is guidance. The quota and the alert are pinned by the CHART-1 unset-quota render failure, the CHART-5 `0.8 × quota` render, and the CODE-11 tier-11 check.
WATCHOUT: Replaced form, SPEC-8 Edit 2's worked Tier 3 sign-rate figures (200 sessions/s, about 15 delegations/s, 10,000 concurrent holders with a 60-second lead, about 230/s sustained and about 690/s at a 3× storm) and the "storm figure" quota instruction. They lost because the holder count and the storm multiplier have no spec source, and owner decision 5 adds API-client holders that no tier parameter counts.
WATCHOUT: Replaced form, the literal `503 token_validation_unavailable` in rule 4 and the bare `401 UNAUTHORIZED` in rule 7 of SPEC-11 Edit 2. They lost because §15 defines no `token_validation_unavailable` row and the rule-7 response omitted the §27.3.1 `details.reason`; each rule now names the gateway response by the section that states it (D23).
WATCHOUT: Replaced form, a D25 table of token classes with minting component and revocation store. It lost because it restated SPEC-1 "Signing topology", §13.3, and §27.3.1, listed credential-lease tokens as key-set tokens, and attributed delegation children to the Token Service.
CORRECTS [spec.5.review-performance.1]: its DECISION to add a credential-lease term is withdrawn.
CORRECTS [spec.5.review-reliability.1]: its FACT that a KMS outage fails credential-lease renewal is wrong. Renewal reaches the Token Service and Postgres and no KMS `Sign`.
CORRECTS [spec.5.review-fresh.1]: its UNVERIFIED is answered: credential-lease tokens are not JWTs.
CORRECTS [spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]: its BECAUSE wrongly lists credential-lease issuance among the mints. The withdrawal of "existing sessions unaffected" stands on delegation and refresh mints alone.

Each is a target the rules cite, and none is made wrong by this redesign, so no spec or docs edit site is added. A manual grep of spec/ and docs/ for the sign quota, the sign rate, `token_validation_unavailable`, and playground use of `lenny-ops` found no further site.


### [spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: The `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` default is scoped to JWTs ("every Lenny-issued JWT whose holder ...", "A JWT issued on `POST /v1/oauth/token` ...") in SPEC-3 Edit 1, the §27.2 row (SPEC-3 Edit 3), the §17.8.1 row (SPEC-8 Edit 1), summary D13, summary What-changes bullet and SPEC-3 index line, and the non-spec configuration.md docs row — BECAUSE a credential-lease token is an opaque handle (§13.3 `requested_token_type` row) whose expiry is the §4.9 lease TTL (`issuedAt + min(leaseTTLSeconds, providerMaxTTL)`, default 3600), and the unscoped predicate gave leases a second, 900 s lifetime — ALTERNATIVES: a "; a credential-lease token keeps the §4.9 lease TTL" exception clause (patches one class, leaves the predicate over-broad).
DECISION: SPEC-3 Edit 7 adds a request `expires_in` row (Lenny extension, optional, seconds) to the §13.3 "RFC 8693 parameter mapping to Lenny claims" table before "| Response `access_token` |", pointing to the §10.2 "JWT claims" `expiry` rule — BECAUSE §15 `/v1/oauth/token` defers to §13.3 and the table listed only Response `expires_in` (spec/13_security-model.md:632), while pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:255 already accepts `expires_in` on `Request` — ALTERNATIVES: restating the default/caps in the row (second home of owner decision 5), defining the field inline in Edit 1 (endpoint table still silent), staging it under SPEC-7 (SPEC-7 owns signing order).
WATCHOUT: The new §13.3 row is a pointer. Do not write `requested_exp = now + expires_in`, the absent-value default, or the caps into it; SPEC-3 Edit 1 is the one home of owner decision 5. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 7
FACT: In code the Token Service's `lease_issue` exchange-type label classifies a `/v1/oauth/token` exchange that issues a `session_capability` JWT with no actor (pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go `classifyExchangeType`); the credential-lease TTL itself comes from pkg/credential/lease_mint.go `ResolveLeaseTTL`, never from `durationFromExpiresIn`. A reviewer reading "lease_issue" as a lease token taking the JWT default is misreading the metric label. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:295-317, :574, :1131; pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:46
DEFERRED [proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.implementation-checklist.md]: S3 says "Lands the §10.2 and §27 lifetime edits, including the `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` default for playground bearers and `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens." True now: SPEC-3 also lands the §13.3 request `expires_in` row (Edit 7), so S3 should name §10.2, §13.3, and §27, and "tokens" should read "JWTs".
DEFERRED [proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.review-log.md]: Standing context "Owner decision 5" and the D13-scope trap say "`POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens requested without `expires_in`". DEFERRED [proposals/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on/0097_fix_a-bearer-token-that-lenny-issues-verifies-only-on.non-spec-changes.md]: docs/reference/configuration.md:271-272 documents `tokenExchange.defaultTokenTTLSeconds` (default 3600, "Default lifetime for newly minted access tokens") and `tokenExchange.maxTokenTTLSeconds`, which no chart, code, or spec value backs; after SPEC-3 Edit 1 the default for `/v1/oauth/token` JWTs is `auth.capabilityTokenTtlSeconds` (900). The configuration.md DOCS bullet should remove or replace those rows. Found by the related-passages lookup; predates this round's edit, so the non-spec loop owns it.

### [spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The request `expires_in` gets a new SPEC-3 Edit 7 row in the §13.3 "RFC 8693 parameter mapping to Lenny claims" table. The row gives only the name, the unit, and that the parameter is optional, and points to the §10.2 "JWT claims" `expiry` rule for its semantics. SPEC-3 Edit 1 stays the one home of the default and the explicit-value rule (owner decision 5). BECAUSE the wire field's home is the endpoint parameter table (spec/15 `/v1/oauth/token` row defers to §13.3), while the lifetime rule already has a home. ALTERNATIVES: a row that restates `requested_exp = now + expires_in` and the caps (the reviewer's suggestion) states the rule twice; defining the field inside Edit 1 puts a wire field in the claims list, where a client reading the endpoint table never finds it; moving the explicit-value clause from Edit 1 into the row would split owner decision 5 across two homes.
DECISION: The SPEC-3 default is scoped to JWTs by replacing "token" with "JWT" in Edit 1 and in its restatements: the §27.2 row, the §17.8.1 row, summary D13, summary lines 9 and 141, and the non-spec configuration.md docs row. BECAUSE a credential-lease token is an opaque handle (§13.3 `requested_token_type`) whose lifetime is the §4.9 lease TTL. ALTERNATIVES: appending a lease exception clause to "Tokens whose holder has no refresh path" is an exception clause, which is hair.
FACT: Code computes credential-lease lifetime in `pkg/credential/lease_mint.go` `ResolveLeaseTTL` (default 3600, provider cap). It does not go through `pkg/tokenservice` `durationFromExpiresIn`, so CODE-8 item 3 does not touch lease TTL. EVIDENCE: pkg/credential/lease_mint.go:46-53,119; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:574,1131
DEFERRED [implementation-checklist.md]: S3 says SPEC-3 "Lands the §10.2 and §27 lifetime edits ... default for playground bearers and `POST /v1/oauth/token` tokens". After G1, SPEC-3 also lands the §13.3 request `expires_in` row (Edit 7), and the default covers `POST /v1/oauth/token` JWTs, which excludes credential-lease tokens.
OPEN: (non-spec loop) docs/client-guide/authentication.md does not document the request `expires_in` parameter or its default. Whether a client-guide mirror is owed was not decided here.


### [spec.6.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: SPEC-9 Edit 2 replaces the §17.4 guard-rail intro "(TLS, JWT signing)" with "(TLS, dev bearer trust)" and item 3's list with "including TLS bypass, the gateway's dev bearer-trust hook (`--bearer-trust-hmac-key-file`), and any future relaxations."; the sentence "JWT signing is not relaxed in dev mode (§10.2)." is deleted — BECAUSE the gateway hook is a dev-mode trust widening gated on --dev-mode (cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:143-145; flags.go binds --dev-mode to LENNY_DEV_MODE) and named by the unchanged §17.4 OIDC row and banner (spec/17_deployment-topology.md:173, :195), and SPEC-1 Edit 1's **Development** bullet already states "no HMAC signer exists for Lenny-issued tokens" — ALTERNATIVES: leaving "(TLS)" alone (contradicts :173/:195); naming the hook without "the gateway's" (reads as covering the lenny-ops hook, which no LENNY_DEV_MODE gates); adding --dev-mode to lenny-ops (scope growth, see OPEN).
FACT: lenny-ops has no --dev-mode/LENNY_DEV_MODE; outside --production it admits the bearer-trust hook and dev tenant/role headers (cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:171-195). Item 3's generic "all security relaxations" was already false for lenny-ops before 0097. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:173-195
OPEN: (separate finding, predates 0097) §17.4 "Dev mode guard rails" item 3 says LENNY_DEV_MODE gates every dev relaxation, but lenny-ops gates its relaxations (bearer-trust hook, AllowDevHeaders, AllowDevRoles) on --production alone. CODE-6 item 4 and summary D12 follow the tree, not item 3. A later round decides whether lenny-ops gains a dev-mode gate or item 3 is scoped to the gateway.
WATCHOUT: do not move CODE-6 item 4 or D12 to a LENNY_DEV_MODE gate as part of this fix; that is a new lenny-ops flag and a chart change nobody has reviewed. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-6 item 4

### [spec.6.review-mechanism.1]
DECISION: filed one finding: SPEC-3 Edit 1's default-lifetime predicate ("every Lenny-issued token whose holder obtains a new token before exp by requesting one", and "a token issued on POST /v1/oauth/token from a request that carries no expires_in") covers credential-lease tokens, which §13.3 and §15 route through /v1/oauth/token and whose holder renews them before expiresAt (§4.9 Proactive Lease Renewal) — BECAUSE §4.9 fixes lease expiresAt = issuedAt + min(leaseTTLSeconds, providerMaxTTL), default 3600 s, so the applied spec gives a lease two lifetimes; D13 and owner decision 5 list only playground bearers, rotation and scope-narrowing user bearers, and operability-scope tokens — ALTERNATIVES: re-filing a lease Sign term in SPEC-8 (barred by Standing context Traps); filing §10.2's `service_token` example "credential lease bearers in direct mode" as contradicting SPEC-8's "opaque handle" (not filed: unclear whether the direct-mode bearer is the llm-proxy lease token).
FACT: Today the Token Service defaults an absent expires_in to one hour (`durationFromExpiresIn`), so the 15-minute default for operability-scope tokens shortens how long an agent holding a Lenny-issued operability token keeps `lenny-ops` access through a Postgres outage (token issuance is unavailable then, §13.3 "Write-before-issue ordering" follow-on paragraph). This is a consequence of owner decision 5, not a finding; IdP-token agents (SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 6) are unaffected. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:1131-1135; spec/13_security-model.md:654
FACT: Every staged-edit anchor I checked resolves: §27 bearerTtlSeconds sites (5, all staged), §17.8.2 burst_arrival_rate formula above the 17.8.3 heading, §16.5 "Session creation rate (sustained)" and "Workload profile assumptions", §25.4 lenny-ops-egress webhook comment line, §15 DELEGATION_PARENT_REVOKED row, and §13.3 401 token_revoked / 503 token_validation_unavailable responses that SPEC-11 rules 3 and 4 cite. — EVIDENCE: spec/27_web-playground.md:40,96,127,135; spec/10_gateway-internals.md:259; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:998-1003,1295; spec/25_agent-operability.md:1259; spec/13_security-model.md:672,676
USEFUL [Standing context Traps]: "trace every caller" pointed me at Path C/D of the §25.15 Total-Outage runbook; Path D uses a `kubectl create token` SA token (not Lenny-issued), so rule 4 does not lock the emergency path out.


### [spec.6.review-performance.1]
FACT: `revocation.Cache.Rehydrate` carries every existing jti forward into the fresh map, so the cache never shrinks; it holds jti only (no exp), so nothing can age entries out — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:121-150
FACT: Under owner decision 5, a holder that refreshes through the §13.3 self-rotation grant revokes its predecessor every ~15 min (§13.3 "Token rotation and revocation — no grace period"), so the revoked-jti arrival rate is about holders/(900 - lead) per second; an additive-only set grows without bound — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md "Token rotation and revocation"; proposal spec-changes.md:303
FACT: SPEC-7 Edit 1's sign-before-transaction sentence names only the Token Service; SPEC-1 binds the gateway session-creation mint to statements (1)-(3) only, so the highest-rate mint path (200/s at Tier 3, 2,000/s at Tier 4) has no rule keeping the remote sign outside the per-tenant audit lock (§11.7 50 ms P99 lock SLO) — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md:37, :181; spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:382; spec/16_observability.md:596
WATCHOUT: D20 (no §12.3 issued_tokens write row) is defensible as pre-existing because current §4.3 item 3 already has the Token Service issuing session tokens through write-before-issue; do not file the session-mint Postgres write load as new — EVIDENCE: spec/04_system-components.md §4.3 item 3; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:62-69


### [spec.6.review-plan-conformance.1]
FACT: The only plan step 0097 claims is the queued direct fix "F-10.2.22" under "Queued direct fixes filed by 0095's implementation" in §10.2 of the remediation plan. It requires one shared key source plus its rotation set on every gateway and Token Service replica, and routes through the pipeline when the spec is silent. The P1-P5 drafting rules and the test instruction cover only the terminal-transition block and do not bind this step. EVIDENCE: gateway-runtime-comms-remediation.md:3136-3142, :2731-2737
FACT: The plan's "shared sealed key" mechanism is a recorded divergence (owner decision 1; summary rejected-alternatives "Persisting the sealed HS256 key"). Shared key source and rotation set are staged in SPEC-1 "Signing topology" and SPEC-2 "Verifier key set and rotation". The plan-conformance lens has no gap here, and later rounds need not re-run it. EVIDENCE: 0097 summary.md:71; spec-changes.md:37, :77-79


### [spec.6.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no reliability findings in round 6 — BECAUSE the redesign.2 and owner-decision-6 edits (rule 4 precedence, rule 7 playground refusal, permissive readiness, listener-after-key-load) add no retried or replayed operation without a guard, no unbounded wait, and no recovery path that acts on unreconciled state; rule 4 cold-start refusal and the Ready-but-refusing replica are accepted costs under D22 and owner decision 6 — ALTERNATIVES: considered Retry-After: 1 with no jitter on throttled KMS_SIGNING_UNAVAILABLE as a stampede; rejected because every §15 TRANSIENT 503 uses the same Retry-After convention and §15 states no jitter rule anywhere (lookup meaning query over all 834 passages of §15 found none).
FACT: the lenny-ops startup probe (failureThreshold 30 × 2 s) bounds a hung KMS GetPublicKey at startup, so SPEC-11 Edit 3's listener-after-key-load cannot stall a pod indefinitely; it crashloops, which edge case 1 accepts — EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md:1040-1045
WATCHOUT: under owner decision 5 an operability agent's Lenny-issued token defaults to 15 min and cannot be re-minted during a Postgres outage (§13.3 "token issuance is unavailable by design"), so the §25.15 loop survives a long Postgres outage on Lenny-issued tokens only for the token's remaining lifetime (≤1 h cap with explicit expires_in), and on IdP tokens otherwise. This bound predates 0097 in kind (1 h cap) and follows from a settled owner decision; do not file it as a §25.15 contradiction — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:656, spec/25_agent-operability.md:5007
USEFUL [spec.4.review-reliability.1]: its READ COMMITTED serialization fact for concurrent rotation saved re-deriving the step (1a) visibility argument.
USEFUL [spec.5.review-reliability.1]: its WATCHOUT on the never-evicting revocation set kept me from re-filing rule 1 growth (performance lens, predates 0097).


### [spec.6.review-security.1]
FACT: The gateway's `--bearer-trust-hmac-key-file` hook is fatal without `--dev-mode` (the LENNY_DEV_MODE gate), and it stays under D12. It is the dev-mode relaxation of bearer trust that remains after 0097, so SPEC-9 Edit 2's §17.4 guard-rails wording ("relaxes security defaults (TLS)", item 3 without the JWT entry) drops it from the enumerated relaxations. Filed this round. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:143-145; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:173,195,279,283
FACT: Pods hold session_capability tokens ("Pods present these back to the gateway"), and lenny-ops now accepts them (rule 7 refuses only playground). Nothing in the spec restricts a session_capability token on the gateway admin API either, so this matches the gateway and is not a regression. Not filed. — EVIDENCE: spec/10_gateway-internals.md:223; spec/13_security-model.md:607-612
FACT: The Token Service applies no per-dialect cap when an exchange carries no audience (resolveDialectCap returns 0), so a token with no audience is capped only by its subject's exp, which can be the 10-year bootstrap admin. The spec defaults audience to lenny-gateway, which makes this a code gap rather than a spec gap. It matters for SPEC-2 step 3's claim that the cap bounds every old-key token. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:581-616,1131-1134; spec/13_security-model.md:629
WATCHOUT: An alert for a lenny-ops replica whose revocation reads fail while Postgres is up partly exists through OpsSelfHealthDegraded (spec/16_observability.md:573). Check that before filing a missing degraded-state alert. Not filed.
OPEN: Under owner decision 5, an operability agent's lenny-ops-audience token requested without expires_in lives 15 min. During a Postgres outage no token can be minted (write-before-issue), so a §25.15 loop running on such a token ends within 15 min unless the agent requested up to 1 h. A design or §25 lens should confirm that the §25.15 closing-paragraph claim in Standing context holds under that default.

### [spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-7 Edit 2 (§13.3 "`exp` granularity and parent-expiry handling") is the single home of the exchange `exp` formula, widened to every token minted on an exchange: `min(requested_exp, subject_token.exp, per-dialect cap)`, plus `actor_token.exp` when an `actor_token` is present. SPEC-3 Edit 1 points at that formula and no longer says "the §13.3 caps" or "child formula" — BECAUSE `tokenexchange.Derive` clamps every exchange to the subject and gates only the actor term, and the current §13.3 sentence scopes the formula to actor_token children, which left rotation and scope-narrowing exchanges bounded only by the per-dialect cap in the spec — ALTERNATIVES: writing the min formula into SPEC-3 Edit 1 as well (a second statement of the one rule); a separate §13.3 sentence for non-actor exchanges (splits one formula into two rules).
FACT: `Derive` applies the subject_token.exp term unconditionally and the actor term only when `r.Actor != nil`; `buildExchangeRequest` sets `RequestedExp` on every exchange — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go:247-259, pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:574
WATCHOUT: Do not drop or reword the SPEC-3 Edit 1 refresh-path class ("whose holder obtains a new token before `exp` by requesting one"). Rotation cannot extend past the subject's exp, but holders refresh by a new exchange from an upstream subject (IdP token or playground cookie), so the class stays true, and it is owner decision 5 — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 1
WATCHOUT: Keep the SPEC-3 Edit 1 sentence "For an `a2a_delegation` child token minted through `actor_token`, an absent `expires_in` leaves `requested_exp` out of that formula." It is the only statement that children take no default `requested_exp`; CODE-8 item 4 and its tier-1 test depend on it.
OPEN: (separate finding, predates 0097) §8.2 "Child-token minting" states the child `exp` cap as `min(parent.exp, now + leaseTTL)`, which differs from the §13.3 formula (no per-dialect cap, no subject term named) — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59


### [spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the subject_token.exp clamp has one home, SPEC-7 Edit 2 (§13.3 "`exp` granularity and parent-expiry handling"), generalized from "a child token minted via `actor_token`" to every token minted on an exchange, with the actor term applying when an `actor_token` is present. SPEC-3 Edit 1 points at that formula rather than saying "the §13.3 caps". BECAUSE the Token Service already clamps every exchange to subject_token.exp, and the actor term is the only part gated on an actor. ALTERNATIVES: writing the min formula into SPEC-3 Edit 1 was rejected because it would state the rule a second time next to §13.3.
DECISION: the SPEC-3 Edit 1 refresh-path class ("whose holder obtains a new token before `exp` by requesting one") stays unchanged. BECAUSE it stays true: the holder gets a fresh token through a new exchange from its upstream subject (an IdP token or the playground cookie), not by rotating the Lenny token past its exp. The class is also owner decision 5, which is settled. Problem statement :61, summary D13's class sentence and :32, the §27.2 row, the §17.8.1 row, and the SPEC-8 Σ term are not sites.
FACT: pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go `Derive` (the "§13.3 exp = min(...)" block) clamps to r.Subject.Exp unconditionally and gates only the actor term on r.Actor != nil. pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go `buildExchangeRequest` sets RequestedExp from `durationFromExpiresIn` on every exchange, and that function returns 1 h when expires_in is absent. Current spec/13 §13.3 states the min formula only for actor_token children. EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go:247-259, pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:574,1131-1135, spec/13_security-model.md:658

### [spec.8.review-mechanism.1]
FACT: `tokenexchange.Derive` clamps every exchange, actor or not, to `subject_token.exp` (`exp = RequestedExp`, replaced by `Subject.Exp` when that is earlier or RequestedExp is zero), then to the per-dialect cap. §13.3 states the subject clamp only inside the actor_token child formula. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go:247-259; spec/13_security-model.md:658
WATCHOUT: SPEC-3 Edit 1 says an oauth/token JWT gets `requested_exp` and then "the §13.3 caps", or "the lifetime it asks for within those caps". The CODE-8 tier-1 test asserts the subject-exp clamp, which no spec text states for rotation or scope narrowing. Because of that clamp, the self-rotation grant can never extend a token past its subject's exp. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-3 Edit 1; non-spec-changes.md:248
FACT: The gateway gates `--bearer-trust-hmac-key-file` on `--dev-mode` (LENNY_DEV_MODE), so the SPEC-9 Edit 2 guard-rail wording matches the code. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/token_signing.go:134-145
FACT: The §13.2.4 Token Service row already carries KMS egress (unconditional, `kms.endpointCIDR`), so SPEC-10 correctly leaves it out. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:276


### [spec.8.review-performance.1]
FACT: No §17.8 or §12 sizing figure is derived from the Lenny token lifetime or the token-exchange rate. The only Token Service capacity figure is a replica count (Tier 3: 4), so the 900 s default falsifies no stated budget. EVIDENCE: spec/17_deployment-topology.md:1240; spec/04_system-components.md:220
FACT: SPEC-8 Edit 2's delegation_rate term resolves. §16.5 "Workload profile assumptions" derives a sustained delegation rate of concurrent × 0.05 × 10 / 333 (≈15/s at Tier 3), and burst_arrival_rate sits in §17.8.2 above the insert point. EVIDENCE: spec/16_observability.md:609-616; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:999-1018
WATCHOUT: Revoked issued_tokens rows grow under the 900 s default. Each rotation-grant refresh revokes the prior row, and rows live until exp + 1h, so a holder carries about 75/14 ≈ 5.4 revoked rows against about 2 at a 1 h lifetime. SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 1 rereads that full set every 30 s on each lenny-ops replica through the partial (revoked_at) index. No spec figure fixes the holder count, so this was not filed. Do not file it without a holder-count source. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:662,676; spec/12_storage-architecture.md:17
USEFUL [review-log FACT line 432 and line 670]: the session_mint audit row adds one row per session to a per-tenant locked audit path that is already synchronous, and it adds no new Postgres outage dependency. This settled the session-creation write-amplification question without new work.
