### [spec.10.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-7 Edit 2's exchange `exp` formula is scoped to JWTs ("A JWT minted on an exchange receives ...", "bounds the minted JWT") — BECAUSE §13.3 routes credential-lease issuance through the same exchange and names `llm-proxy` as the lease audience, so an unscoped subject capped every lease at 1 h and clamped it to subject.exp, contradicting §4.9 `issuedAt + min(leaseTTLSeconds, providerMaxTTL)` (aws_bedrock ceiling 43200 s); SPEC-3 Edit 1 is already JWT-scoped and the code applies PerDialectCap to exchange JWTs only — ALTERNATIVES: revert to "A child token minted via `actor_token`" (drops the subject_token.exp clamp from rotation and scope-narrowing JWTs, re-opening round 8); an exemption clause for leases (restates §4.9 in §13.3).
WATCHOUT: Do not widen the SPEC-7 Edit 2 formula subject back to "a token" or "every token minted on an exchange", and do not add a lease-lifetime sentence there; a credential-lease token is an opaque handle whose expiry is the §4.9 lease TTL (ledger [redesign.2]). — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:615-629,655; spec/04_system-components.md:1274; pkg/credential/lease_mint.go MintLease (ResolveLeaseTTL); cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go buildServer PerDialectCap
MISTAKE: The round-9 rewrite of SPEC-7 Edit 2 widened the formula's subject to every exchange-minted token, reintroducing the lease conflict an earlier round had closed in SPEC-3 Edit 1. Cost: one round-10 finding.
USEFUL [redesign.2]: its DECISION that a credential-lease token is an opaque handle with the §4.9 TTL settled this finding's scope without re-derivation.
FACT: The `llm-proxy` 1 h entry in PerDialectCap stays in the SPEC-7 Edit 2 cap list as a true statement of the code; whether any JWT carries that audience (dead map entry) is unexamined. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go buildServer


### [spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-7 Edit 2 (§13.3 "`exp` granularity and parent-expiry handling") stays the single home of the exchange `exp` formula, and its subject becomes "A JWT minted on an exchange" (and "bounds the minted JWT") — BECAUSE §13.3 routes credential-lease issuance through the same exchange, and a lease is an opaque `access_token` whose expiry is the §4.9 lease TTL (`issuedAt + min(leaseTTLSeconds, providerMaxTTL)`, aws_bedrock up to 43200 s) with no subject clamp. The JWT scope matches SPEC-3 Edit 1 and the code (dialect cap applied to exchange JWTs in pkg/tokenservice; leases via credential.MintLease) — ALTERNATIVES: reverting to "A child token minted via `actor_token`" undoes the round-8 single-home fix, so SPEC-3 Edit 1's pointer for non-actor `/v1/oauth/token` JWTs would have no subject_token.exp clamp. Adding a lease exception clause is hair.
WATCHOUT: the `llm-proxy` 1 hour entry in the SPEC-7 Edit 2 cap list stays. It mirrors the PerDialectCap map in cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go:42-46, and removing it is a separate change to the code, not something this narrowing requires — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go:45
MISTAKE: round 9 widened the formula's subject from actor children to "a token minted on an exchange" without checking that §13.3 also puts lease issuance on that exchange. That re-opened the JWT-only scoping that [redesign.2] had already settled for SPEC-3 Edit 1.

### [spec.10.review-mechanism.1]

FACT: `tokenexchange.Derive` applies `min(requested, subject.exp, actor.exp, perDialectCap)` to every JWT exchange, with or without an actor, and treats a zero RequestedExp as absent. So the generalized formula matches the code for JWTs. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go:247-260

No spec budget is exceeded at any documented tier. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:662, :676

EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:662.

### [spec.12.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: KMS grant rule widened in its one home, SPEC-1 "Signing topology": the gateway and Token Service ServiceAccounts hold `Sign` and `GetPublicKey` on every key that `platform:jwt-signing` or `platform:jwt-verify` names, and lenny-ops holds `GetPublicKey` only. SPEC-2 rotation step 1 now reads "Create the new key, grant it the permissions "Signing topology" states, and map `platform:jwt-verify` to it." — BECAUSE a grant on "the signing key" left the step-1 key unreadable, so every rolled pod failed the SPEC-2 startup load and crash-looped, and after the step-2 swap the signers lacked `Sign` on the new key — ALTERNATIVES: `Sign` on the signing key only plus `GetPublicKey` on both (needs a second grant step before step 2 and contains nothing, because the verify key is always the outgoing or the incoming signing key); widening `GetPublicKey` alone (no `Sign` after the swap); leaving step 1 to imply the order (map-then-grant crash-loops step 1); leaving it to the DOCS-1 runbook only, as [spec.8.review-security.1] suggested (the two staged spec sections contradicted each other).
WATCHOUT: Do not copy the grant list into rotation step 1 and do not add a separate Sign-grant step between steps 1 and 2; step 1 points at "Signing topology", and the uniform grant on both keys makes a second step unnecessary. — EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1 Edit 1 "Signing topology" last sentence; SPEC-2 rotation step 1
FACT: DOCS-1's runbook sentence "List the IAM grants on every key either alias names, granted on the new key before the first rollout maps it: ..." mirrors the rule, because a docs page cannot cite the spec. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md DOCS-1 paragraph beginning "Remove every `/.well-known/jwks.json` step"
CORRECTS [spec.8.review-security.1]: its UNVERIFIED item on the "on the signing key" grant was a spec contradiction rather than operator provisioning only; it is closed in SPEC-1 and SPEC-2 by this entry.


### [spec.12.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-1 "Signing topology" scopes the KMS permissions to every key that `platform:jwt-signing` or `platform:jwt-verify` names (one uniform grant, Sign included on the verify-only key), and SPEC-2 rotation step 1 grants the new key those permissions before mapping it — BECAUSE step 1 maps the new key and every rolled pod must GetPublicKey it, and step 2 makes it the signing key with no further grant step — ALTERNATIVES: Sign on the signing alias only plus GetPublicKey on both (needs a second grant inserted before step 2, so two ordering clauses for no practical containment, since the verify key is either the outgoing or incoming signing key); widening only GetPublicKey (gateway and Token Service lose Sign after step 2).
WATCHOUT: Signing topology is the one home of the grant rule; step 1 and the DOCS-1 runbook point at it or mirror it, and must not restate a different key scope. EVIDENCE: spec-changes.md SPEC-1 "Signing topology" last sentence; SPEC-2 step 1.
USEFUL [spec.8.review-security.1]: its UNVERIFIED entry named this exact gap; it is now filed and closed by this design.

### [spec.12.review-security.1]
DECISION: returned no findings for the security lens on the round-12 spec staging. BECAUSE the only change since r10 is SPEC-7 Edit 2's "token" to "JWT" scoping, which keeps the subject/actor exp clamp on every exchange JWT, and the revocation bounds hold. lenny-ops reloads its set from Postgres (the authoritative store) and fails closed on a cold start (rule 4), and step (1a) reads `issued_tokens` directly. I rejected that because summary.md:90 records it as a rejected alternative, and the local provider already holds the KEKs under the same gate.

A misconfigured lenny-ops role therefore fails closed under SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 4 and does not silently empty the set. ### [spec.14.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed one finding. The sign-outside-the-lock rule in SPEC-7 Edit 1 names only the Token Service, and SPEC-1 "Signing topology" binds the gateway session-creation mint to statements (1), (2), and (3) only, so nothing places the gateway's remote sign before statement (1). D14 rejects in-lock signing for this reason but states it only for the Token Service — ALTERNATIVES: rewording SPEC-7 Edit 1 to "the minting component" was rejected, because that paragraph describes the Token Service exchange.

### [spec.14.review-reliability.1]
DECISION: no findings under the reliability lens for round 14 — BECAUSE every recovery path the staged edits add or change holds under crash, restart, and failover: sign-before-transaction discards the signed token on any rollback or (1a) rejection (SPEC-7 Edit 1); concurrent rotations serialize on the per-tenant advisory lock and the rotation's revoked_at UPDATE sits inside that transaction (spec/13_security-model.md:662), so the second (1a) re-read sees it; session creation is atomic and rolls back the pod claim on a mint or issued_tokens failure (spec/29_communication-scenarios.md:200-206); the lenny-ops revocation set's 30 s Postgres read bounds a missed Redis event; KMS startup failure is a bounded kubelet crashloop — ALTERNATIVES: filing the uniform `Retry-After: 1` on throttling as a stampede (rejected: provider throttling rejects per request, no cascading store, design preference); filing a missing §25.15 "KMS down" row (rejected: an absent row makes no existing row false).

### [spec.16.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 1 now says each successful read replaces the set with the rows it returns and the `jti` of each rule-2 event added after the read began; CODE-6 item 6 changes the shared `revocation.Cache.Rehydrate` swap to match, for the gateway too — BECAUSE add-only grows without bound (every self-rotation revokes a predecessor, ~holders/840 per s) against the 256Mi lenny-ops limit, and one cache must have one rehydration semantics — ALTERNATIVES: exp-based pruning (rule-2 events carry no exp); a lenny-ops-only replace method (two semantics, leaves gateway growth).
WATCHOUT: Do not let the swap keep jtis Revoked BEFORE the read began; that is today's carry-forward (`for jti := range c.revoked`) and restores the growth. Replacing is safe because every Cache.Revoke caller pushes after the durable revoked_at commit, so a read beginning after it returns the row — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:137-143; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:319-322; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/issued_tokens.go:65,81; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users_invalidate.go:178-181; spec/13_security-model.md:676.
FACT: Rehydrate's "read began" point is its entry (ListTenants runs before the per-tenant ListRevoked loop); Rehydrate callers are cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:203,214 only — EVIDENCE: grep `.Rehydrate(` over pkg and cmd.
FACT: The summary's deliverable index CODE-6 row now names pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation, since Rehydrate changes unconditionally.


### [spec.16.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 1 replaces the set on each successful read (the rows the read returns plus the jtis rule 2 added after the read began), and CODE-6 item 6 changes the shared `revocation.Cache.Rehydrate` to that behavior rather than adding a lenny-ops-only method — BECAUSE the add-only wording keeps every jti for the life of the process (unbounded growth against the 256Mi ops limit), and one canonical Rehydrate fixes the gateway's identical carry-forward too — ALTERNATIVES: prune by `exp` (rejected: a rule-2 event carries only the jti, so rule-2 entries have no exp to prune on); a new `Cache.Replace` method used only by lenny-ops (rejected: two rehydration semantics for one cache, and the gateway keeps growing); keeping every jti Revoked before the read (rejected: that is today's growth).
FACT: every gateway `Cache.Revoke` call site pushes only after the durable `revoked_at` commit (cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go durableRevoke; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/issued_tokens.go after tokenRevoker.Revoke; users_invalidate.go after RevokeUserTokens), and §13.3 "Token rotation and revocation" publishes `token.revoked` only after COMMIT, so a read that begins after a Revoke sees that row, and replace semantics drops no live revocation. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:662, :664; cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:308-321
WATCHOUT: `TestRehydrateUnionsLocalRevocations` (pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation_test.go:69) asserts the old carry-forward of a jti Revoked BEFORE the read; it must be rewritten (a Revoke made inside the fake source's ListRevoked survives; one made before Rehydrate and absent from the source is dropped), not deleted. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:137-143
WATCHOUT: the replace rule assumes the rule-1 read sees committed rows (primary, not a lagging replica); a lagging read would drop a rule-2 jti for one 30 s cycle. No spec change made for this.

### [spec.16.review-performance.1]
DECISION: filed one finding. SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 1 ("each successful read adds the rows it returns to the set") makes the lenny-ops revocation set add-only, so it grows for the life of the process against the chart's 256Mi `ops.resources.limits.memory`. The fix replaces the set on each successful read and carries forward the rule-2 jti that arrived during the read — BECAUSE the growth is unbounded at any non-zero rotation rate, so no holder-count source is needed to show the defect (the earlier rounds' "do not file without a holder count" applies to the 30 s read cost and not to unbounded memory), and the gateway's add-only `Cache.Rehydrate` is a code defect with no spec mandate, while rule 1 is new spec text that mandates it for lenny-ops — ALTERNATIVES: filing it as a code-only fix (rejected: the staged spec wording itself requires add-only behavior).
FACT: SPEC-6 Edit 2 (the §17.7 audit-lock remedy now lowers the tenant concurrent-session quota) holds. §11.1 "Concurrent-session count" refuses creation at the per-tenant quota (§11.2 row), and the gateway session mint takes the per-tenant lock under SPEC-1, so lowering the quota does cut new-session lock pressure. Not filed. — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:13,29; spec/17_deployment-topology.md:858
CORRECTS [spec.6.review-performance.1, spec.8.review-performance.1 WATCHOUT]: the never-evicting set is a staged-spec defect for lenny-ops, because rule 1's "adds" wording is the cause. It is not only a property of the pre-existing gateway code.

### [spec.16.review-reliability.1]
- DECISION: returned no findings under the reliability lens for round 16 — BECAUSE the recovery paths traced hold under crash, restart, and failover. These are the concurrent-rotation (1a) re-read under the per-tenant xact advisory lock, the three-rollout key rotation with rollout ordering, the half-open single-probe breaker, the lenny-ops 30 s revocation read plus Redis events, and the §17.7 quota remedy — ALTERNATIVES: filing the KMS startup dependency of lenny-ops as a correlated-failure regression; rejected because the Edge cases list accepts the crashloop cost explicitly and the alternative is a design preference.
- FACT: the bootstrap admin rotation crash-window rules (named predecessor, leader-gated sweep, per-subject lock) are gateway-only and never pass through step (1a), so SPEC-7 does not interact with them — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:664-672
- FACT: the existing JWTSigner breaker admits exactly one half-open probe, so the SPEC-4 Retry-After equal to the remaining cooldown does not stampede the KMS at reset — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:101
- WATCHOUT: §11.7 item 3 retries audit-lock acquisition up to audit.lock.maxRetries. Under SPEC-7 a statement failure discards the signed token, so a lock-retry costs another KMS Sign, bounded by maxRetries. This is not a defect. Do not file it as one — EVIDENCE: spec/11_policy-and-controls.md:382
- OPEN: (predates 0097) Token Service issuance needs Postgres, so an operability agent that holds only Lenny-issued tokens cannot refresh during a Postgres outage. Under the §25.15 "core operational loop continues" claim, such an agent loses access within its token lifetime (≤1 h before 0097, 15 min default after). No §25 text says agents refresh through /v1/oauth/token, because the lookup judged all of §25 and found no match. Rule 6 keeps upstream IdP tokens working.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 item 1 is the one home of the sign-result classification (ok, throttled, caller-done error, hard error including SigningCallTimeout expiry); the breaker test and CODE-3 item 3 point at it — BECAUSE four findings (consecutive-run test invariant, wedged half-open probe on throttle, no KMS call deadline, client cancel counted as failure) share the one Sign result path — ALTERNATIVES: separate throttle-release and cancel-release clauses (two parallel exception rules); timeout inside the remote ES256 signer (breaker could not tell its own deadline from the caller's); IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE timeout value (needs bounding prose and a known test value); server WriteTimeout (bounds the request, not the KMS call, three binaries); new SPEC-5 `timeout`/`cancelled` labels (new spec surface).
DECISION: SigningCallTimeout is a fixed 5 s constant with a CallTimeout override on BreakerSigner — BECAUSE 4 hung calls must fit inside the 30 s window so a blackholed KMS trips the breaker — ALTERNATIVES: none viable without a spec edit.
WATCHOUT: classify a caller-done result by the CALLER's `ctx.Err()`, never by errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded/Canceled): a SigningCallTimeout expiry is also DeadlineExceeded and must count, or a hung KMS never trips — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 1.
WATCHOUT: any Sign path that calls neither recordSuccess nor trip leaves probeInFlight held for good, because allow() sets it and only recordSuccess and trip clear it — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go allow() (half-open case), recordSuccess, trip.
WATCHOUT: the breaker trip is a CONSECUTIVE run (recordSuccess sets failures=nil); a test asserting a windowed count fails against spec-correct code. The discriminating case is fail, fail, ok, fail, fail inside 30 s, which stays closed — EVIDENCE: breakersigner.go recordSuccess; SPEC-4 Edit 1 "> 3 consecutive signing failures within 30s".
WATCHOUT: a half-open refusal has zero or negative remaining cooldown, so a positive Retry-After is asserted only after the CODE-7 HTTP floor (tier 2), never at tier 1.
FACT: no surface bounds a KMS sign call today: pkg/kms/{aws,gcp,azure} set no Timeout, and the gateway and Token Service http.Servers set only ReadHeaderTimeout — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go (http.Server literal), cmd/lenny-token-service/surfaces.go.
FACT: the related-passages lookup over the four changed statements returned only other breakers (§10.7 OpenFeature, LLM Proxy, Token Service) and the pre-0097 §10.2 KMS paragraph SPEC-4 replaces; none is falsified.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G4.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 6 DSN bullet gains the Postgres NetworkPolicy reach in the same commit as the DSN: `allow-pgbouncer` admits `app: lenny-ops` with the release-namespace selector (§13.2.4 PgBouncer row), and `lenny-ops-egress` renders a 5432 `ipBlock` peer for `postgres.gatewayEgressCIDR` when set, added alongside the PgBouncer rule as `allow-gateway-egress` does — BECAUSE without it an enforcing CNI drops the rule-1 read and rule 4 refuses every Lenny-issued token for the pod's life — ALTERNATIVES: landing it in CHART-4 (S27 lands after S18, leaves a window; CHART-4 owns KMS egress only).
WATCHOUT: Do not add `app: lenny-ops` to `allow-postgres-self-managed` (self-managed-backend-network-policies.yaml). I staged it this round and reverted it in the same pass, because §25.4 `lenny-ops-egress` says "`lenny-ops` does not bypass the pooler; direct `app: postgres` access is reserved for `lenny-backup-job`". — EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md:1179-1180; charts/lenny/templates/self-managed-backend-network-policies.yaml:142-176.
OPEN: In the chart-managed self-managed profile (`postgres.deploy: true`), postgres-cluster.yaml's header says `postgres.dsn` points at `lenny-postgres-rw` directly. Through the shared `postgres-dsn` key that CODE-6 item 6 wires, `lenny-ops` would then bypass PgBouncer, and `allow-postgres-self-managed` drops it, so rule 4 refuses every Lenny-issued token in that profile. Any fix must keep §25.4's no-bypass rule: either route lenny-ops through a pooler DSN or change §25.4. Not closed here; a later round or the owner should decide. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/postgres-cluster.yaml:8-15; spec/25_agent-operability.md:1179-1180.
WATCHOUT: §25.4 says the chart "replaces" the lenny-ops PgBouncer egress rule with an ipBlock for a cloud-managed proxy, while the chart's gateway policy adds the ipBlock alongside. CODE-6 follows the gateway (adds). The replace-versus-add mismatch predates 0097 and is not filed. EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md:1187-1189; charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:253-260.

Over the key set it would reject Lenny-issued tokens whose `iss` is not the IdP's (the Token Service uses `s.issuer`, and playground uses its own). CODE-6 item 3 scopes the issuer check to the dev trust member, and a tier-1 case pins that. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:184-187; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:681.

### [non-spec.1.fix-G5.1]
FACT: The only exported way to take the §11.7 per-tenant audit lock is `auditstore.AppendInTx`, which locks and INSERTs the audit row in one call; `acquireAuditLock` (lock.go) is unexported. Both `issuedtokenstore` methods call `AppendInTx` first, so before CODE-9 there was no post-lock, pre-INSERT point. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/auditstore.go `AppendInTx`; pkg/gateway/storage/issuedtokenstore/issuedtokenstore.go `RecordWithAudit`, `RecordWithRotationAudit`
DECISION: CODE-9 item 1 takes the lock through a new exported `auditstore.AcquireLockInTx` (the lock half of `AppendInTx`, which then calls it), runs step (1a), then calls `AppendInTx`. The transaction-scoped advisory lock is reentrant, so the second acquisition returns at once. — BECAUSE staged SPEC-7 says a (1a) rejection skips steps (2) and (3), and only a lock taken before any INSERT honours that while keeping one home for the lock key and timeout. — ALTERNATIVES: (1a) after `AppendInTx` with rollback contradicts SPEC-7 and burns an audit nextval per rejection; (1a) before `AppendInTx` sits outside the lock and reopens the double-rotation race; a precheck callback on `AppendInTx` changes every caller; a lock-key literal in issuedtokenstore is a second home that drifts.
WATCHOUT: Do not "fix" CODE-9 by reordering the issued_tokens and audit INSERTs to match SPEC-7's (2)/(3) numbering. The audit-first order predates 0097 and lies outside CODE-9.
FACT: The Token Service has no revocation pre-check on the exchange path. `cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go` builds the §4.3 access cache and discards it (`_ = w.accessCache`), so a direct Token Service call presenting a revoked token is refused only by step (1a). That makes the tier-9 CODE-9 case discriminating. Routing it through the gateway would let `childtoken.Minter` refuse first. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go accessCache wiring
DECISION: The CODE-9 tests split by tier: tier 1 `pkg/tokenservice` (item 2 response, rejection audit row, failing Auditor gives 500), tier 2 `tests/tier2_component` (store typed error, no rows committed, no-row passes), tier 7a `tests/tier7a_load_local` (concurrent rotation race, the discriminating test for `AcquireLockInTx` placement), and tier 9 `tests/tier9_security` (end-to-end refusal of a revoked subject and actor). — BECAUSE `persistIssuedToken` owns the rejection audit and status mapping and the store only returns an error, and S22 claims tiers 0, 1, 2, 7a, and 9. — ALTERNATIVES: dropping 7a and 9 from S22 contradicts test-coverage.md; putting the tier-9 case in TEST-1 lands it in S29 instead of S22; the reviewer's "Redis stopped" condition tests nothing, because no pre-check exists.


### [non-spec.1.fix-G6.1]
DECISION: `KMSSignQuotaApproaching` reads its threshold from a gateway startup gauge, `0.8 * scalar(max(lenny_kms_sign_quota_per_second) > 0)`, and renders on every provider from the generated catalog. CHART-5 renders only the gateway `--kms-sign-quota-per-second` flag. CODE-7 owns the flag, the gauge, and the catalog rows — BECAUSE `Rule.Validate` parses every catalog Expr as PromQL (pkg/alerting/rules/rules.go:160), and the tier-2 crosschecks require the rendered set, the catalog, and the gateway evaluator set to be equal with identical exprs. The catalog already handles Helm-valued thresholds this way (GatewayActiveStreamsHigh, rules.go:734; SetStreamCeiling in cmd/lenny-gateway/metricsbackfill.go:346) — ALTERNATIVES: provider-conditional exceptions in three §25.13 crosscheck tests (still leaves no parseable threshold); a Helm placeholder in the generated fragment (fails TestCatalogValidates); a chart-side rule outside the catalog (fails "chart must not invent rules" and evaluator parity); gauge plus conditional render (two mechanisms, no gain).
WATCHOUT: the `> 0` guard and the max() are both required. The gateway pre-materializes threshold gauges at 0 (gatewaymetrics_alertthreshold_test.go:25-30), so without the guard the alert fires on every local install that signs anything. scalar() of a multi-replica vector is NaN, so bare scalar(gauge) never fires on a multi-replica fleet — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-11 item 1.
WATCHOUT: the quota gauge goes in `alertSupportCatalog`, never in `metricCatalog` or `spec161Metrics`. The new counter `lenny_jwt_sign_requests_total` goes in both `metricCatalog` and `spec161Metrics`, or TestMetricCatalogHasNoUnspecifiedMetrics or TestEverySpec165AlertMetricIsCatalogued fails — EVIDENCE: pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:399, :418; catalog_test.go:189-210; alert_catalog_crosscheck_test.go:68.
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: in SPEC-5 Edit 4, the `KMSSignQuotaApproaching` row's sentence "Rendered only when `kms.provider` is not `local`." is false under the staged non-spec design and contradicts §16.5 "Because both consumers share the same source" and §16.9 "one `PrometheusRule` containing the full alert catalog". Delete the sentence. The rule renders on every provider and stays inert under `local` because no quota gauge value is set. The row's `> 0.8 * kms.signQuotaPerSecond` may stay as the abstract threshold statement, as §16.5 states other Helm-valued thresholds.
FACT: the Token Service has no BreakerObserver today (no Observer in cmd/lenny-token-service or pkg/tokenservice non-test code). Its metrics emitter is pkg/tokenservice/promemit/promemit.go. CODE-7 item 2's Token Service observer is new code — EVIDENCE: grep of cmd/lenny-token-service/*.go pkg/tokenservice/*.go.
FACT: neither tests/testinfra/gateway nor tests/testinfra/tokenservice exposes /metrics, and no tier-2 harness enables the playground. That is why the sign-counter and playground Retry-After tests are tier 1 — EVIDENCE: design-stage grep, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go:254, :288.
OPEN: docs/reference/metrics.md "Alert rules" has a GatewayActiveStreamsHigh row (line 512) but no KMSSigningUnavailable row. Whether DOCS-2 should add a KMSSignQuotaApproaching row there was not checked against the tier-11 docs tests. A docs-alignment lens should check it.

S15 keeps 4, S18 keeps 5, S21 keeps 3 and 5, S22 keeps 7a and 9 — BECAUSE removing them would let a broken tier-4 package compile-gap, a Kind crashloop on the flag rename, or the CODE-9 race test pass the step gate — ALTERNATIVES: reducing every list to the tiers of new tests (the finding's suggestion; rejected by the design).

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 item 1 becomes the single home of the sign-result classification (ok / throttled / caller-done / counted error) and of a per-call `SigningCallTimeout` (5 s, `CallTimeout` override) applied inside `BreakerSigner.Sign`. Throttled and caller-done results are neutral: they neither count nor clear the failure run, and in half-open state they clear `probeInFlight` and leave the breaker half-open. BECAUSE one classification closes all four G1 findings, and the breaker already owns Threshold/Window/Cooldown, so the timeout sits beside them and the breaker can tell the caller's context from its own deadline. ALTERNATIVES: timeout in the remote signer (CODE-2 item 2), rejected because the classification then depends on the error type rather than on the caller ctx; IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE timeout value, rejected because a fixed constant is fewer words and testable; a `timeout` or `cancelled` reason/outcome label, rejected because it adds metric surface SPEC-5 does not define.
DECISION: no edit to SPEC-4 (spec-changes.md "KMS signing failure mode") or SPEC-5 `inner`. A caller cancellation is not a signing failure (the signer did not fail to obtain a signature; the request was abandoned), and a timeout is a signing failure whose KMS call returned an error. BECAUSE the spec states the trip predicate and the throttle carve-out; which Go errors map to "signing failure" is implementation detail.
WATCHOUT: classify caller-done by `ctx.Err()` on the caller's context, never by `errors.Is(err, context.DeadlineExceeded)`, because the breaker's own timeout expiry is also DeadlineExceeded and must count. EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:108-114 (today any error counts).
FACT: `probeInFlight` is set only in `allow()` (open->half-open, half-open admit) and cleared only in `recordSuccess` and `trip`; any inner result that calls neither leaves the breaker refusing every call until restart. EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:169-180, :193, :227.
FACT: `recordSuccess` clears `failures`, so the trip predicate is a consecutive run (fail, fail, ok, fail, fail inside 30 s stays closed); a test asserting a windowed count fails against correct code. EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go:185-187.
FACT: no KMS adapter sets its own timeout, and the gateway and Token Service http.Servers set only ReadHeaderTimeout, so request contexts carry no deadline. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:976-980; cmd/lenny-token-service/surfaces.go:28-32; grep of pkg/kms/{aws,gcp,azure} for Timeout is empty.
WATCHOUT: a half-open refusal's "remaining cooldown" is zero or negative; only the HTTP layer (CODE-7) floors it to 1. Do not assert a positive `RetryAfter` at tier 1. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 2.

### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G4.1]
DECISION: buildAuthConfig returns an authmw config whenever `--production` or `--bearer-trust-hmac-key-file` is set (verifier = key set, MultiVerifier with the dev trust member when the flag is set); the production-requires-HMAC error is deleted; the nil (unauthenticated) return stays only for non-production without the trust flag — BECAUSE item 4 plus today's error leaves production unable to start, and removing the nil dev path would break every tier-4 opsprocess test that sends no X-Lenny-Roles (runbook_discovery, lenny_ctl_ops, event_subscription_cold_start, mcp_management_event_subscription, backup_restore_resume_lock) and pre-empts the D26 dev-relaxation follow-up — ALTERNATIVES: always return a config (reviewer's suggestion; unbounded tier-4 cascade, closes a separate pre-existing §25.4 "No anonymous access" gap out of scope); a bearer-only authmw mode for the dev path (new opsserver posture, hair).

The check must wrap only the dev trust member. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:184-186, pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/playground/token.go:271, pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:681

WATCHOUT: tier-5 Kind uses kindnet, which does not enforce NetworkPolicy, so the Helm unittest is the only pin on the lenny-ops Postgres reach. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/kind/cluster.yaml:59

CODE-6 mirrors the gateway (additive); the replace-versus-add gap is pre-existing and was not filed. — EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md:1186-1188, charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:253-259


### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G5.1]
DECISION: CODE-9 step (1a) runs after a new exported `auditstore.AcquireLockInTx` (the lock half of `AppendInTx`, which becomes AcquireLockInTx + sealAndInsert) and before `AppendInTx` — BECAUSE staged SPEC-7 says a (1a) rejection "skips steps (2) and (3)", and the only post-lock point the tree offers today is after the audit INSERT — ALTERNATIVES: run (1a) after AppendInTx and let the rollback discard the audit row (contradicts staged SPEC-7 "skips (3)", which this non-spec loop cannot edit, and burns an audit nextval); run (1a) before AppendInTx (outside the lock, reopens the double-rotation race); a precheck callback parameter on AppendInTx (more surface); a second copy of the `audit:`+tenant lock key in issuedtokenstore (two homes for the lock key).
FACT: `AppendInTx` takes the §11.7 lock and inserts the audit row in one call; `acquireAuditLock` is unexported. Re-taking `pg_advisory_xact_lock` in the same transaction returns at once (session-reentrant), so AcquireLockInTx followed by AppendInTx is safe. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/auditstore.go:379-384; pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/lock.go:174-196
FACT: `pgtenant.InTx` uses `pool.Begin` (READ COMMITTED), so a SELECT issued after the lock wait sees the winner's commit. A REPEATABLE READ transaction would not, because its snapshot is taken by the lock statement itself. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pgtenant/pgtenant.go:83
FACT: the Token Service checks no subject or actor revocation before persist today. `pkg/tokenservice/cache` is wired into no non-test code. So after CODE-9, step (1a) is the only refusal on a direct Token Service call, and "Redis stopped" adds nothing to a tier-9 case. EVIDENCE: grep of tokenservice/cache importers; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go persistIssuedToken
FACT: no pkg/ test starts Postgres. Tier-2 Postgres store tests live under tests/tier2_component (`component` tag, e.g. issuedtokencascade). Tier-7a Postgres race tests use containers.StartPostgres under tests/tier7a_load_local (`load_local` tag). The tier-9 write-before-issue fail-closed tests are at tests/tier9_security/write_before_issue_failclosed_test.go. EVIDENCE: tests/tier2_component/issuedtokencascade/cascade_test.go:1; tests/tier7a_load_local/lifecycle_fence_scenarios_test.go:236
WATCHOUT: the tier-9 (1a) case must call the Token Service directly. Routed through the gateway, `childtoken.Minter`'s pre-check refuses first and the test no longer discriminates (1a). EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-9 item 5
WATCHOUT: do not reorder the issued_tokens and audit INSERTs to match SPEC-7's (2)/(3) numbering. The code runs the audit INSERT first, that order predates 0097, and it is outside this group.
DECISION: split "Tests that land with CODE-9" by tier: tier 1 tokenservice (status codes and rejection audit through a fake store), tier 2 store (typed error, no commit), tier 7a concurrent rotation, and tier 9 end-to-end refusal — BECAUSE the store cannot observe HTTP status or the rejection audit (persistIssuedToken writes both), and test-coverage.md puts atomicity at 7a and auth at 9, which S22 already claims — ALTERNATIVES: drop 7a and 9 from S22 (contradicts test-coverage.md's tier rules for this change).


### [non-spec.1.fix-design-G6.1]
DECISION: KMSSignQuotaApproaching reads its threshold from a gateway startup gauge `lenny_kms_sign_quota_per_second` (new gateway flag `--kms-sign-quota-per-second`, rendered by CHART-5 from `kms.signQuotaPerSecond` on cloud providers), with catalog Expr `sum(rate(lenny_jwt_sign_requests_total{outcome!="circuit_open"}[5m])) > 0.8 * scalar(max(lenny_kms_sign_quota_per_second) > 0)`, and renders on every provider from the generated fragment. CODE-11's IMPLEMENTOR'S CHOICE is deleted and CHART-5 becomes the gateway flag render. — BECAUSE this is the catalog's existing pattern for Helm-valued thresholds (BillingCorrectionRateHigh, GatewayActiveStreamsHigh), and it leaves TestCatalogValidates and the three tier-2 crosschecks green with no test edits. — ALTERNATIVES: a Helm placeholder in the generated fragment, which fails PromQL parsing in `Rule.Validate` and the expr field match; a chart-side rule outside the catalog, which fails crosscheck direction 2 and evaluator parity; keeping conditional rendering plus named exceptions in TestPrometheusRuleMatchesAlertCatalog, TestGatewayEvaluatorTracksCatalogAndRenderedChart, and TestPrometheusRuleFieldsMatchCatalog, which is hair in three §25.13 invariant tests and still needs a threshold the catalog Expr can parse.
WATCHOUT: the gateway pre-materializes its threshold gauges at 0 (gatewaymetrics_alertthreshold_test.go asserts "lenny_gateway_stream_ceiling 0"). A quota gauge at 0 without the `> 0` guard makes `rate > 0.8*0` fire on every local install that signs anything. Use `max()` inside scalar() too, because scalar() of a multi-replica vector is NaN. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/metrics/gatewaymetrics/gatewaymetrics_alertthreshold_test.go:25-30; tests/tier2_component/observability/catalog_crosscheck_test.go:208-226
FACT: inproceval rejects rate() with ErrUnsupportedExpr, so the gateway fallback never fires KMSSignQuotaApproaching or KMSSigningUnavailable. Prometheus is the only evaluator that fires them, and the gauge's in-process value does not matter. — EVIDENCE: pkg/alerting/inproceval/inproceval.go:13-26, :87
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md SPEC-5 Edit 4, `KMSSignQuotaApproaching` row]: delete "Rendered only when `kms.provider` is not `local`." The sentence contradicts §16.5's "both consumers share the same source" (the gateway evaluator and the rendered chart carry one rule set, and the tier-2 crosschecks enforce it on the default `local` render). The rule is inert under `local` because no quota is configured. The expression `0.8 * kms.signQuotaPerSecond` can stay as written, since it names the configured value the way GatewayActiveStreamsHigh's row names "the configured stream ceiling".
USEFUL [spec.1.review-mechanism.1]: its FACT that Helm-valued thresholds use a startup gauge plus scalar() pointed straight at the coherent fix.
CORRECTS [redesign.2]: the WATCHOUT saying the quota alert is pinned by "the CHART-5 `0.8 × quota` render" no longer holds. After G6 the alert is pinned by the CODE-11 tier-11 check, the unchanged tier-2 catalog crosschecks, the CODE-7 tier-1 gauge case, and the CHART-5 flag-render unittest.
FACT: lenny_jwt_sign_requests_total needs a metricCatalog row and a spec161Metrics entry. The quota gauge is an alert-support series and belongs in alertSupportCatalog, which needs no spec161Metrics entry. — EVIDENCE: pkg/observability/metrics/catalog.go:62, :399; alert_catalog_crosscheck_test.go:68-93; catalog_test.go:189-210

- FACT: every cache Revoke caller on the gateway writes issued_tokens first (user_revocation.go:296-322, issued_tokens.go:65-81, users_invalidate.go:172-181), and the only token:revocations publisher is the gateway propagator, so CODE-6's replace-semantics Rehydrate drops no revocation that Postgres lacks. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/propagator/propagator.go:39,83-90
- FACT: §13.3 rehydration window is exp-based (exp > now() - 1h), so a revoked 10-year admin credential stays in the replace-semantics set. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:676
- FACT: go-redis PubSub.Channel reconnects on its own, so propagator.Run on lenny-ops resumes rule 2 after a Redis blip; missed events are covered by the 30 s read. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pubsub/bus.go:74-93
- FACT: lenny-ops pgxpool.New is lazy, so a lenny-ops started during a Postgres outage still reaches its first successful read after recovery. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/deps_setup.go:86-91

### [non-spec.2.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: `lenny-ops` stays on the pooler; CODE-6 item 6 gains one trap sentence that `allow-postgres-self-managed` does not admit `lenny-ops`, per the §25.4 `lenny-ops-egress` Postgres rule, so the rule-1 read runs only when `postgres.dsn` names PgBouncer or the `postgres.gatewayEgressCIDR` endpoint — BECAUSE §25.4 says "`lenny-ops` does not bypass the pooler; direct `app: postgres` access is reserved for `lenny-backup-job`" and the §13.2.4 `lenny-ops` row grants egress to PgBouncer only, so a direct ingress clause would need a spec edit — ALTERNATIVES: admitting `app: lenny-ops` in `allow-postgres-self-managed` (contradicts §25.4 and §13.2.4, widens the operability-plane containment boundary); rewriting the self-managed answer files so `postgres.dsn` names PgBouncer (a separate, pre-existing gateway defect); a render gate that string-matches the DSN (new mechanism).
WATCHOUT: Do not re-file "lenny-ops cannot reach chart-managed Postgres, add it to allow-postgres-self-managed". The direct-DSN topology is the defect, and the fix is keeping `lenny-ops` on the pooler. EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md:1178-1179; charts/lenny/templates/self-managed-backend-network-policies.yaml:142-176; charts/lenny/values.yaml:1325-1328.

Owner: a direct chart fix or a follow-up proposal.
FACT: `allow-pgbouncer` and `lenny-ops-egress` both render in the dev-preset embedded manifests, so any CODE-6 item 6 policy edit needs `make generate` in the same commit, and S18 now carries tier 11 for `TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender`. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:308, :859; tests/tier11_docs/embedded_manifests_sync_test.go.


### [non-spec.2.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: CODE-1 item 2 requires `Sign` to disable the SDK retry for its own call (AWS per-call `RetryMaxAttempts = 1`, Azure `policy.WithRetryOptions` `MaxRetries: -1`, GCP `gax.WithRetry` returning nil), and CODE-1's tier-1 tests gain a real-SDK, local-server case asserting one request — BECAUSE the SDK retry middleware sleeps past the 5 s `SigningCallTimeout`, the error chain then holds only the context error, and CODE-3 counts it as `recordFailure`, so sustained throttling tripped the breaker against SPEC-4 — ALTERNATIVES: a CODE-3 classification change (cannot recover a throttle the SDK already discarded; a hung KMS must still trip), raising the timeout above the SDK budget (Azure honours Retry-After up to 60 s and retries add load), client-construction retry off (removes retries from WrapDEK/UnwrapDEK/PublicKey), a throttle-only custom retryer (per-provider code lists drift; a 5xx retry still hits the deadline), AWS/Azure-only rule (depends on today's GCP defaults).
WATCHOUT: Do not edit CODE-3 item 1's classification for throttle handling again; it is correct, and round-1's two breaker-side rewrites did not hold. The fix lives in the adapter that owns the SDK call. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-1 item 2, CODE-3 item 1
WATCHOUT: The GCP `clientAdapter` in pkg/kms/gcp/gcp.go takes `option.ClientOption` per call and discards it; the AsymmetricSign wrapper the implementor adds must forward a `gax.CallOption` (`gax.WithRetry(func() gax.Retryer { return nil })`) to `inner.AsymmetricSign`, or the default Unavailable/DeadlineExceeded retry stays on. — EVIDENCE: pkg/kms/gcp/gcp.go:62-79; cloud.google.com/go/kms@v1.26.0 apiv1/key_management_client.go:417-429
FACT: Per-call retry knobs verified in the module cache: aws service/kms@v1.52.0 options.go:76-84 (operation RetryMaxAttempts wraps the retryer); azcore@v1.21.1 policy/policy.go:193 `WithRetryOptions`, runtime/policy_retry.go:27-30 (negative MaxRetries means 0) and :106-109 (ctx override read per request); gax-go/v2@v2.21.0 invoke.go:124-131 (nil Retryer returns the error at once). GCP AsymmetricSign default retries Unavailable and DeadlineExceeded only. — EVIDENCE: paths above

### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: the lenny-ops Postgres path is the pooler only. CODE-6 item 6 does not admit `app: lenny-ops` in `allow-postgres-self-managed`; it gains one trap sentence pointing at the §25.4 `lenny-ops-egress` Postgres rule — BECAUSE §25.4 ("`lenny-ops` does not bypass the pooler; direct `app: postgres` access is reserved for `lenny-backup-job`") and the §13.2.4 Gateway/Token Service/controller/lenny-ops egress rows (PgBouncer only) admit no client other than PgBouncer and the backup job to the Postgres pods, and the non-spec loop cannot stage the spec edit a direct path needs — ALTERNATIVES: admit lenny-ops in allow-postgres-self-managed plus a §25.4/§13.2.4 spec edit (widens the operability-plane containment boundary the spec reserves for the backup job; extends the chart's own pooler bypass to a fourth client; the spec loop is closed).
FACT: every shipped self-managed answer file (charts/lenny/answers/catalog/bare-metal-self-managed.yaml:12-13,26-28; openshift-self-managed.yaml; airgap-self-managed.yaml) and the values.yaml `postgres.deploy` comment (charts/lenny/values.yaml:1325-1328) tell the operator to point `postgres.dsn` at `lenny-postgres-rw`, bypassing the PgBouncer the same answer file deploys with `connectionPooler: pgbouncer`. No answer file sets `postgres.gatewayEgressCIDR`, and `allow-gateway-egress` reaches Postgres only through pgbouncer pods or that CIDR (charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:555-588), so under an enforcing CNI the gateway cannot reach Postgres in those renders either. `allow-postgres-self-managed` admitting gateway/token-service/controller (self-managed-backend-network-policies.yaml:159-176) is chart-ahead-of-spec.
OPEN: (separate finding, predates 0097) the self-managed answer files and the `postgres.deploy` values comment point `postgres.dsn` at `lenny-postgres-rw` rather than the deployed `lenny-pgbouncer` Service, which bypasses the §12.3 connect_query sentinel the declared `connectionPooler: pgbouncer` relies on and contradicts the §13.2.4 PgBouncer-only client egress rows. Under CODE-6 lenny-ops shares that DSN, so on those renders the rule-1 read fails and SPEC-11 Edit 2 rule 4 refuses every Lenny-issued token. The remedy (dsn → lenny-pgbouncer, `pgbouncer.databaseURLSecret` → the CNPG app secret) fixes the gateway and lenny-ops together; a later round or a direct chart fix should own it.
WATCHOUT: do not re-file "lenny-ops cannot reach chart-managed Postgres" as a missing allow-postgres-self-managed clause; the gap is the DSN target in the OPEN above, not a NetworkPolicy omission. — EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md §25.4 `lenny-ops-egress` Postgres comment; spec/13_security-model.md §13.2.4 PgBouncer row.
FACT: the round-1 `postgres.gatewayEgressCIDR` ipBlock peer on lenny-ops-egress is the §25.4 cloud-managed/external substitution ("the chart replaces this rule with an `ipBlock` egress entry resolved at render time"), so it stays under the pooler-only decision. — EVIDENCE: spec/25_agent-operability.md §25.4 `lenny-ops-egress` Postgres comment.
DECISION: CODE-6 Targets gain `pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml` (regenerated by `make generate` in the same commit) and S18 gains tier 11; no new Testing bullet — BECAUSE the `allow-pgbouncer` ingress clause renders unconditionally into the dev-preset render (pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:859) and the existing tier-11 sync test pins it; checklist tier lists are final-gate sets ([non-spec.1] decision at review-log "checklist tier lists are final-gate sets over regression reach") — ALTERNATIVES: a CODE-6 tier-11 Testing bullet (restates an existing test; the tier-list convention does not need it).
FACT: of CODE-6's chart edits only the `allow-pgbouncer` clause changes the dev render: the `lenny-ops-egress` CIDR peer and both env vars are conditional on unset dev values (charts/lenny/presets/dev.yaml:81 `dsn: ""`), item 5 is `ops.production`-gated, and the template header comments are `{{/* */}}` comments that do not render.


### [non-spec.2.fix-design-G2.1]
DECISION: close the throttle-swallowed-by-SDK-retry finding in CODE-1 item 2 (the adapter owns the SDK call), not in CODE-3 — BECAUSE CODE-3's classification is right once the adapter surfaces kms.ErrThrottled from one provider request; the two earlier CODE-3 rewrites edited the breaker and could not see the SDK middleware — ALTERNATIVES: (a) classify SigningCallTimeout expiry as neutral in CODE-3: rejected, a hung KMS must count toward the SPEC-4 trip; (b) raise SigningCallTimeout above the SDK retry budget: rejected, Azure honours Retry-After up to MaxRetryDelay 60 s and retries amplify load on a throttling KMS; (c) disable retries at client construction (AWS Options / azkeys ClientOptions): rejected, it also strips retries from WrapDEK/UnwrapDEK and the startup PublicKey fetch; (d) retry everything except throttle codes via a custom AWS Retryer / Azure StatusCodes list: rejected as hair, a per-provider code list that drifts with SDK versions.
FACT: default SDK retry on the sign path. AWS standard retryer treats ThrottlingException and LimitExceededException as retryable throttles, and a ctx expiry during its backoff sleep returns aws.RequestCanceledError wrapping the ctx error, dropping the throttle — EVIDENCE: aws-sdk-go-v2@v1.41.7 aws/retry/standard.go:60-69, aws/retry/middleware.go:292-293. Azure azcore retries 429 up to 3 times, honouring Retry-After up to 60 s, and returns ctx.Err() when the context ends during the wait — EVIDENCE: azcore@v1.21.1 runtime/policy_retry.go:23-55, :199-216. GCP cloudkms AsymmetricSign retries only Unavailable and DeadlineExceeded, not ResourceExhausted — EVIDENCE: cloud.google.com/go/kms@v1.26.0 apiv1/key_management_client.go:417-429.
FACT: per-call retry knobs that leave the client defaults alone: AWS per-call option func setting kms.Options.RetryMaxAttempts = 1 (service/kms@v1.52.0 options.go:76-84); Azure policy.WithRetryOptions(ctx, policy.RetryOptions{MaxRetries: -1}), which the retry policy reads from the request context (azcore policy/policy.go:193, runtime/policy_retry.go:106-109, setDefaults maps -1 to 0); GCP gax.WithRetry(func() gax.Retryer { return nil }), and gax returns the first error when the Retryer is nil (gax-go/v2@v2.21.0 invoke.go:124-131). The GCP clientAdapter discards its option.ClientOption varargs today (pkg/kms/gcp/gcp.go:72-80), so its AsymmetricSign wrapper has to pass the gax option to the inner call itself.
WATCHOUT: an httptest server behind a real azkeys client receives the Key Vault challenge-auth request (unauthenticated, expecting 401 with WWW-Authenticate) before the sign request. The one-request assertion counts sign requests, so it must not count the challenge. The test also needs a TLS server and a fake TokenCredential, and the Provider has to be built through newWithClient around a real azkeys client, because New passes nil ClientOptions (pkg/kms/azure/azure.go:90).
WATCHOUT: GCP's default policy does not retry ResourceExhausted, so a GCP throttle response cannot show that retries are disabled. The GCP case of the one-request test answers with codes.Unavailable, which the default policy does retry.

CODE-11 item 1 stays the one home of the gauge, the `> 0` guard, and `max()` — BECAUSE the spec states the threshold, the catalog carries the mechanism, and the §16.1 staging does not name the alert-support gauge — ALTERNATIVES: replacing the sentence with "It does not fire while no quota is configured" (rung 3 where deletion closes it; CODE-11 already pins the guard); writing the gauge expression into §16.5 (puts an alertSupportCatalog metric the §16.1 staging never names into the spec); restoring a provider-conditional render (fails the tier-2 bundled-vs-catalog crosschecks, see [non-spec.1] DECISION at review-log "KMSSignQuotaApproaching reads its threshold from a gateway startup gauge").

WATCHOUT: the firing test's `firingCase` table builds only firing expectations (it always sets one `exp_alerts` entry); the non-firing KMSSignQuotaApproaching cases need a no-alert form (`exp_alerts: []`). — EVIDENCE: tests/tier4_integration/alerting_rules_prometheus_firing_test.go:85-95, :230-240
FACT: pkg/alerting/rules/rules_test.go TestCatalogHasNoUnspecifiedAlerts fails for any Catalog() rule missing from spec165CriticalAlerts/spec165WarningAlerts/spec165BurnRateSLOs, and the reverse test fails for a listed name missing from Catalog(), so the list edit and the rules.go edit land in one commit. healthComponents (health_components.go) is checked only in the map-to-catalog direction, so KMSSignQuotaApproaching needs no entry there. — EVIDENCE: pkg/alerting/rules/rules_test.go:252, :319, :337-353; pkg/alerting/rules/health_components_test.go:10-17

### [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 6 stages a lenny-ops-only DSN, `postgres.opsDsn` rendered as `lenny-datastore-conn` key `ops-postgres-dsn` (precedence over `postgres-dsn`), and the self-managed answer files and its values.yaml comment tell operators to point it at `lenny-pgbouncer` — BECAUSE the chart-managed self-managed install points the one shared `postgres.dsn` at `<release>-postgres-rw`, which `allow-postgres-self-managed` does not admit for lenny-ops, so lenny-ops refused every Lenny-issued token for life — ALTERNATIVES: admit lenny-ops in `allow-postgres-self-managed` (contradicts §25.4 "lenny-ops does not bypass the pooler", needs a spec edit); retarget the shared `postgres.dsn` at PgBouncer in the answer files (moves the gateway, controller, and migrations onto transaction pooling, unverified); only qualify the refusal (leaves the recommended install broken); derive a PgBouncer DSN by rewriting the host of `postgres.dsn` with sprig urlParse (fails on keyword-form DSNs).
MISTAKE: the round-2 fixer predicate "the rule-1 read runs only when `postgres.dsn` names PgBouncer or the endpoint `postgres.gatewayEgressCIDR` admits" was wrong for postgres-rw installs: the CIDR admits the endpoint at egress, but `allow-postgres-self-managed` drops lenny-ops at ingress. Deleted.

### [non-spec.2.review-performance.1]
- FACT: `acquireAuditLock` is `pg_advisory_xact_lock(hashtext('audit:'+tenant))` under a `SET LOCAL statement_timeout`; it is reentrant within one transaction, so CODE-9's `AcquireLockInTx` followed by `AppendInTx` (which re-acquires) does not deadlock and adds only three cheap statements to the per-tenant critical section. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/lock.go:174-196, auditstore.go:379-384
- FACT: the gateway alert evaluator queries Prometheus (fleet-wide), so the KMSSignQuotaApproaching `sum(...)` across gateway and Token Service components is evaluated fleet-wide rather than per replica. EVIDENCE: pkg/alerting/evaluator/evaluator.go:10,40
- WATCHOUT: `allow-pgbouncer` and `lenny-ops-egress` render in the dev-preset embedded manifests, so CODE-6 item 6's network-policy edit changes pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml and must regenerate it in S18, or tier-11 `TestEmbeddedManifestsMatchDevProfileRender` fails until S25. The ops-deployment.yaml header sentence is a `{{/* */}}` comment and does not render. EVIDENCE: pkg/embedded/manifests/manifests.yaml:308,859-875; charts/lenny/templates/ops-deployment.yaml:35-36
- USEFUL [review-log DEFERRED spec-changes.md SPEC-5 Edit 4 "Rendered only when"]: already tracked; not re-filed.


### [non-spec.2.review-reliability.1]
FACT: The KMS adapters build SDK clients with default retry policies: Azure `azkeys.NewClient(..., nil)` (pkg/kms/azure/azure.go:90) gets azcore's MaxRetries 3, 800 ms base delay, and honours a 429 `Retry-After`; on context expiry during the wait it returns `ctx.Err()` (azcore v1.21.1 runtime/policy_retry.go:199-217). AWS `awskms.NewFromConfig` (pkg/kms/aws/aws.go:88) gets the standard retryer (3 attempts, ThrottlingException retryable) and wraps a sleep cut short by the context as `aws.RequestCanceledError` (aws-sdk-go-v2 aws/retry/middleware.go:292-293). Any per-call deadline therefore turns a throttle into DeadlineExceeded. EVIDENCE: the cited lines
WATCHOUT: CODE-3's 5 s `SigningCallTimeout` counts a deadline expiry as a hard failure, so the SDK retry loops above let sustained throttling trip the breaker. The CODE-1 fake-client tests bypass SDK middleware and cannot catch it. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md:28, :50-54, CODE-1 tests
WATCHOUT: CODE-11 now renders KMSSignQuotaApproaching on every provider (needed because tier-2 catalog_crosscheck_test.go asserts that every catalog alert appears in the rendered PrometheusRule), but staged SPEC-5 Edit 4 (spec-changes.md:169) still says "Rendered only when kms.provider is not local". EVIDENCE: tests/tier2_component/observability/catalog_crosscheck_test.go:14-18
FACT: Advisory xact locks are re-entrant, so CODE-9's AcquireLockInTx followed by AppendInTx (which calls it again) works. pgtenant.InTx uses the default READ COMMITTED, so the step (1a) SELECT after the lock sees a committed concurrent rotation. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pgtenant/pgtenant.go:83; pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/lock.go:180
UNVERIFIED: The harness `waitReady` returns on /readyz before the lenny-ops rule-1 startup read completes, so a tier-4 assertion that a key-set token is accepted immediately after start can race the rule-4 gate. I judged it low-probability and did not file it. The test-coverage lens should check it.

### [non-spec.3.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 6 deletes `postgres.opsDsn`, the `ops-postgres-dsn` Secret key, the `datastore-secret.yaml` edit, and the answer-file/values instruction; `lenny-ops` renders `LENNY_POSTGRES_DSN` from `lenny-datastore-conn` key `postgres-dsn` when `postgres.dsn` is set, as `gateway-deployment.yaml` does. The broken self-managed Postgres topology is one summary Defects bullet owned by a direct chart fix — BECAUSE no Lenny client has a working Postgres path in the self-managed answer-file renders, so any lenny-ops-only route through it fails — ALTERNATIVES: admitting `app: lenny-ops` in `allow-postgres-self-managed` (contradicts §25.4 `lenny-ops-egress` "does not bypass the pooler", needs a spec edit, and `lenny-ops-egress` has no postgres-pod peer); rendering policy ports from `pgbouncer.port` plus wiring `databaseURLSecret` (pre-existing chart defect touching gateway, Token Service, and controller policies, larger than 0097).
MISTAKE: Rounds 1, 2, and the round-2 follow-up each staged a narrower lenny-ops-only Postgres route (`postgres.opsDsn` → `lenny-pgbouncer` last). It could not work: PgBouncer listens on 6432 behind 5432-only policies and has no `[databases]` upstream. Cost: three rounds. CORRECTS [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1] (the opsDsn DECISION).
WATCHOUT: Do not re-stage any lenny-ops-only Postgres route for the self-managed renders, and do not write "lenny-ops reaches Postgres wherever the gateway does" (false when `gatewayEgressCIDR` covers the pod CIDR: `allow-postgres-self-managed` admits the gateway but not lenny-ops). The fix for that topology fixes every client at once and lives outside 0097. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/values.yaml:1263 `port: 6432`; charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:442-444 allow-pgbouncer `port: 5432`, :115-117/:179-181/:242-244/:564-566 client PgBouncer egress peers `port: 5432`; charts/lenny/templates/ops-network-policies.yaml:91-94; charts/lenny/templates/pgbouncer-config.yaml:38-47 (only `[pgbouncer]`); charts/lenny/answers/catalog/*.yaml set neither `gatewayEgressCIDR` nor `databaseURLSecret`.
FACT: `gateway-deployment.yaml` renders `LENNY_POSTGRES_DSN` from `lenny-datastore-conn` key `postgres-dsn` gated on `$hasPg` — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/gateway-deployment.yaml:1422-1428.
FACT: spec §13.2.4 PgBouncer row states ingress on TCP 5432 while the chart listens on 6432; pre-existing drift, covered by the new Defects bullet's port sentence. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:278.


### [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: delete the `postgres.opsDsn` / `ops-postgres-dsn` route, its `datastore-secret.yaml` edit, and the answer-file instruction from CODE-6 item 6; `lenny-ops` renders `LENNY_POSTGRES_DSN` from `postgres-dsn` exactly as the gateway does, keeps the round-1 `allow-pgbouncer` clause and `gatewayEgressCIDR` ipBlock peer, and the self-managed Postgres path becomes one summary Defects bullet (a direct chart fix owns it) — BECAUSE the chart's self-managed renders give NO client a Postgres path under an enforcing CNI, so any lenny-ops-only route there repairs one client of a broken topology — ALTERNATIVES: admit `app: lenny-ops` in `allow-postgres-self-managed` (the finding's preferred fix: contradicts §25.4 "`lenny-ops` does not bypass the pooler" and the §13.2.4 lenny-ops egress row, needs a spec edit this loop cannot stage, and is insufficient alone because `lenny-ops-egress` has no peer for postgres pods); render the PgBouncer policy ports from `pgbouncer.port` and wire `pgbouncer.databaseURLSecret` (repairs the chart PgBouncer path for gateway, Token Service, controller, and lenny-ops alike: a pre-existing chart defect larger than 0097).
CORRECTS [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]: the `postgres.opsDsn` → `lenny-pgbouncer` remedy cannot work. PgBouncer listens on `pgbouncer.port` 6432 while every PgBouncer policy port is 5432, and its pgbouncer.ini has no `[databases]` section with no answer file setting `databaseURLSecret`. The DECISION is superseded by [non-spec.3.fix-design-G1.1].
FACT: in the self-managed answer-file renders the gateway cannot reach Postgres either. `allow-gateway-egress` reaches Postgres only through pgbouncer pods on 5432 (PgBouncer listens 6432) or `postgres.gatewayEgressCIDR`, which no answer file sets, and `default-deny-all` covers Egress — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/system-network-policies.yaml:14-20, :560-588; charts/lenny/values.yaml:1261-1263; charts/lenny/templates/pgbouncer-deployment.yaml:58-59; charts/lenny/templates/pgbouncer-config.yaml:38-47; charts/lenny/answers/catalog/bare-metal-self-managed.yaml:12-32.
FACT: §13.2.4 states PgBouncer ingress on TCP 5432 for every client, and the chart's `pgbouncer.port` default is 6432 (spec/17 runbooks also use `-p 6432`). This spec-versus-chart port drift predates 0097 — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md §13.2.4 PgBouncer row; charts/lenny/values.yaml:1263.
WATCHOUT: Do not answer "lenny-ops cannot reach Postgres in self-managed installs" with another lenny-ops-specific route (a DSN override, an allow-postgres-self-managed clause, or a port override). Three rounds tried narrower routes. The topology is broken for every client, and that is the summary Defects bullet.
USEFUL [non-spec.2.fix-design-G1.1]: its FACT that the gateway cannot reach Postgres in the self-managed renders was the key to choosing the delete.

### [non-spec.5.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-5's key-set verifier first makes gateway-minted playground bearers exchangeable on POST /v1/oauth/token, so the Token Service refuses a playground-origin caller token with the existing `401 invalid_client` (SPEC-7 Edit 7 in §13.3 "Client authentication on `/v1/oauth/token`", CODE-5 item 4, summary D27 as the one reason home) — BECAUSE the child loses `origin` (pkg/tokenexchange has no Origin field), so §27.3.1 logout and the §27.6 caps never reach it, and no flow presents a playground caller (the playground refreshes via POST /v1/playground/token) — ALTERNATIVES: a Defects entry only (accepts a regression this proposal introduces); refusing origin in every position (breaks the §8.2 delegation subject_token); a pg:revoked read on the Token Service (adds Redis fail-closed path, does not stop a pre-logout exchange); carrying origin to the child (restores caps, not logout).
WATCHOUT: Do not extend the origin refusal to the `subject_token` or `actor_token`. §8.2 "Child-token minting" sets subject_token to the authenticated user's JWT, which in a playground session is the playground bearer; refusing it repeats the withdrawn step (1a) missing-row trap. CODE-5 item 4's second sentence carries this. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59
WATCHOUT: §8.2 does not name the caller token of the internal delegation exchange. Whoever implements delegation through the Token Service (Defects: childtoken.Minter bypasses it) must not present the playground bearer as the caller, or SPEC-7 Edit 7 refuses it. — EVIDENCE: spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59; pkg/gateway/mcpfabric/delegation/childtoken/childtoken.go
FACT: Today the Token Service cannot verify a gateway-minted token: `buildServer` sets only `Signer`, and `NewServer` falls back to it as verifier; `authenticateCaller` writes `invalid_client` on any verify error and checks no claim. — EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go buildServer; pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:208-211, :435-449
FACT: `invalid_client` has zero hits in spec/ before SPEC-7 Edit 7; the code already emits it for caller-auth failures, so Edit 7 adds no new error code. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:438,444
CORRECTS [Standing context Open item 3]: the playground subject_token gap does not predate 0097 in reachability; the pre-0097 Token Service rejects every gateway-minted token. The Open is rewritten to the spec-ambiguity half and points at D27.
CORRECTS [ledger OPEN at review-log "A revoked playground or impersonation bearer presented as a `subject_token`" and the security-lens OPEN "Nothing in spec or code applies the §27.3.1 playground revocation check"]: "predates 0097" and "no staged edit addresses it" are false after this round; the caller path is closed by SPEC-7 Edit 7 and the subject_token residual is accepted in D27.
OPEN: §27.3.1 "Per-request revocation check" and §13.3 do not state whether a body-borne playground `subject_token` on a Token Service exchange is checked against pg:revoked, or whether the child inherits `origin`. A later proposal owns it (design siteDisposition: separate-finding on spec/27_web-playground.md "Per-request revocation check").


### [non-spec.5.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: `jwt.LoadKeySet(ctx, provider, aliasToKey map[string]string)` decides whether `platform:jwt-verify` is mapped from the alias map and returns every `PublicKey` error; CODE-4 item 1 passes `kmsOpts.AliasToKey` — BECAUSE the cloud adapters map provider not-found to `kms.ErrUnknownKEK` (aws.go `errors.As(err, &notFound)`, gcp.go `codes.NotFound`, azure.go `isNotFound`), so a skip keyed on that sentinel silently dropped a mapped but mistyped or deleted verify key, against SPEC-2 "A process that cannot load a mapped entry does not start" — ALTERNATIVES: narrowing ErrUnknownKEK for `PublicKey` in each adapter (contradicts the adapters' Wrap/Unwrap/CurrentKEKVersion convention, three exceptions to remember); callers passing an empty verifyAlias (copies one conditional into three wirings); a provider "is mapped" method (new surface the map already covers).
WATCHOUT: Do not re-add a `PublicKey` not-found adapter test case or a "PublicKey returns ErrUnknownKEK only for unmapped" rule. Nothing reads the sentinel after this fix; the discriminating test is the CODE-2 `LoadKeySet` case where a mapped alias returns an error wrapping `kms.ErrUnknownKEK` and load fails. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md Testing, `pkg/auth/jwt` bullet.
FACT: Every caller holds the alias map as `providerflags.Options.AliasToKey`: gateway `f.kmsOpts` (cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go `providerflags.Bind`, read in token_signing.go as `kmsOpts := f.kmsOpts`, a pointer), Token Service `f.kmsOpts` (cmd/lenny-token-service/flags.go `providerflags.Bind`), lenny-ops through CODE-6 item 1. EVIDENCE: pkg/kms/providerflags/providerflags.go `AliasToKey map[string]string`.
DECISION: the local provider's unmapped-verify `ErrUnknownKEK` special case is removed and any unmapped alias uses its name as reference (local.go already derives for any alias) — BECAUSE it existed only to feed the deleted skip rule; spec-changes **Development** names only the unmapped signing alias and stays true.
DECISION: CODE-1 item 4's per-provider repointable-reference forms are deleted and the spec-changes edge case "A repointable key reference" cut to a pointer — BECAUSE SPEC-1 **Production** is the one home, and CODE-1's GCP copy ("contain") had already drifted from SPEC-1 ("ending in") — ALTERNATIVES: rewording the copy to "ending in" keeps a second home.
FACT: spec-changes.md was edited this round (edge-case bullet only, a reduction to a pointer). The related-passages lookup judged 0 dependents for each changed statement.


### [non-spec.5.fix-G3.1]
FACT: the testinfra KMS stub (tests/testinfra/stubs/kms) is in-process only (`AsProvider`), and providerflags.Resolve builds only local, aws, gcp, and azure, so a subprocess started by tests/testinfra/gateway or tests/testinfra/tokenservice can never sign through the stub. Stub fault injection and the stub call counter are usable only in in-process compositions. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/stubs/kms/provider.go `AsProvider`; pkg/kms/providerflags/providerflags.go `Resolve` switch; tests/testinfra/gateway/gateway.go `StartWith` exec.Command(binary, ...)
WATCHOUT: do not re-add a stub or endpoint flag to providerflags or any binary to let subprocesses dial the stub; it is a second code path in production binaries. The CODE-4..7 tier-2 KMS test is in process (key set, remote signer, `BreakerSigner` with fake `Now` over `stub.AsProvider()`); the tier-9 unknown-kid "no KMS call" assertion was deleted because tier 1 (CODE-2 tests) pins it. — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go `Now func() time.Time`
FACT: the gateway's client-visible KMS failure responses for impersonation start and admin rotate-token are written in pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin (`(*Router).writeImpersonationError` default branch 500 INTERNAL_ERROR; `handleRotateToken` 500 INTERNAL_ERROR on any Rotate error). The service packages (impersonation, admintoken) only wrap the sign error. CODE-7 Targets now name these writers. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/impersonation.go `writeImpersonationError`; admin_token.go `handleRotateToken`
DECISION: Retry-After rounding has one code home, `RetryAfterSeconds() int` on `ErrSigningThrottled` and `ErrSigningUnavailable` (CODE-3 item 2), read by every CODE-7 writer; pinned at tier 1 (400 ms gives 1, 2.3 s gives 3) and at the playground writer (2.3 s gives `Retry-After: 3`) — BECAUSE a test at one writer leaves the others free to truncate — ALTERNATIVES: a 400 ms/2.3 s case per writer (three implementations of one rule).
FACT: aws-sdk-go-v2/config v1.32.17 honours `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_<SERVICE>` (so `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_KMS`) through `EnvConfig.GetServiceBaseEndpoint`, and the gateway harness passes os.Environ() to the child, so a subprocess key-set load failure is injectable with no production code change. — EVIDENCE: ~/go/pkg/mod/github.com/aws/aws-sdk-go-v2/config@v1.32.17/env_config.go `GetServiceBaseEndpoint`; tests/testinfra/gateway/gateway.go cmd.Env = append(os.Environ(), ...)
WATCHOUT: the startup-failure test cannot use the harness `Start`/`StartWith` as is: both wait for the listener and fail the test when the process exits, so the test builds the binary through the harness and runs it to exit. Set static dummy AWS credentials, or the SDK's credential chain probes IMDS and the test spends its time there. — EVIDENCE: tests/testinfra/tokenservice/tokenservice.go `Start` ("Returns when the gRPC listener is responsive")
OPEN: the bootstrap `provisionAdminToken` path swallows a `Provision` error; whether a KMS failure at bootstrap needs a surfaced outcome was not examined (out of G3 scope).

### [non-spec.5.fix-G4.2]
DECISION: a tier-7a race test in `tests/tier7a_load_local` pins the CODE-6 item 6 `Rehydrate` swap rule; the fake's last `ListRevoked` starts the `Revoke` goroutines and returns without waiting, so a `Revoke` can land between the return and `c.mu.Lock()`; S18 gains 7a — BECAUSE `Cache.Rehydrate` takes `c.mu` only after the last `ListRevoked` returns (pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go `Rehydrate`), and the tier-1 "revoked inside ListRevoked" case cannot separate "record until the swap" from "record until the read returns" — ALTERNATIVES: specifying the record mechanism in CODE-6 item 6 (still untested); a test-only hook in `Cache` (production surface for tests); a `-race` pkg test (that runs at tier 1; tier-7a tests live under the `load_local` tag, as CODE-9's does).
WATCHOUT: do not let the fake's `ListRevoked` wait for the `Revoke` goroutines; that collapses the 7a case into the tier-1 case and stops discriminating — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md "Tests that land with CODE-4, CODE-5, CODE-6, and CODE-7", the tier-7a bullet.
FACT: today's `Rehydrate` unions every `jti` already in `c.revoked` into the fresh set under the lock; CODE-6 replaces that with keeping only `jti`s added since the read began — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go `Rehydrate` ("Carry forward any revocation added locally").

### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: Close the caller path only. SPEC-7 Edit 7 (§13.3 "Client authentication on `/v1/oauth/token`") makes a token carrying `origin: "playground"` a non-credential as the caller token (`401 invalid_client`); CODE-5 item 4 implements it in `authenticateCaller`; the reason and the accepted subject_token residual live in a new summary D27 — BECAUSE the key set (CODE-5 item 2) is the first Token Service verifier that accepts gateway-minted tokens, a child minted from a playground bearer carries no `origin` (pkg/tokenexchange has no Origin field) so §27.3.1 logout never names it and §27.6 caps never bind it, and no flow presents a playground caller (no client or SDK calls /v1/oauth/token with it; the playground refreshes through POST /v1/playground/token) — ALTERNATIVES: record-the-exposure only (accepts a security regression the change introduces against a §27.3.1 MUST); refuse `origin: playground` in every position (rejects the §8.2 delegation, whose subject_token is the authenticated user's JWT, i.e. the playground bearer in a playground session: the round-1 missing-row trap again); refuse the subject only when no actor_token (an attacker supplies a playground bearer as the actor too, so it needs an actor refusal that may break a playground session's delegation actor); Token Service `pg:revoked` read (a Redis fail-closed path that does not stop a bearer exchanged before logout); enforcing the §13.3 caller/subject identity match (the same §13.3 paragraph explicitly allows a caller presenting the target user's token as subject_token, so it does not block the two-credential case).
FACT: The gateway's authmw runs on /v1/oauth/ despite it being a public route whenever a Bearer header is present, so a revoked playground bearer as the CALLER is already refused at the gateway (pg:revoked); only the body-borne subject_token/actor_token skip that check. The caller refusal therefore exists to stop the pre-logout exchange into a surviving origin-less child, not the revoked-caller case. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:422-425 (Bearer branch before any public-path test), cmd/lenny-gateway/authposture.go:53 ("/v1/oauth/" public)
FACT: The Token Service enforces only tenant match between caller and subject; it does not compare `sub`. — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenexchange/tokenexchange.go:166-167; no Subject comparison in pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go
WATCHOUT: Do not extend SPEC-7 Edit 7 / CODE-5 item 4 to the subject_token or actor_token. §8.2 "Child-token minting" sets subject_token to the authenticated user's JWT (spec/08_recursive-delegation.md:59), which is the playground bearer for a playground session; this is the same trap as the withdrawn step (1a) missing-row rule. — EVIDENCE: review-log Standing context Settled bullet on step (1a)
WATCHOUT: The review-log WATCHOUT "Do not reintroduce it in a narrower form (external-only, by origin, typ, or caller)" ([spec.2.fix-G1.1]) governs the step (1a) missing-row rule. The playground caller refusal is not a missing-row rule and rejects no delegation; do not file it under that WATCHOUT.
WATCHOUT: The delegation-mint follow-up must not present the playground bearer as the caller token of its internal exchange, or SPEC-7 Edit 7 refuses it. — EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:688 does not name the caller of the internal §8.2 exchange
CORRECTS [spec.2.fix-G1.1] OPEN and [spec.2.fix-design-G1.1] OPEN and WATCHOUT ("a revocation-store gap that predates 0097"): the spec ambiguity predates 0097, but the path is unreachable in today's tree, because cmd/lenny-token-service/server.go buildServer sets no Verifier and NewServer falls back to the per-process HMAC signer, which rejects every gateway-minted token. CODE-5 makes it reachable. The caller half is closed by SPEC-7 Edit 7; the subject_token half is the accepted residual in summary D27.
CORRECTS [spec.5.review-security.1] FACT "the impersonation half ... predates 0097 on every verifier": on the Token Service an impersonation bearer does not verify before CODE-5. After CODE-5 the Token Service can exchange it, but the child's exp is capped at the bearer's, and End revokes nothing anywhere (summary Defects), so the exposure window does not widen; no staged change for impersonation.
OPEN: (separate finding) Summary D27's residual: §27.3.1 "Per-request revocation check" and §13.3 do not state whether a Token Service exchange that carries a playground bearer as the body-borne subject_token must consult `pg:revoked`, or whether the child inherits `origin`. A later proposal (plausibly the §8.2 delegation-mint follow-up) owns it.
UNVERIFIED: whether the docs-alignment lens wants a reader-facing note in docs/client-guide/authentication.md that a playground bearer is refused on /v1/oauth/token. No staged docs change was designed; no client flow sends one.


### [non-spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]
FACT: every cloud adapter maps a provider not-found on a MAPPED key to kms.ErrUnknownKEK, so the sentinel means "unmapped or not found", never "unmapped" alone. — EVIDENCE: pkg/kms/aws/aws.go:181-183, pkg/kms/gcp/gcp.go:139-140, pkg/kms/azure/azure.go:165-166
FACT: every LoadKeySet caller already holds the alias map as providerflags.Options.AliasToKey (gateway cmd/lenny-gateway/flags.go:1343, Token Service cmd/lenny-token-service/flags.go:139, lenny-ops through providerflags.Resolve per CODE-6 item 3). — EVIDENCE: pkg/kms/providerflags/providerflags.go:66
DECISION: LoadKeySet takes the alias map, `LoadKeySet(ctx, provider, aliasToKey map[string]string)`. It fetches platform:jwt-signing always and platform:jwt-verify only when the map has that entry, and it returns any PublicKey error. The ErrUnknownKEK skip, CODE-1 item 1's unmapped-alias sentence, and the local provider's unmapped-verify ErrUnknownKEK special case are deleted. — BECAUSE SPEC-2 defines "mapped" by the alias map, so deciding it from a provider error was the defect. The rewrite removes the sentinel dependency altogether instead of narrowing what the sentinel means. — ALTERNATIVES: the reviewer's fix, which makes PublicKey not-found stop wrapping ErrUnknownKEK. That splits the sentinel's meaning by method against the adapters' WrapDEK/UnwrapDEK/CurrentKEKVersion convention, and it is the same sentinel-keyed answer one step narrower. Also rejected: callers pass "" for an unmapped verify alias, because that copies one conditional into three wiring sites.
DECISION: G2 finding 0 deletes CODE-1 item 4's per-provider predicate and cuts the spec-changes edge case to "names a repointable key reference (SPEC-1)". SPEC-1 Edit 1 **Production** is the only home. — BECAUSE the copies already disagree on GCP ("contain" against "ending in").
WATCHOUT: do not re-add a PublicKey not-found case to the adapter tests, and do not narrow ErrUnknownKEK for PublicKey. After this round LoadKeySet never reads the sentinel. The discriminating test is the LoadKeySet case in which a MAPPED verify alias returns an error wrapping kms.ErrUnknownKEK, and LoadKeySet must fail on it.
