DECISION: the gateway/Token Service startup-refusal test is a tier-2 subprocess case using that env with a key-ID `platform:jwt-signing` reference; the discriminating assertion is that the log names the key-set load failure (an earlier startup failure would also exit non-zero). The verify-alias failure case stays with `LoadKeySet`'s tier-1 test, because the binary has one error branch on `LoadKeySet`. — ALTERNATIVES: per-alias `PublicKey` faults in the stub (cannot reach a subprocess); tier-1 re-exec of `main` (gateway startup may fail earlier for unrelated reasons without real backing services).
DECISION: `Retry-After` rounding gets one code home, `RetryAfterSeconds()` on `ErrSigningThrottled` and `ErrSigningUnavailable`, replacing CODE-3 item 2's restatement of the SPEC-4 rounding rule; one tier-1 case pins the rounding and the playground case with 2.3 s pins a writer. — ALTERNATIVES: rounding cases at each of the three writers (three implementations of one rule, three tests).

UNVERIFIED: whether the tests/testinfra gateway and tokenservice harnesses can start a process expected to exit and pass extra environment; the implementor of the startup test checks.

### [non-spec.6.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 item 2 now makes every error `BreakerSigner.Sign` returns a `*SigningError{RetryAfter}` (throttled, breaker-open, hard, and caller-done), wrapping the inner error when there is one; callers detect it with `errors.As`; `RetryAfterSeconds()` rounds up with a floor of 1. Item 1's bullets keep only the classification and outcome — BECAUSE the admin writers (`writeImpersonationError`, `handleRotateToken`) receive the Sign error through `%w` wraps in `impersonation.Service.Start` and `admintoken` `mint` beside audit-append, store, and Secret errors that must stay 500, so they need a type to tell a signing error apart; and SPEC-4 says only "whole seconds and at least 1", so the rounding direction the tests pin (2.3 s gives 3) needs a stated home — ALTERNATIVES: a third `ErrSigningFailed` type beside `ErrSigningThrottled`/`ErrSigningUnavailable` (three types no caller tells apart, and the caller-done branch would still be raw and reach the admin 500 default); defaulting the admin writers to 503 (audit/store/Secret errors would become 503); package-local markers in impersonation and admintoken (two markers, neither carries RetryAfter); pointing at SPEC-4 for rounding (round 5's text; SPEC-4 does not fix the direction).
MISTAKE: round 5 replaced "Round up to whole seconds with a floor of 1" with "the SPEC-4 `Retry-After` value", which pointed at a home that does not state the rounding direction, while its own tests pinned ceiling rounding. Cost: one round-6 finding.
CORRECTS [spec.5.fix-G2.1] and [spec.5.fix-design-G2.1]: their rejection of "a typed BreakerSigner error carrying RetryAfter for hard errors" because "the HTTP-layer floor of 1 already covers it" held only for writers that see the raw Sign error (Token Service, playground). The admin writers see it wrapped beside non-signing errors, so the typed error is required. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go:158-160 vs :141 and :163; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go:449-452; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/impersonation.go:255-269; admin_token.go:93-96.
CORRECTS [the WATCHOUT at review-log line 1590, "only the HTTP layer (CODE-7) floors it to 1"]: the floor of 1 now lives in `RetryAfterSeconds()` (CODE-3 item 2). A half-open refusal's raw `RetryAfter` may still be zero or negative, so a tier-1 test asserts on `RetryAfterSeconds()`, never on a positive `RetryAfter`.
WATCHOUT: the tier-3 `oauth_token`/`playground` case uses "a test-local signer whose `Sign` returns a hard error". The writers read `Retry-After` from `*jwt.SigningError`, so that signer must return a `*jwt.SigningError` (or be a `BreakerSigner` over a failing inner signer); a raw error leaves the header value undefined. I did not edit that bullet, because the design ruled it unchanged. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md "Tests that land with CODE-4, CODE-5, CODE-6, and CODE-7", tier-3 bullet.
WATCHOUT: `jwt.ErrSigningUnavailable` is a sentinel today, read with `errors.Is` at pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:705 and in pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner_test.go (incl. `TestErrSigningUnavailableIsExported`, F-10.2.6). Under CODE-3 item 2 the breaker-open result is a `*SigningError`; whether the sentinel survives as its wrapped cause is left to the implementor, and those call sites must move to `errors.As`. No spec or docs text names either type (lookup exact search, 2026-10-24).


### [non-spec.6.fix-G2.1]
DECISION: The tier-1 Token Service bullet in "Tests that land with CODE-4, CODE-5, CODE-6, and CODE-7" gains an allow-side case: a caller token with no `origin` claim plus a key-set `subject_token` carrying `origin: "playground"` returns an `access_token` (D27). BECAUSE `authenticateCaller` and `verifyExchangeTokens` share one `s.verifier.Verify` call, so an origin check placed in the verifier passes every deny-side test and still breaks the §8.2 playground delegation. ALTERNATIVES: an `actor_token` case (discriminates nothing beyond the subject case); turning TEST-1 item 6 into an allow case (it is a valid deny case, and tier 1 builds the delegation directly); the wording "is not refused for the origin claim" (asserts nothing observable). EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:442,481,490
WATCHOUT: TEST-1 item 6 presents the playground bearer as both caller and subject_token, so the caller refusal alone yields its 401. Do not read it as covering the subject_token boundary; the tier-1 allow case owns that. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 6


### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-3 item 2 replaces the two refusal types with ONE `*jwt.SigningError{RetryAfter time.Duration}` that `BreakerSigner.Sign` returns for every non-success result (throttled, breaker-open, hard, caller-done), wrapping the inner error when there is one; `RetryAfter` is the SPEC-4 duration and `RetryAfterSeconds()` rounds it up to whole seconds with a floor of 1. Item 1 bullets 2 and 3 drop their "returns ..." clauses (item 1 classifies, item 2 says what is returned). — BECAUSE the admin paths receive the Sign error only through `%w` wraps beside audit, store, and Secret errors that stay 500, so they need a type on every Sign error, and the caller-done branch would otherwise still reach their 500 default against CODE-7 item 1. — ALTERNATIVES: a third type `ErrSigningFailed` for hard errors (the finding's suggestion; three types with one method, no caller distinguishing them, and the caller-done branch still raw); writers defaulting to 503 (turns audit and store failures into 503); comparing error strings (code-best-practices bars it).
CORRECTS [spec.3 era review-log entries at the "typed BreakerSigner error carrying RetryAfter for hard errors (new mechanism; the HTTP-layer floor of 1 already covers it)" DECISION and its twin]: that rejection held only while every writer saw the raw Sign error. The impersonation and rotate-token writers see it wrapped beside non-signing errors, so the HTTP layer cannot apply the 1 s default without a type. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/impersonation/service.go:141,158-160,163; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admintoken/admintoken.go:449-452; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/impersonation.go:255-269; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/admin_token.go:93-96.
CORRECTS [non-spec.5 entries naming `RetryAfterSeconds()` on `ErrSigningThrottled` and `ErrSigningUnavailable` as the rounding home]: the home is now `SigningError.RetryAfterSeconds()`, and it must state the direction (round up, floor 1). SPEC-4 says only "whole seconds and at least 1", so a pointer to SPEC-4 left floor-truncation conforming while the tier-1 and playground tests pin 2.3 s -> 3.
WATCHOUT: the rounding direction is stated once, in CODE-3 item 2's `RetryAfterSeconds` sentence. Do not move it to CODE-7 or restate it at each writer, and do not replace it with "the SPEC-4 value" again; that was the round-5 regression. EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md CODE-3 item 2; spec-changes.md SPEC-4 Edit 1 "in whole seconds and at least 1".
FACT: production callers of a `jwt.Signer.Sign` are impersonation.Service.Start, admintoken mint (Provision and Rotate), the playground token handler, and tokenservice mintToken; devauth uses the concrete HMACSigner (CODE-2 item 5). EVIDENCE: grep of `.Sign(` under pkg/ and cmd/.


### [non-spec.6.fix-design-G2.1]
FACT: The Token Service verifies the caller, the subject_token, and the actor_token through one verifier call, `s.verifier.Verify`, in `authenticateCaller` and `verifyExchangeTokens` (pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go), so an origin check placed in the verifier or in a shared wrapper refuses a playground subject_token as well. That is the implementation the allow-side test separates. EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:442, :481, :490

### [non-spec.8.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: permissive `/healthz` runs the existing `probe.Run` over only the `postgres` and `kubernetes` entries, and the chart sets `timeoutSeconds: 3` on startup, liveness, and readiness in every render (CODE-13 items 1 and 4) — BECAUSE `probe.Run` waits on every probe it is given (wg.Wait) up to `probeTimeout` = 2 s, and kubelet's 1 s default fails the probe first, so any hanging dependency restarted every replica and emptied production readiness — ALTERNATIVES: a first-success runner in pkg/ops/probe (second mechanism, and a 1-2 s reachable dependency still loses to kubelet's 1 s default); a sub-second permissive timeout (turns a slow Postgres into a restart); keep running every probe with timeoutSeconds 3 (calls gateway summary, MinIO, Prometheus every period); a tier-4 black-holed Postgres case (harness has no kubelet).
WATCHOUT: `timeoutSeconds` must stay above `probeTimeout`; do not make it conditional on `ops.production`, because non-production `/readyz` runs every probe under the same 2 s bound — EVIDENCE: pkg/ops/opsserver/opsserver.go:43 (`probeTimeout = 2 * time.Second`), pkg/ops/probe/probe.go:42-81, charts/lenny/templates/ops-deployment.yaml:395-418 (no timeoutSeconds today).
FACT: probe names are `ProbePostgresName = "postgres"` and `ProbeK8sAPIName = "kubernetes"`; deps_setup registers postgres, redis, minio, prometheus, kubernetes, and gateway (`/v1/admin/health/summary`) — EVIDENCE: pkg/ops/opsserver/opsserver.go:504-511, cmd/lenny-ops/deps_setup.go:136-156.
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: SPEC-11 Edit 6's §25.4 "Probes" YAML sets no `timeoutSeconds`, so it implies kubelet's 1 s default against the §25.6 2 s probe timeouts. True instead: each probe in that YAML should carry `timeoutSeconds: 3`. Predates 0097 and the YAML is illustrative (its liveness values already differ from the chart), so it is a separate finding for a later spec round.

### [non-spec.8.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-13 permissive /healthz runs `probe.Run` over a two-entry map (`postgres`, `kubernetes`, taken from `s.probes`) and the chart sets `timeoutSeconds: 3` on startupProbe, livenessProbe, and readinessProbe in every render — BECAUSE `probe.Run` waits for every probe it is given (each bounded by `probeTimeout` = 2 s) and kubelet's default timeout is 1 s, so the handler verdict must be able to land before kubelet gives up; a two-probe map keeps Redis/MinIO/Prometheus/gateway off the liveness path, and timeoutSeconds > probeTimeout makes the handler's answer the verdict — ALTERNATIVES: a first-success short-circuit runner in pkg/ops/probe (a new helper; still leaves a reachable-but-slow 1-2 s dependency timing out at kubelet's 1 s, so it needs the chart line anyway); a permissive probe timeout below 1 s (silently couples handler to kubelet's default and counts a 900 ms Postgres ping as unreachable); timeoutSeconds alone with all probes run (fires gateway/MinIO calls on every probe period per replica).
FACT: `probe.Run` returns only after every probe settles or hits its own timeout; there is no early return — EVIDENCE: pkg/ops/probe/probe.go `Run` (wg.Wait); `K8sAPIProbe` ignores its ctx (`disc.ServerVersion()`), so only Run's `time.After` bounds it — EVIDENCE: pkg/ops/opsservice/probes.go `K8sAPIProbe`.
FACT: no ops-deployment probe sets timeoutSeconds, and the §25.4 "Probes" YAML already diverges from the chart's liveness values (spec: failureThreshold 5/periodSeconds 10; chart: initialDelaySeconds 15/periodSeconds 20) — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/ops-deployment.yaml startupProbe/livenessProbe/readinessProbe; spec/25_agent-operability.md §25.4 "Probes".
WATCHOUT: the non-production readiness probe on `/readyz` runs every registered probe with 2 s bounds under kubelet's 1 s default, a pre-existing timeout defect; setting timeoutSeconds unconditionally on all three probes fixes it incidentally. Do not make timeoutSeconds conditional on ops.production.
DEFERRED [spec-changes.md]: the §25.4 "Probes" YAML (SPEC-11 Edit 6 touches only the readinessProbe path line) sets no timeoutSeconds, so it implies kubelet's 1 s default against the §25.6 2 s probe timeouts. True after CODE-13: the chart renders timeoutSeconds: 3 on all three probes. A spec round may add `timeoutSeconds: 3` to each probe in that YAML; it is a separate finding (the YAML predates 0097 and already disagrees with the chart's liveness values).

### [non-spec.8.review-performance.1]
FACT: Switching the gateway's `Cache.Rehydrate` from union to swap (CODE-6 item 6) is not a fail-open. Every gateway `Revoke` caller pushes a jti only after its durable `revoked_at` write commits (cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:300-322, pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users_invalidate.go:170-182, issued_tokens.go:65-81). The rehydration lister includes tombstoned tenants (`allTenantsLister`, cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:194-203). Postgres RPO is 0 (spec/12_storage-architecture.md:150). A swap therefore drops only rows that the GC has deleted. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/credentials/revocation/revocation.go:121-150
FACT: CODE-9 takes the §11.7 lock twice in one transaction (`AcquireLockInTx`, then `AppendInTx` calls it again). It is a transaction advisory lock, which is reentrant within a session, so it cannot self-deadlock. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/auditstore.go:10,216,371
FACT: The Token Service metrics are scraped by the `lenny-control-plane` ServiceMonitor, so the `lenny_jwt_sign_requests_total{component="token-service"}` term reaches the KMSSignQuotaApproaching sum. — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/servicemonitor.yaml (targets list)
DECISION: no findings under the performance and failure-mode lens in round 8 — BECAUSE the round-7 diff (CODE-3 item 2 `SigningError`, and the playground, tier-3, and D27 tests) matches SPEC-4's rule "remaining cooldown when open, 1 second otherwise", and the lenny-ops 30 s read, PgBouncer use, KMS GetPublicKey at startup, and lock-hold growth are all marginal at Tier 3 — ALTERNATIVES: considered filing the synchronized Retry-After: 1 retry herd under throttling, but rejected it as a converged spec choice (SPEC-4) and a design preference.

### [non-spec.8.review-reliability.1]
FACT: `probe.Run` (pkg/ops/probe/probe.go:42-82) runs every registered probe concurrently and waits for all of them, each bounded by `probeTimeout` = 2 s (pkg/ops/opsserver/opsserver.go:43). lenny-ops registers postgres, redis, minio, prometheus, k8s, and gateway probes (cmd/lenny-ops/deps_setup.go:136-156). The lenny-ops startup, liveness, and readiness probes set no `timeoutSeconds` (charts/lenny/templates/ops-deployment.yaml:395-423), so kubelet's 1 s default applies. A permissive `/healthz` that calls `probe.Run(s.probes, ...)` like the strict branch therefore fails liveness and readiness whenever any probe hangs. — EVIDENCE: the files above
WATCHOUT: a stopped Postgres container refuses connections at once, so TEST-1 item 2's "Stop Postgres" case never exercises the hung-dependency case. — EVIDENCE: non-spec-changes.md TEST-1 item 2
FACT: every Revoke caller on revocation.Cache writes the durable issued_tokens row first (users_invalidate.go:173-181, issued_tokens.go:65-81, cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:300-321), so the CODE-6 Rehydrate swap that drops cache-only jtis loses no durable revocation. Rows are deleted only after exp + 1 h (issuedtokenstore.go:578-660). — EVIDENCE: those lines
FACT: pubsub.Bus.Subscribe relies on go-redis PubSub.Channel(), which reconnects on its own, so rule 2 resumes after a Redis restart without a new goroutine. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/storage/pubsub/bus.go:74-95
FACT: pg_advisory_xact_lock is reentrant within a transaction, so CODE-9's AcquireLockInTx followed by AppendInTx (which calls it again) does not self-deadlock. — EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/audit/auditstore/lock.go:180

### [non-spec.8.review-security.1]
- FACT: The gateway's /v1/playground/token route is in publicRoutePatterns, but authmw runs serveBearer (key-set verify plus the Revocations cache) whenever an Authorization: Bearer header is present, before the PublicPath check, so an apiKey-mode pasted bearer that was revoked is refused by authmw before the playground handler's own Verifier runs. Widening the playground Verifier to the key set (CODE-4 item 2) therefore opens no revocation bypass. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:423-427, 487-499; cmd/lenny-gateway/authposture.go:44-63
- FACT: Every gateway caller of revocation.Cache.Revoke writes the durable issued_tokens revocation first (admin single-token revoke, §11.4 user invalidation, admin rotation), the Token Service publishes after COMMIT, and the gateway rehydration lister includes tombstoned tenants. So CODE-6 item 6's change to Rehydrate (keep only the jtis Revoke added after the read began) drops no revocation the read would not return. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-gateway/user_revocation.go:300-322; pkg/gateway/externalapi/admin/users_invalidate.go:172-182; cmd/lenny-gateway/billing_security_workers.go:194-203
- FACT: The child token that tokenservice.mintToken builds carries no Origin, so a playground subject_token yields a child with no origin claim. That is the D27 accepted residual, and the new tier-1 Token Service case pins it on purpose. EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:680-700
- UNVERIFIED: lenny-ops authmw sets no PlatformRoles override, so lenny-ops trusts the token's roles claim, while the gateway prefers the stored platform-managed role (spec/10 "Role assignment"). The gap predates 0097 because lenny-ops never applied the mapping, so it was not filed. A later proposal that touches lenny-ops authorization should check it. EVIDENCE: cmd/lenny-ops/main.go:187-196; cmd/lenny-gateway/httpsurface.go:880-884
- WATCHOUT: authmw's PlaygroundRevocations check runs after ExtractTenant, so on lenny-ops a playground token with a bad tenant gets a tenant error rather than the rule-7 response. Both responses refuse the token, so this is not a defect, but a rule-7 test should use a valid tenant. EVIDENCE: pkg/gateway/middleware/auth/auth.go:505-538

### [non-spec.10.fix-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-8 item 3 moves `expires_in` presence into the wire type: `tokenservice.Request.ExpiresIn` becomes `*int64` tagged `expires_in,omitempty`, and `parseRequest`'s form branch sets it when present — BECAUSE `Request` is both the decoded type and the struct every in-tree JSON client marshals (no omitempty), so a parser-level presence record could not tell a deliberate 0 from an absent field and would 400 every existing JSON exchange — ALTERNATIVES: keep int64 plus a second decode into a map or RawMessage (second wire representation, deliberate 0 unsendable through the struct); omitempty on int64 alone (decoder still reads absent as 0).
FACT: The only code that sets `Request.ExpiresIn` is tests/tier3_contract/oauth_token/oauth_token_test.go (the two 48 h literals); the only production reader is `buildExchangeRequest` (`durationFromExpiresIn(req.ExpiresIn)`); the form branch of `parseRequest` hard-codes `ExpiresIn: 0`. Other `pkg/tokenservice` and tier-3 tests build `Request` without `ExpiresIn` and compile unchanged after the type change. EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:255, :574, :1070; tests/tier3_contract/oauth_token/oauth_token_test.go:370, :412
WATCHOUT: Do not add the other `pkg/tokenservice` or tier-3 test files that build `Request` to CODE-8's targets; none sets `ExpiresIn`. The CODE-8 test bullets need no change: a JSON 0 is built with a pointer to 0, and the `exchange` helper omits a nil field. EVIDENCE: tests/tier3_contract/oauth_token/oauth_token_test.go:70-72


### [non-spec.10.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: CODE-8 item 3 represents `expires_in` presence in the type: `tokenservice.Request.ExpiresIn` becomes `*int64` tagged `json:"expires_in,omitempty"`, and `parseRequest`'s form branch sets it when the form carries the field. This replaces the claim that `parseRequest` records presence. — BECAUSE `Request` is both the decoded type and the struct every in-tree JSON client marshals, so with `int64` and no omitempty every client sends `"expires_in":0`, and no parser-side record can tell that apart from a deliberate 0. — ALTERNATIVES: keep `int64` plus omitempty and detect presence with a second decode (map or RawMessage) in `parseRequest`, rejected because it adds a second wire type and leaves a deliberate 0 unsendable through the struct; omitempty on `int64` alone, rejected because the decoder still cannot tell absent from 0.
FACT: The only callers that construct `tokenservice.Request` are tests. pkg/tokenservice tests (drift_test, rotation_test, request_validation_test:59/75, audit_test doExchange) and tier-3 oauth_token_envelope_test/oauth_token_record_test never set `ExpiresIn`, so the type change leaves them compiling, and they now omit the field. The only literals that set it are tests/tier3_contract/oauth_token/oauth_token_test.go:370 and :412 (48 h). No production Go client marshals `Request` (searched sdks, pkg/ctlcli, cmd). — EVIDENCE: pkg/tokenservice/tokenservice.go:255,574,1049-1072,1131
FACT: A `parseRequest` error already becomes 400 `invalid_request` (tokenservice.go:457-461), so a non-integer form `expires_in` that returns a parse error needs no extra rule.
WATCHOUT: The reviewer's suggested fix adds "the pkg/tokenservice tests that build Request" to the CODE-8 targets. That is unnecessary, because none of them sets `ExpiresIn`. Only oauth_token_test.go needs an edit.

### [non-spec.12.fix-design-G1.1]
DECISION: The existing Token Service case "omits mTLS volume mounts and flags when mtls.enabled=false" keeps its name and intent and swaps its two `isNull` asserts for `notContains` with `any: true` on `{name: lenny-token-service-tls}` (volumes and container[0].volumeMounts). The disposition is one sentence in the CHART-2 Testing sub-bullet, and the test file joins CHART-2's Targets. BECAUSE the chart default `kms.provider: local` makes the master-key volume unconditional after S25, so `isNull` fails, and the case exists to pin the mTLS gate rather than to assert that no volumes exist. ALTERNATIVES: setting `kms.provider: aws` in the case so `isNull` still holds was rejected, because it pulls in the cloud-provider render refusals (`kms.signQuotaPerSecond`, `kms.endpointCIDR`) and couples an mTLS case to the KMS provider. Deleting the case was rejected because it is the only pin on the mtls gate for the Token Service volumes.
WATCHOUT: In helm-unittest, a `notContains` whose content is a partial map passes vacuously unless it carries `any: true`, because otherwise it matches whole elements only. Prior art for `any: true`: charts/lenny/tests/gateway-deployment_test.yaml:1188 and pgbouncer-deployment_test.yaml:107. EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/tests/token-service-deployment_test.yaml:267-280; charts/lenny/templates/token-service-deployment.yaml:84-85,138-141.
FACT: Only tests/tier1_unit/helm/helm_test.go runs `helm unittest`. The tier-4, tier-8, and tier-9 hits for "unittest" are scaffolds_test.go. A checklist step whose deliverable adds or edits a charts/lenny/tests case has to list tier 1. EVIDENCE: tests/tier1_unit/helm/helm_test.go:58.
FACT: Among charts/lenny/tests/*.yaml, only token-service-deployment_test.yaml:277-280 asserts that volumes are absent. The gateway and ops deployment tests use contains and notContains on volumes, so CHART-2's mount breaks no other existing case.

ALTERNATIVES: filing the gateway/Token Service `local` refusal keyed on `--environment=prod` against SPEC-1's "production deployment"; rejected because summary Defects line 125 records the LENNY_ENV drift and review-log:1309/2474 settle it.
FACT: MultiVerifier tries every member and returns the first error, so KeySet's alg rejection never stops the dev HMAC trust member from verifying. IsUnknownKey is not used for fall-through. EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/multiverifier.go Verify loop
FACT: the rehydration read keys on `exp > now() - 1h`, so a revoked bootstrap admin credential (10-year exp) stays in the lenny-ops rule-1 set until it expires. The round-16 bound does not drop it. EVIDENCE: spec/13_security-model.md:676

### [non-spec.1.follow-up-fix.1]
CORRECTS [non-spec.1 fixer, breaker invariant]: the iff over the SPEC-4 trip predicate holds only for a closed breaker. A half-open breaker reopens on one counted failed probe (`BreakerSigner.recordFailure`, `case breakerHalfOpen: s.trip(now)`), so the Tier-1 invariant now splits closed and half-open.
WATCHOUT: any invariant stated against the SPEC-4 "> 3 consecutive failures within 30s" predicate must exclude half-open state, where one failed probe reopens — EVIDENCE: pkg/auth/jwt/breakersigner.go recordFailure and the BreakerSigner type doc.

### [non-spec.2.follow-up-fix.1]
DECISION: CODE-6 item 6 stages a lenny-ops-only DSN, `postgres.opsDsn` rendered as `lenny-datastore-conn` key `ops-postgres-dsn` (precedence over `postgres-dsn`), and the self-managed answer files and its values.yaml comment tell operators to point it at `lenny-pgbouncer` — BECAUSE the chart-managed self-managed install points the one shared `postgres.dsn` at `<release>-postgres-rw`, which `allow-postgres-self-managed` does not admit for lenny-ops, so lenny-ops refused every Lenny-issued token for life — ALTERNATIVES: admit lenny-ops in `allow-postgres-self-managed` (contradicts §25.4 "lenny-ops does not bypass the pooler", needs a spec edit); retarget the shared `postgres.dsn` at PgBouncer in the answer files (moves the gateway, controller, and migrations onto transaction pooling, unverified); only qualify the refusal (leaves the recommended install broken); derive a PgBouncer DSN by rewriting the host of `postgres.dsn` with sprig urlParse (fails on keyword-form DSNs).
MISTAKE: the round-2 fixer predicate "the rule-1 read runs only when `postgres.dsn` names PgBouncer or the endpoint `postgres.gatewayEgressCIDR` admits" was wrong for postgres-rw installs: the CIDR admits the endpoint at egress, but `allow-postgres-self-managed` drops lenny-ops at ingress. Deleted.
FACT: `allow-postgres-self-managed` ingress admits postgres, pgbouncer, gateway, token-service, and controller only — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/self-managed-backend-network-policies.yaml:156-174; the self-managed answer files point `postgres.dsn` at `lenny-postgres-rw` — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/answers/catalog/bare-metal-self-managed.yaml:12-13, airgap-self-managed.yaml:14, openshift-self-managed.yaml:11-12; the PgBouncer Service is `lenny-pgbouncer` — EVIDENCE: charts/lenny/templates/pgbouncer-deployment.yaml:156.
WATCHOUT: `values.schema.json` does not constrain `postgres`, so `postgres.opsDsn` needs no schema edit; `datastore-secret.yaml` gates its Secret on a `$has*` disjunction, which must gain the `opsDsn` term.
UNVERIFIED: whether the operator-configured PgBouncer accepts the CloudNativePG app credentials for client auth; the implementor of CODE-6 item 6 should confirm against `pgbouncer.databaseURLSecret` behaviour.
